CHAPTER 1.0 
 
 
INTRODUCTION 
 
===============

## Page 17

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 1 - INTRODUCTION 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 1 of 6 
Issue No : 6 
Rev 
:  5 
Effective Date:01.05.2006
 
 
1.1    BACKGROUND 
 
This is the ‘6th issue' of GASCO Maintenance & Inspection Policy. The main aim of 
this issue is to integrate the ASAB-AGD-I policy as well as to incorporate other due 
revisions as part of regular up-date of the document. The previous ‘issue No. 5’ was 
the outcome of a major exercise undertaken to integrate and comprehensively review 
the Maintenance and Inspection Policies of GASCO (JV) plants and GASCO (ASR) 
HABSHAN Gas Complex. 
 
This issue of the policy supersedes all previous issues of this document, which shall 
be removed from use in line with clause 3.4.4 of procedure “CONTROL OF 
ENGINEERING & MAINTENANCE PROCEDURES & POLICIES – EPR/ AGM 
(O)/ 0001, ISSUE NO.1 Dated 01.05.2003). 
 
 
The policy, in general, reflects the experience gained since GASCO Plants were 
commissioned and particularly during rationalisation and fine tuning..  The problems 
inherent to new equipment, have been solved and experience in operation and 
maintenance of the Plants have now been gained over a period of several years .  This 
experience, specific to the GASCO conditions, was used as a firm basis for setting the 
Maintenance & Inspection Policy.  
 
Prior to the effective date of issue No.5 of this policy, HABSHAN Gas Complex had 
applied the Maintenance Policy of ADNOC Processing Directorate (Effective Date 
1.5.1994), and the inspection policy, which was developed during April 2002, based 
on their experience of the plant and pipelines that also included the experience gained 
in monitoring of sour gas processing plant at HABSHAN. 
 
ASAB-AGD-I followed ADCO policy prior to its integration with GASCO (from 
April 2004). AGD-I shall fully apply this policy (issue No.6) since their requirements 
have been integrated into the policy.     
 
 
1.2    PURPOSE 
 
This document is intended to provide all GASCO Maintenance Departments in its 
operating Divisions, a clear description of policies and guidelines related to the 
maintenance and inspection of all its operating Plants, in order to determine 
appropriate maintenance and inspection programme to meet the GASCO corporate 
objectives and its Performance Management Contract.      
 
This maintenance and inspection policy is an important element of the GASCO Asset 
Integrity management system.

1.3 
AREAS OF APPLICATION 
 
This Maintenance & Inspection Policy shall be applied at the following Plants/ 
Divisions of GASCO:  
ASAB (AGD-0 & AGD-I), BUHASA, HABSHAN & BAB ,RUWAIS and 
PIPELINES.   
 
 
1.4       RELATED DOCUMENTS 
 
Whilst it is intended, through the provided information, to make this policy manual a 
stand-alone document, the following may be referred to for further information and 
related details. 
 
-

1.5       MAINTENANCE & INSPECTION TERMINOLOGIES 
 
1.5.1 Definitions 
 
The definitions of commonly used maintenance & inspection terminology are 
listed below for reader’s information. British Standards Institution developed a 
standard (BS 3811:1993) for the terms used. 
 
Asset Management: The systematic planning and control of a physical 
resource throughout its life. This may include the 
specification, design, and construction of the asset, its 
operation, maintenance and modification while in use, and its 
disposal when no longer required. 
 
Availability: 
Ability of an equipment to be in a state so that it performs a 
required function, at a given instant or with in a given laps of 
time, assuming the availability of all necessary means. 
 
Benchmarking: The 
process 
of 
comparing 
performance 
with 
other 
organisations, identifying comparatively high performance 
organisations, and learning what it is they do that allows them 
to achieve that high level of performance. 
 
Condition Monitoring: The use of specialist equipment to measure the 
condition of equipment. Vibration Analysis, Tribology and 
Thermography are all examples of Condition Monitoring 
techniques (on line & off line). 
 
Integrity: 
The integrity of a facility is achieved when, under specified 
operating conditions, there is no foreseeable risk of failure 
endangering the safety of personnel, environment or asset 
value. 
 
Key Performance Indicators: A select number of key measures that enable 
performance against targets to be monitored. 
 
 
Maintenance: 
All actions in order to maintain or restore equipment (asset) 
to specified state or to perform a required function.

## Page 20

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 1 - INTRODUCTION 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 4 of 6 
Issue No : 6 
Rev 
:  5 
Effective Date:01.05.2006
 
 
Maintainability: Ability of an equipment to be maintained within a given laps 
of time to perform a required function. 
 
Mean Time Between Failures: A measure of equipment reliability. Equal to 
the number of failures in a given time period, divided by the 
total equipment uptime in that period. 
 
Mean Time To Repair: A measure of maintainability. Equal to the total 
equipment downtime in a given time period, divided by the 
number of failures in that period. 
 
Non-Destructive Testing: Testing of equipment, which does not destroy the 
equipment, to detect abnormalities in physical, chemical or 
electrical characteristics. Vibration analysis and tribology are 
not generally considered to be NDT techniques, even though 
they meet the above criteria. Techniques which are 
considered to be NDT techniques are ultrasonic thickness 
testing, dye-penetrant testing, x-raying, and electrical 
resistance testing. 
 
Protective Device: Devices and assets intended to eliminate or reduce the 
consequences of equipment failure. Some examples include 
standby plant and equipment, emergency systems, safety 
valves, alarms, trip devices, and guards. 
 
Reliability: 
Ability of an equipment to perform a required function within 
a given laps of time with given conditions. 
 
Tribology: 
The process of monitoring the condition of equipment 
through the analysis of properties of its lubricating and other 
oils. Typically conducted through the measurement of 
particulates in the oil, or the measurement of the chemical 
composition of the oil (Spectrographic Oil Analysis). 
Commonly used for monitoring the condition of large 
gearboxes, 
engines 
and 
transformers, 
amongst 
other 
applications. 
 
General definitions:  
 
The word ‘shall’ indicates a mandatory requirement. 
The word ‘should’ indicates a recommendation.

REVIEW AND UPDATE 
 
 
As part of continuous efforts to upgrade and sustain maintenance services at state of 
the art levels, it is obligatory that this policy document is reviewed and updated once 
every two years. Engineering Planning & Development Department should invite 
user’s requirements for any need to up-date the policy on annual basis. However if 
any revision deemed necessary during any time before periodic scheduled review, any 
concerned Division can initiate the requirement of such revision. Once agreed by all 
concerned parties, the revised policy can be issued after management approval.

Having successfully overcome the initial problems inherent to each Plant and passed the 
period of consolidation and optimisation, GASCO has moved into a period where the age 
of equipment is becoming an important factor and a peak of equipment major overhauls is 
planned. 
 
Every component or piece of equipment on the facilities serves the purpose to sustain the 
long term GASCO business objectives, for which the facilities were built.  In achieving 
this objective ,the required standards of HSE and Asset Integrity must be met.  The 
functionality of each item of equipment and its components needs to be upheld for the life 
cycle of the facility unless its function is no longer required in which case it needs to be 
removed.  Thus, for each item of equipment there needs to be a maintenance/inspection 
plan or strategy to retain its functionality for the life cycle of the facility.    ‘Operate to 
failure’ can be a valid strategy only if it results from a deliberate decision to adopt that 
strategy for that item of equipment. 
 
The Maintenance Objectives of GASCO are: 
 
- 
Assure life cycle asset integrity  
 
The assurance of Asset Integrity is a GASCO corporate HSE objective.  The 
required maintenance and inspection activities on many items of equipment, 
including HSE Critical Equipment and Systems (HSECES), are specified in or 
derived from this Policy.  Hence the proper application of the Policy makes a 
major contribution to Asset Integrity assurance. 
 
An Asset Integrity and Reliability Management System (AIRMS) is being 
developed for GASCO.  The AIRMS Framework and other documents will make 
reference to many of the items contained in this Policy. 
 
- 
maximise overall system availability both in the short and long term whilst 
maintaining high level of plants’ reliability. 
 
 
Downtime for planned or unplanned shutdowns have a direct impact on production 
losses and shall be minimised as such.  As downtime of process packages 
generates loss or opportunity losses of marketable product, it is of the utmost 
importance to minimise the total downtime (planned or unplanned) in view of the 
serious financial consequences and its impact on the upstream and downstream oil 
& gas facilities within the on-shore integrated hydrocarbon chain. 
 
 
- 
minimise life cycle cost while achieving above objectives 
 
 
 
Direct maintenance costs (GASCO personnel, contractors and material 
consumption) shall be optimised/minimised.  However, equipment shall be 
maintained to such an extent and in such a way (condition monitoring, on-stream

inspection) that breakdowns are prevented as far as is feasibly possible.  
Breakdowns may cause considerable production losses or may result in large repair 
costs.  Minimising maintenance costs shall not result in lowering the high HSE 
standards and plants integrity as set by GASCO Management, or Plants high 
reliability levels achieved up to date. 
 
GASCO is committed to pursue the HSE objectives of: 
 
• 
No harm to people 
• 
Protecting the environment 
• 
Improving asset integrity

To achieve the set Maintenance & Inspection Objectives, GASCO has developed the 
following Maintenance Strategy: 
 
 
3.1 
EQUIPMENT CRITICALITY TYPE 1, 2, & 3 
 
 
For equipment directly related to the production and to the safety of the Plant: 
 
 
The cornerstone of the Maintenance Strategy is combination of Time Based and  
Predictive Maintenance (condition monitoring, on stream inspection) together with 
approach of Risk & Reliability Management (RRM) system.  This means 
minimising breakdowns/ downtime with optimum  efforts for Inspection, 
Monitoring and Maintenance. 
 
This Maintenance Strategy is characterised by: 
 
 
- 
Risk & Reliability Management (RRM) system approach and its underlying 
methodologies i.e. RCM, RBI and IPF. 
 
- 
Maintain high “integrity levels” at all times. 
 
 
- 
Condition based maintenance, supported by condition monitoring 
techniques and on-stream inspections. 
 
 
- 
GASCO & contractors staff of high-skill technicians and supervisors, 
supported by engineers, equipment specialists and inspectors for 
maintenance of the vital equipment. 
 
 
- 
maintenance engineering to investigate the causes of breakdowns and to 
improve the maintainability (e.g. use of Root Cause Analysis - RCA 
techniques). 
 
 
- 
use of specialist vendor assistance to provide in-depth knowledge as and 
when required, with special emphasis on major rotating equipment and 
sophisticated instrument/ electrical systems. 
 
- 
Utilisation of own workshop and resources, to the extent this is feasible, for 
the repair of equipment, spares and electronic cards.  Otherwise, competent 
pre-qualified workshops are to be used. 
 
- 
Use of quality spares of proven suppliers/manufacturers.

3.2 
EQUIPMENT CRITICALITY TYPE 4 
 
 
For equipment not directly related to the production: 
 
 
For this category of equipment so called 'Breakdown Maintenance' strategy can be 
applied, however it should be evaluated on case by case basis.  
 
 
Contractors play a more important role here in maintenance of mainly the support 
equipment (e.g. HVAC/Vehicle maintenance) than in the equipment mentioned 
under 3.1 above. 
 
 
3.3 
PRODUCTION SYSTEM AVAILABILITY 
 
 
To manage and optimise  the availability of the production system, yearly targets 
will be set by Management and reported on a monthly basis (Performance 
Management Contract & Division Balance Scorecard under Performance 
Management System). 
 
 
3.4 
MAINTENANCE ANALYSIS & COST CONTROL 
 
 
To manage the maintenance programmes and costs, GASCO uses the computerised 
Maintenance Management System (Maximo).  Accuracy of reporting depends on 
data quality, which is continuously being improved.  Various reports are available 
for costs analysis i.e. according to maintenance types (Preventive maintenance, 
Condition based, Breakdowns and modifications) and elements (own personnel, 
contract payments, materials).  Equipment performance (availability, reliability, 
etc.) is reported  based on the Runlog application of Maximo . 
 
 
Sites shall prepare their own programmes and budget, follow their commitments 
and cross check on a monthly basis the financial cost report. 
 
 
3.5 
REMNANT LIFE /REMEDIAL STRATEGY  
 
 
A remnant life / remedial strategy is required for all critical items of equipment and 
systems.  The implementation of this strategy will identify when and how aged, 
obsolete and under-performing equipment and systems will be up-graded or 
replaced. 
 
 
Case by case evaluation shall be made to define a reasonable life limit/ RLA 
(Remaining Life Assessment) for the equipment (or part of the equipment).  This 
evaluation shall be based on the recommendations of the O.E.M., the findings 
during inspections and the experience of the worldwide users of similar units.

Replacement of equipment/components due to ageing shall be done based on the 
following guidelines: 
 
 
- 
During refurbishment of major equipment parts (e.g. Frame 5 gas turbines 
and power turbines), the recommendation of the vendor for destructive tests 
and metallurgical analysis should be followed.  This will help in assessing 
the condition of those parts and the remaining life span and thus set the 
limits and scope of subsequent inspections.  A third party assistance is 
highly recommended for that purpose. 
 
 
 
 
 
The equipment shutdown planning shall be amended accordingly. 
 
- 
A similar approach has been applied for Avon gas generators.  During zero 
time reconditioning, the assessment of the overhauling workshop at the 
detailed inspection stage would lead to the reuse, repair or replacement of 
the various components as might be necessary. 
 
 
- 
For other equipment an assessment of the total annual cost of ownership 
against the total cost of replacement is to be made i.e. 
 
 
 
 
direct cost    : manpower + material + overheads 
 
 
 
consequential cost  : production loss 
 
 
 
 
If the ratio reaches above 50% then replacement of the component/ 
equipment with a more reliable one is to be considered (Also refer to 
chapter 4 clause 4.7).  
 
The Sites have the responsibility for the remnant life / remedial strategy and need to own 
and drive the required updates with support of OES. 
 
 
This Maintenance Strategy is detailed in the chapters that follows.

The feedstock to GASCO Plants is both Associated and Non-Associated Gas.  Because of 
the integral part GASCO plays in the On-shore Hydrocarbon Chain any interruption in 
GASCO operations can have a severe impact on third parties. 
 
For Non-Associated Gas processing plants at HABSHAN any interruption impacts on the 
supply of Sales Gas to the network which, if significant, may result in a shortfall of gas to 
major consumers such as power generation companies.  Any extended interruption to 
HABSHAN or AGD-I Non-Associated Gas processing plants will also result in reduction 
of Condensate available for downstream refinery processing. 
 
Since the introduction of the no flaring law any interruption in GASCO plants processing 
Associated Gas impacts on On-shore oil production. 
 
GASCO plants are the sole provider of Ethane feedstock for a petrochemical plant.  The 
Ethane is provided from both Associated and Non-Associated (HABSHAN) Gas sources 
and any interruption in plant processing may result in consequent reductions in the 
petrochemical plant processing. 
 
In particular, interruptions at RUWAIS results in reductions at GASCO upstream plants 
with the consequent impact on third parties. 
  
In addition to impact on third parties, any interruption in processing plants will also result 
in a loss of GASCO products.  
 
Because of the above business losses associated with shutdowns, GASCO plants strive to 
minimize the duration of shutdowns. In the case of planned shutdowns the impact on third 
parties and GASCO can either be mitigated or minimized.  However unscheduled 
shutdowns have a greater effect on third parties and GASCO business and therefore 
maintenance plans should be prepared to ensure high reliability of GASCO plants whilst 
maintaining safety and environmental requirements. 
 
For GASCO plants processing Associated Gas the production levels are dictated by oil 
production levels, which are outside GASCO control.  However oil production can be 
adjusted, with the agreement of ADNOC / ADCO, to minimize production losses during 
scheduled shutdowns. 
 
 
 
The Maintenance Policy defines: 
 
- 
the required level of maintenance. 
 
- 
the means and resources to achieve and maintain this level. 
 
- 
the parameters (KPI) to measure the maintenance performance.

EQUIPMENT CLASSIFICATION (CRITICALITY ASSESSMENT) 
 
 
GASCO Equipment are classified into four types:

- 
Type 1:  (Vital - HSE) 
 
 
 
Equipment which can be considered as vital for HSE reasons (people & 
equipment) or failure of which will immediately affect environment e.g. 
fire fighting equipment. 
 
 
- 
Type2:  (Vital - Production) 
 
 
 
All equipment, failure of which will cause an immediate loss of production,  
mainly process equipment with no standby unit installed, or utilities 
equipment without excess capability, a failure of which could result in an 
immediate shutdown (Equipment configuration 1x100%). 
 
- 
Type 3:  (Critical) 
 
 
All process equipment, failure of which can affect production after a certain 
period of time:  process equipment with standby unit installed (Equipment 
configuration 2x100%) or the equipment, failure of which will have a risk 
to affect environment or will affect health & welfare.

All equipment, failure of which will not affect the production either

It also includes the equipment, failure of which will have no affect on 
Safety, Environment, Health or welfare.  
 
 
Note: - Under certain conditions the criticality of some equipment may 
change e.g. weather conditions. 
 
 
The classification shall be agreed at each Site by Maintenance, Operations and 
Safety Departments and approved by the Plant Division Manager. The approved 
equipment criticality rating shall be assigned to each Location Tag under 
Operating Locations application of Maximo, if not already done.  
 
 
The classification shall direct an agreed approach to maintenance intervention on 
failure, spare parts holding and preventive and condition monitoring programmes. 
 
 
The equipment details are existing in Maximo Data Base under Equipment 
Module, separately for each Plant, and is under the responsibility of Plant 
Maintenance.

The general guidelines for identifying the various maintenance classes are: 
 
 
Preventive Maintenance 
 
 
This is a maintenance activity, which is in accordance with an existing plan and is 
time based. Time based means that the subject maintenance activity is to be carried 
out at preset calendar duration or accumulated running hours. 
 
 
Existing plan could be any of the following:  
Long Term Shutdown Planning sheets issued by Engineering Planning & 
Reliability Department/Plants (Appendix 4.1), annual shutdown planning issued by 
OPP (Appendix 4.3), annual Site Inspection Plan or maintenance schedules of 
Maximo Preventive Maintenance application. 
 
 
Condition Based Maintenance 
 
 
This is a planned maintenance activity. It is based on monitoring parameter(s) of 
equipment and executing a predefined maintenance activity once the monitored 
parameter(s) reaches a preset level or condition. 
 
 
Breakdowns  
 
 
This is a maintenance activity, which is not foreseen in any existing plan. It is 
conducted due to a trip/failure of an equipment or deterioration in its condition. 
Breakdown maintenance should be identified as such, whether it results in 
production loss or not. 
 
 
Modification/Projects 
 
 
This is an activity, which involves changes in equipment installation to improve its 
reliability, availability, maintainability or safety. 
 
 
The maintenance activities are further subdivided into following classifications 
based on the budget allocation: 
 
 
Extraordinary Maintenance (EOM): 
 
 
These are the non recurring activities , which are costing or estimated to be above 
a set value for each item. 
 
 
Major Maintenance (MM): 
 
 
These activities have recurring nature, which are costing or estimated to be above a 
set values for each item..

The reason for isolating Extraordinary and Major Maintenance work in this way is: 
 
 
- 
To facilitate Management control on expenditure of abnormal nature. 
 
 
- 
To create a reasonable ground for a better comparison of routine costs year 
by year. 
 
 
4.3 
SHUTDOWN POLICY 
 
 
The GASCO Shutdown policy is directed to minimise the impact on the overall 
Onshore business while meeting all the requirements to ensure plant safety, 
integrity and reliability.   
 
 
For Maintenance related shutdown work the frequency and duration are 
determined by the life cycle considerations to maintain equipment which require 
unit or plant shutdowns to do the necessary work.  Such work may include repair, 
testing  and inspection activities.   
 
 
The shutdown drivers for both maintenance and project works are established and 
the following identified: 
 
 
 
• 
the earliest date the shutdown can be achieved based on equipment / 
component  lifecycle requirements and material availability; 
 
 
• 
the latest acceptable dates for shutdown completion based on 
equipment / component lifetime, regulatory inspection requirements 
or project schedules; 
 
 
• 
the period of the year where there will be least impact on the 
business; 
 
 
• 
the critical path job(s) and duration based on 24 hour working. 
 
 
The above information is collated for all GASCO plant / unit shutdowns for the 
coming five year period.  Shutdowns of related plants are then either aligned or 
segregated based on maximising the overall business requirements.   Shutdowns of 
related plants external to GASCO such as ADCO, BOROUGE and TAKREER are 
also taken into consideration when defining the timing of the shutdowns.   
 
 
Major integrated shutdowns are planned in detail up to 24 months prior to the 
event. 
 
 
Having identified the need for a shutdown and the work which must be completed 
during the shutdown then, when there is synergy in the efficiency or cost of 
execution, additional work can de included during the shutdown window.  Such 
work should not result in an increase in the duration of the shutdown either due to 
its own requirement or to interference with the critical path job(s).

In any GASCO plant shutdown there will be product losses to GASCO and third 
parties which are non-recoverable and therefore the duration of shutdowns are to 
be minimised.  This will be achieved by: 
 
 
 
• 
Scope Challenge process for shutdown content for: 
• 
Shutdown drivers based on life cycle considerations. 
• 
Additional ‘non-driving’ work. 
 
 
• 
Work procedures challenge – to do the job more efficiently. 
 
 
• 
Coordination with Operations to minimise the operational shutdown 
and start up periods. 
 
 
• 
Work pattern including 24 hour/day and 7 days/week on critical path 
items. 
 
 
An overall shutdown manager will be nominated for each shutdown with 
responsibility for maintenance and project works and the integration of both into a 
single shutdown plan.   
 
 
The shutdown of some auxiliary units such as utilities may be achieved without 
direct impact on the business.  These will be planned at site level but due regard 
must be taken of any reduction in back-up capability due to the shutdown of the 
unit.  
 
 
For equipment which requires a shutdown for maintenance which cannot be 
aligned with major plant shutdowns the module will be maintained separately, 
which will in general cause only a short duration  or partial unit shutdown thus 
minimizing  production loss from the plant. 
 
 
The essential maintenance requirements of major rotating equipment are aligned to 
prepare long term shutdown plans, inspection of static equipment shall follow the 
shutdown scheme for major rotating equipment with respect to timing, unless 
prevailing conditions dictate a shutdown timing that might be an extension to 
rotating equipment shutdown or independent of it.  
 
 
4.3.1 Process Equipment Without Standby 
 
 
 
The GASCO 5 year business plan is prepared and updated regularly twice a 
year by Engineering Planning & Reliability Department, a planning chart is 
attached as an example (Appendix 4.1). 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The shutdown time has to be as short as possible.  The major overhaul 
shutdown preparation shall make use of computer software available in 
GASCO (Maximo, MxProject) for planning, resource scheduling and spare 
parts planning.  Updating of the planning and utilisation in future 
shutdowns optimisation is a major advantage of using such computer 
software.

4.3.2 Equipment With Standby 
 
 
 
Major equipment is incorporated in the 5 year planning.  Shutdowns are 
planned during the standby period taking into account spare parts and 
manpower availability.  For process equipment shutdowns shall as much as 
possible be planned and carried out in such a way that the equipment can be 
operational within 24 hours in case of breakdown of the running equipment 
(not applicable for major overhauls). 
 
 
 
If it is not the case, Management will have to be informed before starting 
maintenance activities. 
 
 
 
In view of the environment (sand) opening of rotating equipment on Site 
shall be as short as possible. 
 
4.3.3 Total Plant Shutdown 
 
 
 
As GASCO has chosen a modular shutdown concept and approach of 
aligning the shutdown requirements with the major shutdowns, a total 
planned Plant shutdown shall only be considered in an exceptional case. 
 
 
 
However a total Plant shutdown is required for the inspection and 
maintenance of common plant facilities such as below: 
 
 
 
   HABSHAN & BAB Plant  
 
 
- 
Acid gas flare for complete HABSHAN Gas Complex 
 
 
   -    Hydrocarbon Flare for OGD-0 (Thammama ‘C’) 
 
 
   -    Hydrocarbon Flare/ cold Flare for OGD-I 
- 
Hydrocarbon Flare for OGD-II  
- 
The Amine unit in BAB Plant area. 
 
 
BUHASA Plant 
 
 
- 
Common facilities & Blow-down vessels at BUHASA. 
 
 
ASAB AGD-0 
 
 
- 
HP/ Propane Package. 
 
ASAB AGD-I 
- 
Vapour Recovery Compressor. 
- 
Flare/Burn Pit. 
 
 
 
In addition to the above, some other systems of the plants may also force 
total plant shutdown, every effort shall be made to minimise production 
losses. 
 
 
 
 
 
Detailed shutdown planning shall be prepared by the sites.

4.4 
5-YEAR BUSINESS PLANNING 
 
 
The 5-year plan for rotating and static equipment major shutdown is a guide for 
Plants and Management and gives the overall indication of the scheduled 
maintenance activities. The major rotating equipment shutdown schedules of all 
plants shall be prepared by Engineering Planning & Reliability Department at 
Head Office in coordination with the plants, Engineering & Technical Support 
Departments (Appendix 4.1). Production Planning Department shall coordinate 
with Projects group. 
 
 
The planning process takes into account the following factors: 
 
 
- 
condition monitoring highlights 
 
- 
period (winter preferable) 
 
- 
process and production constraints (upstream & downstream) 
 
- 
spare parts availability 
 
- 
results of past inspections or overhauls 
 
- 
maximum run lengths 
 
- 
Plant workload 
 
- 
    Projects related work 
 
An example of shutdown planning of a package considering operation hours and 
spares availability is the attached AVON movement-tracking chart (Appendix 4.2). 
 
 Similarly movement-tracking chart of LM-2500 (driver for HABSHAN ETB 
Compressors 13C102, 13C101A & B and injection compressors 94C101 & 
95C101) is prepared and maintained based on the following agreed guidelines 
(Appendix 4.2A). 
 
 
- 
No dedicated spare LM-2500 will be kept either for ETB compressors or 
for Injection compressors. 
 
- 
The overhaul workshop turnaround duration will be 4 months. 
 
- 
It is preferred to remove the LM-2500 engines of Injection Compressors 
(94C101 & 95C101) (when due for overhaul or hot section replacement - 
refer to chapter 6.1.1b of this policy manual) during low demand period 
(generally summer and/or along with process train shutdown for periodic 
inspection). 
 
 
 
- 
OSP Department is responsible for preparing the movement plan in 
coordination with ESR & Plant, taking into consideration all necessary 
elements in order to reduce the risk due to unavailability of spare LM-2500 
engine. The plan shall be continuously revised to take into account any

emergencies or change in schedule of inspections, shutdowns or overhauls 
and any new developments. 
 
   
 
The 5-year plan integrates, on a permanent basis, the results of the condition 
monitoring follow-up, and therefore is adjusted twice a year for major rotating 
equipment (Vital-Production Packages). 
 
 
It also defines the utilisation factor for power generators in order to minimise spare 
parts stock level for the common equipment. 
 
 
4.5 
YEARLY PLANNING 
 
 
In the third quarter of every year the Plant Division Managers shall submit for the 
approval of Assistant General Manager (Operations), the list of rotating and static 
equipment due for inspection in the following year in the form of budget forecast 
during budget preparation cycle.  Any deviation from the 5-year plan shall be 
highlighted and justified. 
 
 
Based on the 5-year business plan the Production Planning shall issue a yearly 
Plant/Train shutdown approved planning particularly for shutdowns that cause 
production losses and requires special arrangement with ADCO to reduce oil 
production level to minimise shutdown losses.  
(Example of OPP Plant Overview Appendix 4.3) 
 
 
4.6 
BREAKDOWN OF EQUIPMENT 
 
 
Breakdown of equipment may cause considerable production losses or involve 
heavy repair costs, therefore a technical evaluation of breakdowns is essential to 
identify the cause of the problem and to eliminate by carrying a structured defect 
elimination process i.e. RCA, or at least to minimise these causes.  A proper 
analysis of the root cause of breakdowns may sometimes show up deficiencies in 
design, operating or maintenance procedures and practices or in condition 
monitoring and on-stream inspection techniques and frequencies. 
 
 
To minimise maintenance cost it is essential to utilise experience gained at one 
Plant in eliminating problems at other plants.  It also has to be realised that 
breakdowns at one Plant may influence planned overhauls at other Plants, 
especially for common insurance spares (e.g. for the overhaul of AVONs). 
 
 
Therefore: 
 
 
 
 
 
- 
to arrive at optimum maintenance planning for whole of GASCO,

## Page 40

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 4 - GENERAL 
 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 10 of 18 
Issue No: 6 
Rev   :  6 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
 
- 
to assist the Plants in proper analysis of the causes of breakdowns, and 
subsequently, 
 
 
- 
to advise on the best techno-economical solution, based on life cycle costing 
(LCC) 
 
 
- 
to ensure proper exchange of information between the Plants. 
 
 
Engineering & Technical Support Division shall coordinate the exchange of 
information between Sites and the Plants shall adhere to the following: 
 
 
1. 
Reporting of major rotating equipment failures/ trips and related downtime 
shall be done via Runlog application of Maximo by the respective operations 
staff. 
 
 
2. 
Detailed shutdown reports are to be prepared by the Sites for each major 
shutdown they carried out.  It is to include among other things, supporting 
photographs (if possible), sketches, bar charts and detailed cost report. A 
standard format shall be adopted by all plants (such feed back should help in 
cross learning, long term shutdown planning and up dating of Maintenance & 
Inspection Policy where applicable) .  
 
 
3. 
In case of breakdown of important process or utility equipment a FAILURE 
REPORT shall be completed for significant events as soon as possible and 
forwarded to the Engineering & Technical Support Division for their 
evaluation. Maximo work order  Failure Reporting shall be completed by the 
respective maintenance section, which can be printed using registered Failure 
Report within Work Order Tracking Application as and when required. The 
work order shall be linked to respective Runlog record whenever it is related. 
 
4. 
Root Cause Analysis (RCA) Procedure for Equipment (Technical) 
(EPR/AGM(O)/0005) was developed to effectively analyse and eliminate the 
root cause(s) of any failures of equipment. The procedure shall be applied 
across all plants/ sites by all Operation Divisions, which addresses both the 
reactive and proactive approaches.   
 
 
5. 
For failures which may have an effect on the overall GASCO maintenance 
planning (specifically the Gas Turbine  packages) Engineering & Technical 
Support Division shall be consulted before a final decision is taken on repair. 
 
This approach does not take away any responsibility from the Plant, being the 
custodian of the equipment, but ensures that the best decision will be made, taking 
into account the overall GASCO situation, and that optimum use is made of the 
available resources within GASCO.

The Engineering & Technical Support Division shall evaluate the failure data with 
the equipment manufacturer as necessary and forward as needed the conclusions 
and recommendations to the Sites. 
 
 
4.7 
EQUIPMENT/ASSET REPLACEMENT STRATEGY 
 
GASCO considers proactive approach to meet its maintenance objectives set under 
Chapter 2 of this policy document. Most of GASCO’s equipment/assets are now 
crossing 20 years since their installation. This extended operation demands high 
rate of replacement of spare parts and possibly some of these spare parts might be 
out of production (obsolete). In order to ensure proper decisions on whether to 
maintain or replace an equipment/asset the following shall be considered: 
 
- 
Cost – benefit analysis (total maintenance cost including indirect cost Vs cost 
of replacement) 
- 
Obsolescence (availability of spare parts to ensure maintainability) 
- 
HSE requirements 
 
 
4.7.1 Cost – Benefit Analysis 
 
This aspect should be fully covered under Reliability Policy and Reliability 
Management System. GASCO’s CMMS (Maximo) provides a great deal of 
facility to carry out this analysis, though its potential is yet to be fully 
utilised. 
 
 
4.7.2 Obsolescence Studies 
 
Obsolescence studies shall be conducted for the Vital & Critical equipment, 
which are not planned to be replaced under any other consideration (e.g. 
projects/ upgrading/ modernisation/ HSE) in order to ensure 
maintainability.  Engineering & Technical Support Division shall lead such 
studies; however the custodian Departments/ Divisions may indicate the 
need. The studies’ recommendations for the identified obsolete equipment 
shall, as a minimum, cove the following: 
 
-  Spare parts stock levels (considering the dead end for spare parts 
availability & equipment phasing out plans)  
- 
Equipment reconditioning/refurbishment 
- 
Retrofit or replacement of obsolete equipment

4.8 
REPORTING AND RECORDING 
 
 
High personnel turnover and the work rotation system, stress on the importance of 
writing proper and detailed reports and recording them conveniently with a clear 
identification of location to such records for easy accessibility to those who might 
require them.  
The details of Break Down Maintenance works performed shall be recorded under 
failure reporting tab of Maximo Work Order Tracking Application. The work 
orders related to Major Rotating Equipment shall be linked to the Runlog records 
after completing the failure report. Efforts shall be made to obtain all maintenance 
related reports (including vendor reports) in electronic formats in order to link 
those to the related record in Maximo. 
All the maintenance records/ readings shall be retained in line with GASCO 
Records Management User Guide Manual.    
 
 
4.9 
TECHNICAL COMPLIANCE AUDITS 
 
 
Technical compliance audits shall be performed as follows. 
 
4.9.1 Audit of Inspection Disciplines 
 
 
In order to maintain a consistently high inspection standard, the 
inspection functions of each discipline should be subject, when deemed 
necessary by the Assistant General Manager (Operations), to an external 
audit, so that the Management may be assured that the inspection 
functions are being carried out satisfactorily.  Audits will be carried out 
by competent authority. 
 
4.9.2 Audit of other Maintenance Disciplines 
 
Audits of other Maintenance disciplines shall be performed to discover 
area of shortfalls/practices requiring improvement and to review:  
 
- 
Maintenance procedures in place. 
- 
Compliance with Maintenance policy 
- 
History data & Linked documents. 
- 
Quality system in place. 
 
The audit recommendations shall include the options for change, 
improved techniques and work methods as well as the strategies for 
achievement. The audit should be performed in line with the 
recommendations for above audit (4.9.1).

4.9.3 GASCO Reliability & Integrity Action Tracking System (GRIATS) 
 
GRAITS is a computer program to effectively manage the integrity and 
reliability related actions/recommendations, which are usually the outcome 
of maintenance audits, integrity reviews, surveys or root cause analysis of 
equipment. The actions are registered into the system and can be tracked 
through quick reports according to search criteria, which shows  actions’ 
status using traffic light system.  
 
All Operation Divisions are responsible to ensure the entry, follow-up and 
close-out of their actions (refer to Procedure No. EPR/AGM(O)/0006).   
 
4.10 
PLANT CHANGES 
 
 
A procedure has been developed to satisfy the Company's objectives with respect 
to the safe and economical implementation of changes to the existing installations, 
without hindering its effective management by the Plant Division Managers. 
The changes are defined as additions or deletions to various facilities of the plant, 
which shall be undertaken where there is a clear justifiable need for doing so, 
provided that the integrity of the Plant assets and adherence to GASCO health, 
safety and environment policies are maintained.   
 
This procedure covers the steps to be followed for processing of any Plant Change 
Request (PCR), specifies the approval system and indicates the roles and the 
responsibilities of all individuals involved.  The aim of the procedure is to ensure 
that all plant changes are thoroughly reviewed by all relevant specialists, 
disciplines and managerial authorities, to determine whether the proposal is 
technically feasible, economically viable, fulfils safety requirements, and 
maintains Plant integrity.  It also ensures that all such modifications are approved 
at the appropriate Management level, are designed by competent, qualified staff, 
and are carried out under the same design and safety standards currently applicable 
at the Site. The manufacturer's advice shall be requested as necessary for the 
critical modification. 
 
4.11 
MEETINGS/ BEST PRACTICES FORUM 
 
Regular meetings between Engineering/ maintenance personnel are very beneficial 
for the exchange of knowledge, best practices, good & bad  experiences and to 
propose recommendations to Management. 
 
Maintenance Superintendents/ Mangers meeting shall be held on quarterly basis. 
Maintenance Section Heads (Mechanical, Instrument, Electrical, Inspection) 
meetings shall be organised periodically within maximum span of 6 months. 
 
 
 
 
 
Terms Of Reference (TOR) shall be developed for all these meetings/ best 
practices forums. (Example of TOR is under Appendix 4.4)

4.12 
TECHNICAL FORUMS 
 
Technical forums between GASCO and other OPCOs should be arranged to share 
best practices and technical knowledge. Engineering Planning and Reliability 
Department Head should organise these forum twice a year and shall coordinate 
and facilitate all the requirements for holding such forums. Participation of 
Operations Division Maintenance Departments should be ensured.     
 
 
4.13 
BENCHMARKING 
 
Benchmarking exercises provide a basis for comparison with other similar 
industry. GASCO, in the past, have conducted maintenance and equipment 
performance benchmarking exercise through its shareholders e.g.: 
 
- 
Maintenance/Benchmarking by TOTALFINA - 1999 
- 
Equipment Performance Review by Shell – 1998 
 
Currently there is an annual benchmarking of GE frame V Gas Turbines within 
Shell Group of operating companies. 
 
 
4.14 
QUICK REFERENCE GUIDE TO PM FREQUENCIES (APPENDIX 4.5) 
 
The tables under Appendix 4.5 provide quick reference to main Preventive 
Maintenance (PM) activities and their related frequencies. It is recommended to 
refer to the respective policies in the following chapters in order to apply each 
clause as it is stipulated in the policy. 
 
 
4.15 
INTEGRITY CRITICAL ACTIVITIES DEVIATION CONTROL 
 
 
4.15.1 Implementation of Preventive Maintenance Frequencies 
 
 
 
 
The frequencies of maintenance & inspection activities, which are 
stipulated in this policy, are applied to the equipment through the PM 
(Preventive Maintenance) Module of Maximo (CMMS) except for major 
rotating equipment (see clause 4.4 above). The program calculates “Next 
Due Date” of PM activities based on the set frequencies. The intervals are 
calculated from the “last completion date” of work order or the “last target 
start date” according to the selected option. The selection option for any 
IPF test intervals shall be from the last completion date in line with the 
recommendations of HSEMS Shareholders’ Audit (Year 2002).

It is the responsibility of each discipline in-charge/ Section Head to ensure 
that the set frequencies in Maximo are matching with this policy and are 
up-dated for any revision as and when applicable. 
 
“Extended Date” field in Maximo PM Application allows the users to 
override the “Next Due Date” of any PM record. This option shall be used 
selectively only for the following cases: 
 
- 
The next due date of a maintenance or inspection activity has to be 
calculated according to the results of previous maintenance or 
inspection in line with criteria given within this policy for such 
equipment, which requires the calendar based next due date to be 
overridden. 
  
- 
The next due date of a maintenance or inspection activity has a 
tolerance according to this policy and the “Extended Date” is being 
entered is with in this tolerance. 
 
Work orders of all due PMs shall be generated regularly without leaving 
any backlog in PM module of Maximo. All the scheduling and backlog 
management shall be handled in the work order application only. It is 
strictly prohibited to cancel any work order generated from PM module, 
any duplicate work order generated due to system error shall be identified 
so in “Engineering remarks” field together with number of its alternate 
work order, which shall be processed, prior to cancellation of duplicate 
work order. 
 
 
4.15.2 Compliance Band and Deviation Control 
 
 
The PM work orders (generated from PM module of Maximo) take the 
“Next Due Date” of PM activity as “Target Start date”, which is the 
‘Expected Date’ when individual maintenance activities, (all work, 
inspections or tests), are due to be carried out .The ‘Maximum Allowable 
Deviation’ from this date is as per table 4.1 
 
 
Compliance Band 
   
 
 
 
For planning purposes, some flexibility is required with regard to the 
scheduling of activity execution; however strict control must be exercised 
over any ‘delay’ to the ‘Expected Date’, in order to maintain the Technical 
Integrity of the Plant or equipment concerned. To ensure an effective 
control over Integrity Critical Maintenance or Inspection activities such

Deviation Control  
 
- Variance Control  
 
 
 
 
Any period of time after the ‘Compliance Band’ Range is known as the 
‘Variance Control Band’. For an activity (PM work order) to be 
rescheduled into the ‘Variance Control Band’, the alternatives, risks and 
consequences shall be assessed by the relevant specialist together with his 
department head (Maintenance Superintendent/ Manager or Head of

Technical Services Department) after which the Division Manager’s 
authorisation shall be obtained, in writing (use Maintenance and Inspection 
Activity Variation Control Form-A, Appendix 4.6).  The Variance Control 
Band allows for a further delay to the ‘Expected Date’ on the same basis 
used to determine the ‘Compliance Band’, which must never exceed 20% 
of the service interval, (up to a maximum delay of 12 months). 
 
 
- Excess Variance Control  
 
To exceed the ‘Variance Control Band’ the Division Manager’s 
authorisation shall be obtained again, in writing (use Maintenance and 
Inspection Activity Excess Variation Control Form-B, Appendix 4.7). In 
this case the Division Manager must also advise the Assistant General 
Manager (Operations). However, the revised Variance Date shall not 
exceed the original Expected Date by more than a total of two 20% of 
service interval periods of time up to a maximum of 24 months, (i.e., two x 
10% of a 5 year service interval).

4.15.3 Roles and Responsibilities 
 
The relevant Maintenance or Inspection discipline Section Head, for the 
particular equipment type, (section 4.15.2, Table 4.1 above) is responsible 
for the application of this Maintenance and Inspection Activity Deviation 
Policy. His duties include: 
 
- 
Raise Variance Control Form 
- 
Raise Excess Variance Control Form 
- 
Keep auditable records on delay authorisation 
- 
Provide monthly performance indicators on number of authorisations. 
 
He is also responsible for the technical evaluation of any proposed delay of 
maintenance or inspection activities. The relevant specialist should 
consider all aspects of fitness for purpose of the item, its known rate of 
deterioration, the estimated time remaining before a failure may occur, any 
practical alternatives, e.g. use of non- intrusive inspection, and record these 
before submitting a decision to support or reject the proposed request. 
Furthermore, where such considerations are extensive or in the very 
exceptional case of a request to exceed the Variation Control Band the 
specialist’s records shall be attached to the Control Form (Appendix 4.6 or 
Appendix 4.7, as appropriate).

ABU DHABI GAS INDUSTRIES LTD (GASCO)  
Date of Issue: 06/07/2004 
 
ENGINEERING SUPERINTENDENTS MEETING  
TERMS OF REFERENCE 
Revision No.: 2 
 
Page: 1 of 1 
 
OBJECTIVE:  
- 
To exchange technical knowledge, share best practices and learn from 
good & bad experiences. Topics shall focus on aspects related to plant 
reliability, integrity, HSE and Maximo system. 
 
ATTENDEES: 
- 
Engineering Planning & Development Department Manager  - OSP  
(Chairman) 
 
- 
Plant Engineering Managers/Superintendents 
 
- 
Engineering & Shutdown Planning Section Head  - OSP/P  (Secretary) 
 
- 
Special Invitees as and when required (e.g. ESX, ESR, OSP/M/C, ESI)   
 
 
FREQUENCY:   
Quarterly  
VENUE:  
 
GASCO Headquarter & Sites (alternating, i.e. HQ. – Site – HQ.) 
GENERAL NOTES: 
- 
Agenda items to be agreed in advance (at least a week). 
- 
Agenda items should be of common interest to all sites. 
- 
Number of agenda items shall be optimized (6 to 8) for 
effectiveness. 
- 
Maximo system to be a permanent agenda item.  
 
  
- 
The presentations, if any, shall be short (15 minutes each). 
- 
Minutes of Meeting (MOM), to be recorded for review and 
comments by attendees, and finalize within a week.

2    INSPECTION POLICY (CHAPTER – 10)   
2.1    SAFETY RELIEF VALVES (CHAPTER – 10.17) 
Activity 
Frequency 
Testing 
- For valves not opening up to 1.05 the set pressure.  
- For valves found leaking, and/or opening outside the set 
pressure tolerance prescribed by the ASME code.  
- For valves passing the test. 
- For valves passing two consecutive tests 
- Testing after overhaul 
 
Note: The computerised Maximo system is used for test-
intervals of PSVs in Bab, Asab, Buhasa & Ruwais and 
Habshan .The test- interval is automatically calculated 
based on factors such as valves passing during initial pop 
tests, extent of repair carried-out to nozzle &disc and 
criticality of the service. 
Test intervals 
- 6 - 12 months 
- 12 - 24 months 
 
- 24 - 48 months 
- Up to 4 years 
- After each overhaul 
Inspection Frequencies 
 
- When found leaking during normal 
operation or scheduled testing. 
- If the valves keep passing the tests, 
dismantling for thorough examination may 
be deferred depending on the design and 
operational requirements until the internal 
inspection of its associated vessel takes 
place, up to a maximum of 6Months. 
Valve Setting/Calibration of Pressure Gauges 
Pressure gauges used in the test block shall 
be calibrated as frequently as necessary to 
ensure accuracy. (Max. six months) 
 
 
2.2    FLEXIBLE HOSES FOR HYDROCARBON SERVICE (CHAPTER – 10.18) 
Activity 
Frequency 
Frequency of Inspection (Visual) 
Every 6 months 
Frequency of Testing 
 
Test Medium: 
The pressure tests should be carried out with fresh water. 
Following the test, the water should be immediately 
drained and the hose blown dry. 
Should be hydrostatically tested twice at 
18 months intervals. The pressure should 
be raised in increments up to the test 
pressure of 1.3 times the operating 
pressure of the hose. 
The useful life of flexible hoses should be 
four years counted from the date of their 
first use, whatever is the frequency of use. 
Exclusions 
The following hoses are excluded from the scope of this 
policy: 
1. Fire hoses. 
2. Breathing apparatus hoses. 
3. Propriety hoses forming an integral part of machinery 
and vehicles e.g. dry chemical powder delivery on fire 
trucks. 
Inspection and testing of these types of 
hoses shall be under the responsibility of 
the HSE Superintendents.

5.1 
MAINTENANCE & INSPECTION ORGANISATION (Appendix 5.1) 
 
 
Due to the size of the Plants and their remoteness GASCO follows a policy of 
DECENTRALISATION.  Therefore at each Plant an Engineering Organisation 
exists, which is fully capable of carrying out maintenance of the Plant.  The 
organisation for ASAB & BUHASA plants is a simple line organisation, split into 
Mechanical, Electrical, Instrument, Inspection, Planning sections and Reliability 
Engineering, headed by a Maintenance Superintendent, who reports to the Plant 
Division Manager. The Organisation at HABSHAN & BAB and RUWAIS Plants 
have Technical Services Department, which is responsible for inspection activities.  
The maintenance department at HABSHAN & BAB plant have  four (4) 
maintenance areas and a central maintenance team. Each area is headed by a 
Section Head and the central team by Deputy Maintenance Manager. The head of 
Maintenance Department is Maintenance Manager, who reports to the Plant 
Division Manager. In   Pipelines Division, CP monitoring is part of Inspection 
activities. 
 
The interfaces between various maintenance disciplines should be defined in the 
internal maintenance procedures of each site. 
  
 
For shutdowns, assistance may be sought from the other Plants for additional 
manpower requirements or equipment before seeking extra contractor assistance. 
 
 
Vendor specialists or consultants, on request of the Plants, provide specialised 
advice and assistance. . 
 
 
Coordination and support of the engineering activities between the Plants is done 
by Engineering & Technical Support Division.  Inspection advice is given by the 
Senior Inspection Engineer (ESI/2). 
 
5.2 
CONTRACTOR PERSONNEL POLICY 
 
 
The GASCO Contractor Policy is based on the Maintenance Strategy and is 
characterised by the following points: 
 
 
- 
Use of specialist vendor assistance with particular emphasis on major rotating 
equipment. 
 
 
 
This assistance is required, not only during shutdowns, but also when the 
machines are running, to assess the condition of the machines and to interpret 
the condition monitoring parameters so that run times can be extended 
without taking undue risks. 
 
 
 
- 
Maintain competent high skilled technicians within GASCO.  This expertise 
and experience needs to be retained in own personnel.  If Contractors are

relied on for this high skilled work then with every change of Contractor 
personnel, the Contractors will go through the 'learning curve', with 
subsequent lower availability of the machines and consequently higher 
production losses. 
 
 
- 
In addition to the high skilled GASCO technicians, use contractors where 
necessary and economically justified. 
 
5.2.1 Outsourcing Policy  
 
Contract out services that can be more effectively delivered by other 
companies with full responsibility. 
 
- 
These services for non-critical and routine tasks comprise mainly: 
 
 
 
 
 
 . Major Civil maintenance  
 
. Painting, insulation, scaffolding 
 
. Heating, ventilation, air conditioning maintenance (HVAC) 
 
 
 . Heavy lifting, (heavy) transport, car maintenance 
 
 
 . Plant house keeping (Sand removal) 
 
. Lighting maintenance 
 
- 
Contract out specialist services, these comprise mainly: 
 
. Major rotating equipment (gas turbines, compressors, , etc.) 
reconditioning and/ or repair. 
 
. Static equipment (heat exchangers, columns, tanks, etc.) major 
repairs and vessel relining. 
 
. Special and non-destructive test and vibration survey, analysis and 
recommendation. 
 
. Lifting equipment inspection. 
 
. Fire and Gas - Maintenance and Technical/Vendor Support 
 
. Process Analyzers - Maintenance and Technical /Vendor Support 
 
 
Outsourcing of Major Shutdowns (Planning, Preparation & Execution) 
is practiced by HABSHAN, where GASCO purchase materials and

retain shutdown maintenance of rotating equipment, control and 
electrical.  
 
 
The following factors should be considered when deciding whether or not to 
engage contractors: 
 
 
 
- 
Flexibility and peak shaving. 
 
 
- 
Contract out specialist service, these comprise: 
 
 
 
. 
Specialised knowledge. 
 
 
 
. 
Cost/ efficiency. 
 
 
 
. 
GASCO junior staff positions as required for Ex-OJT's development. 
 
 
 
. 
Dilution of skills:  the increasing use of contractors will affect 
GASCO's own skill base.  There will be a loss of supervisory training 
and experience assignments for younger staff (graduate development) 
where contractors are dominant. 
 
Attention needs to be paid for the following aspects to manage the contractors: 
 
 
- 
Credibility:  the more GASCO contracts out areas of key importance to its 
operations, the more it puts at risk its credibility in the eyes of third parties 
and Shareholders. 
 
 
- 
Loss of control:  GASCO may lose control over some of the important parts 
of the business.  Certainly quality control and cost consciousness may very 
well be at stake in areas where there is high use of contractors. Areas of 
concern to be turned into controls to ensure a good outcome. 
 
 
- 
Lower commitment/ loyalty:  because the contractor/ client relationship is 
time finite and based essentially on commercial aspects, there will be a lower 
commitment by contractors to the overall GASCO image and reputation.  
This may result in lower levels of safety consciousness, security control, 
efficiency and industrial relations management. To be managed through 
“performance management”. 
 
 
- 
Contractors breed contractors:  the more someone uses contractors to 
supervise contractors, the more he loses that particular range of managerial 
skills and the more he will be dependent on contractors. 
 
 
 
In applying the above policy a proper choice and variety of contractors is required 
to avoid jeopardising GASCO's freedom of action and economic interest.  If

5.3 
SPARES HOLDING STRATEGY 
 
 
GASCO's inventory objectives are to make provision for the availability of those 
materials and spare parts required to safeguard the Company's 'vital operations' 
whilst minimising investment in stock. 
 
 
'Vital' equipment classified as types 1 and 2 (Chapter 4, clause 4.1), the breakdown 
of which could lead to an immediate loss of production or would create unsafe 
conditions respectively.  For 'vital' equipment in particular the spare parts 
purchased shall, in all respects, be identical to the original component and clearly 
identified as such.  This shall mean in most cases purchasing directly from the 
original equipment manufacturer (OEM). 
 
 
For all consumables, i.e. frequently used spare parts and materials, rapid 
availability will be assured, either through stock or by arrangements with Abu 
Dhabi stock lists and suppliers, as appropriate. Long Term Purchase Agreements 
(LTPA) shall be developed when applicable. 
 
 
Stock review exercise is recommended to be carried out every three to four years 
with the participation of the Plants and the Engineering & Technical Support 
Division. 
 
 
Infrequently used spare parts shall, in principle, not be carried in stock for any 
other category of equipment.  However, items may be required when specifically 
identified as needed against planned overhaul schedules  (Reservation Request) or 
against direct charge requisitions in accordance with the Materials Procedures - 
Shutdowns. 
 
 
Infrequently used vital parts shall be carried in stock to safeguard operations.  
Where an item is common to more than one Site the combined risk of stock-out 
will be calculated and the safety stock held at one Site only. 
 
 
Infrequently used vital items, common to two or more Sites, will be stocked at one 
Site only to avoid duplication of stock holding.  Items frequently used will be 
stocked at each Site for convenience. 
 
 
Insurance spares are carried in stock to meet the overhaul requirements of 'vital' 
equipment in the shortest possible downtime of these equipment and to safeguard 
against unexpected failures (Insurance parts are usually high value items which are 
not normally required for ordinary maintenance).

5.3.1 Inventory Data Sharing (IDS) 
 
The Inventory Data Sharing System (IDS) is Web enabled computer 
software, objective of which is to share the inventory data of ADNOC 
Group of companies for the purpose of: 
- 
Exchange of materials during Emergency 
− Inventory reduction 
− Exchange of technical and commercial   information (Price, OCM) 
 
The system provides a single point search for complete inventory data of 
ADNOC Group of companies sharing this system, which is automatically 
up-dated, on daily basis, through interface from member companies’ ERP 
system to the Central Server. 
 
5.4 
CONDITION MONITORING 
 
 
Condition monitoring is a major tool to attain the GASCO Maintenance Policy 
objectives.  It aims first to anticipate possible failure and also to monitor the main 
equipment performance so that they remain in accordance with the requirements.  
The basis of condition monitoring is 'WATCHING OF TRENDS' over a certain 
period, and for this an easy and reliable method of data logging is essential. 
 
 
Under the present GASCO conditions of good quality fuel, good air filtration, 
running on base load, regular boroscopic inspection and good prescribed 
maintenance, condition monitoring should enable extended running time of major 
rotating equipment without taking undue risks. 
 
 
Engineering & Technical Support Division shall play a coordinating role in the full 
establishment and standardisation of condition monitoring techniques used at the 
Plants.  The Plants are responsible for maintaining their condition monitoring 
programme and keeping Engineering & Technical Support Division advised of the 
highlights of their results. 
 
 
Back-up may be sought from vibration expert and vendor specialists. 
 
 
Engineering & Technical Support Division assist the Plants in the follow-up of 
their programmes and disseminates information on rotating equipment findings 
between the Plants. 
 
Note: This aspect will be covered in more detail in the reliability policy.

5.5 
RUNNING MODE OF EQUIPMENT 
 
 
GASCO's equipment are designed for continuous duty and therefore present no 
problem in running for very long periods. 
 
 
As all Plants carry out efficient maintenance, the concern is not only for the 
equipment which is running but also for that which remains on standby mode, i.e. 
idle but ready for operation on demand. 
 
 
 
The plants follow operating instructions for the running modes of the equipment; 
guidelines for the main equipment are covered below. 
 
 
 
- 
Power Generation Package: 
 
 
 
 
GASCO policy is to run N+1 generators where 'N' is the number of 
generators required to supply the Plant load.  In addition there shall be 
one generator Available But Not Required (ABNR).  However this 
ABNR generator may be taken for maintenance work. 
 
 
 
 
The flexibility given by the possible utilisation of the grid shall be 
considered to optimise the modes of operation. The Power 
Interconnection Agreement governs the terms and conditions of 
operation and maintenance of interconnection system between all 
parties connected through the grid.  
 
 
 
Equipment With Standby (Equipment Configuration 2x100): 
 
 
 
- 
Air Compressors: 
 
 
 
 
These equipment are driven by high voltage (HV) electrical motors.  
It is not recommended that high voltage rated motors be started 
frequently therefore equipment change over every two months would 
be satisfactory. 
 
 
 
 
A space of equivalent to six months to one year running hours is 
recommended between units to avoid simultaneous failure due to 
aging or defect as well as spare parts optimisation. 
 
 
 
- 
Auxiliary Pumps (within a package): 
 
 
 
These equipment are driven by low voltage (LV) electrical motors.  
Changeover is recommended on a monthly basis or based on 
opportunity when it involves risk of tripping the package. 
 
 
 
 
 
A space of equivalent to six months to one year running hours is to be 
kept between two pumps of the same function.

.

.

5.6 
MAINTENANCE MANAGEMENT SYSTEMS 
 
 
In order to manage maintenance effectively & properly, computerised management 
systems are essential.   
 
The computerised systems, as minimum requirement, should cover the 
following four elements, which the management can use for control: 
 
 
 
EQUIPMENT, MANPOWER, MATERIALS AND COST. 
 
These systems must work as an integral whole because there are many 
inter-relationships between them.  They exchange information, share data 
and influence each other. 
 
(i) 
Equipment History Control: 
      For the equipment which fails and consumes maintenance effort. 
 
(ii) Manpower Control: 
 
For the manpower hours details to maintain the equipment. 
 
(iii) Material Control: 
 
 
For the materials used to maintain the equipment. 
 
(iv) Cost Control: 
 
 
For the costs incurred while maintaining the equipment. 
 
(v) 
Management Reporting: 
 
 
To keep Management informed.

5.6.1 GASCO CMMMS (Maximo) (Appendix 5.2) 
  
GASCO uses one of the best-Computerised Maintenance & Materials 
Management System (CMMMS), which is interfaced with financial 
computerised system (SmartStream) to cover all the above mention 
aspects. The CMMMS (Maximo) has accumulated considerable 
maintenance history since its implementation in year 1999. Effective 
usage of the system including analysis of historical data could reveal 
appreciable benefits to the company. 
 
The functionalities of the system have been enhanced where it was found 
absolutely necessary to handle the business effectively (refer to Maximo 
Bulletins under Maximo Manuals at GASCO intranet site for more 
information).  
The system has vast capabilities of analyzing maintenance data, various 
analysis reports are available and other reports can be developed as and 
when required using reporting tools. The system provides facility to 
analyse the following two aspects of the total maintenance cost. 
 
 
 
Indirect costs for GASCO are mainly loss of production or loss of 
opportunity (revenue loss): 
 
(i) 
The Runlog reporting application of Maximo is designed to analyse 
the equipment performances.  Failure analysis, Mean Time Between 
Failure (MTBF), utilisation factor, reliability and availability are the 
main aspects covered by this sub-system. 
 
(ii) The Runlog records all events related to the main package 
components. 
 
 
 
 
Analysis of equipment key performance indicators (MTBF, 
reliability, availability, trips and failure rate - bathtub curve) is 
being done at 12 months operation intervals for each Plant.  It leads 
to recommendations on the maintenance being applied and actions 
to maintain or improve the equipment performance.  The analysis 
report should be produced shortly after the completion of the 
operation interval being analysed. 
 
 
 
 
Due to large number of packages, only the most important ones 
have to be studied and priority action list established.  
Recommendations could be:  material changes, manufacturer 
changes, or any other modification, including operating and 
maintenance procedures.

B. 
Direct Costs: 
 
 
 
 The other Modules of Maximo, viz WORK ORDER, LABOUR, 
CONTRACTS and EQUIPMENT give detailed reports of the 
expenses in terms of man-hours, spare parts, contracts and also in 
terms of routine, scheduled, unscheduled maintenance types. 
 
 
 
The aim of the Maximo system is not to produce a finance report 
but to analyse the expenses, to give Engineering a reliable tool for 
COST CONTROL and to enable them to take action to reduce the 
direct costs. 
 
 
 
The direct maintenance cost per package of all GASCO major 
process and power generation packages should be  analysed on a 
calendar year basis.  Action on areas of high maintenance cost either 
in material consumption or manpower/ contracts cost is 
recommended to optimise the maintenance cost.  This analysis can 
be performed through the use of standard Maximo reports or with 
the help of crystal reports.. Subsequent studies shall be done by 
Plant Maintenance Departments in cooperation with Engineering & 
Technical Support Division to draw the final conclusions and 
recommendations. 
 
 
 
 
5.6.2    Maximo Data Up-Dating Responsibilities 
 
The processing of maintenance business through the system (Maximo) 
largely dependents on the accuracy of available static data. it is therefore 
highly important to maintain this data up-to-date in order to ensure the 
integrity of the executed business. The responsibilities for up-dating above 
mentioned data are laid down in the following paragraphs in order to 
remove any ambiguities in this regard. For further information refer to the 
Maintenance 
Management 
Business 
Process 
document 
(EPR/AGM(O)/0007), which have been recently reviewed and developed. 
 
 
5.6.2.1    Asset Management Data Up-Dating 
 
 
 
Any changes to the installed equipment (assets) shall be 
promptly reflected in Equipment, Preventive Maintenance & 
Plans Modules of Maximo. This includes changes due to 
equipment relocation/ replacements, PCRs and Projects. The 
responsibilities in each case are as follows:

a) 
Equipment Relocation or Replacement  
 
  
 
Each respective discipline (Mechanical, Instrument, 
Electrical or Inspection) is custodian of his related data 
in Maximo. The custodian section is responsible to up-
date the data affected due to relocation or replacement of 
any equipment. 
 
b) 
Plant Changes (PCRs) 
 
The responsibility to up-date Maximo data related to 
PCRs is addressed in the PCR procedure. 
 
c) 
Projects 
 
The responsibility to up-date Maximo data for the jobs 
related to projects rests with Projects group. This include 
any job performed by Projects Division e.g. new projects, 
expansions, revamping or upgrading  
 
5.6.2.2     Maintaining Labours’ Data  
 
The labours’ records need to be kept up-to-date since the time 
sheets of contracted manpower are processed through 
Maximo. Engineering Planning & Reliability Department is 
responsible for ensuring the accuracy of Head Office labour 
records in coordination with the respective departments. This 
responsibility for other divisions lies within their organisation 
(e.g. Planning Section for the labour records of Plants and 
Pipeline Divisions).  
 
5.6.2.3     Maintaining Materials and Contracts Data  
 
The accuracy of materials data is highly important for the 
smooth execution of maintenance business. Procurement 
Division (PD) is responsible for maintaining the data related to 
Materials and Contracts/ Agreements. 
Note: Attached block diagram shows Maximo system support 
interfaces (Appendix 5.2)

TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS 
 
 
Refer to Engineering Policy No. EPL/AGM (O)/0002 for the “Application of 
Standards and Specifications”.

CHAPTER 6.0 
 
 
MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
 
 
 
===========================

## Page 87

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 1 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
As set out in the Maintenance Strategy the emphasis for maintenance of process equipment 
is on 'PREDICTION OF WHAT WILL HAPPEN' and 'ANTICIPATION OF THE 
EVENTS'. 
 
To achieve this goal the equipment will be maintained as follows. 
 
'Condition Based Maintenance' for rotating equipment means that the machine is run until 
its condition deteriorates to the point where maintenance action is clearly called for.  The 
number of scheduled shutdowns are reduced to the minimum and only certain maximum 
limitations on run lengths are applied, based on experience gained. 
 
For rotating equipment 'CONDITION MONITORING' and 'FORWARD PLANNING' are 
essential tools to achieve 'on condition maintenance'. 
 
For static equipment 'on condition maintenance' implies ON-STREAM INSPECTION, 
REFERENCE EQUIPMENT AND FORWARD PLANNING. 
 
PREVENTIVE MAINTENANCE also implies a technical evaluation of maintenance 
problems.  The objective is to identify the cause of these problems and to eliminate, or at 
least minimise, their causes.  In this way a continuous improvement can be expected in 
equipment reliability, maintainability and availability. 
 
 
6.1 
MAJOR ROTATING EQUIPMENT 
 
 
On condition maintenance should be applied. Inspection frequencies are 
determined taking into account: 
 
 
- 
manufacturers recommendations 
 
- 
results of past experience 
 
- 
condition monitoring 
 
- 
performance analysis 
- 
Life enhancement programs, retrofits, …etc. 
 
 
Major rotating equipment are usually sent to the OEM or a qualified non-OEM 
facilities for overhaul, repair, or refurbishment based on inspection results and 
running hours. 
 
 
When Avons, LM2500 GE/PE, Solar  or Frame V/ Frame VI Gas Turbine major 
components are sent for overhaul/ repair, a GASCO representative or a nominated 
Third Party  will visit the contractor workshop facilities where the parts are sent: 
 
 
 
- 
to discuss inspection findings, repair procedures, replacement scheme of 
parts and assess remaining lifetime and establish a repair and inspection 
standard for the whole fleet in operation.

## Page 88

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 2 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
- 
to review the applied quality assurance and spot check its implementation. 
 
 
The experiences shall be communicated to all Plants. 
 
 
For compressor, power turbine rotors and any other component refurbishment, the 
requirement of a GASCO representative or a nominated Third Party  visit to the 
contractors facilities is established by considering each individual case. 
 
 
6.1.1    Aero Derivative Gas Generators/ Turbines 
 
6.1.1a Rolls Royce Avon Gas Generator 
 
The following terminology is observed: 
 
- 
Engine running hours since new (TSN) 
- 
Engine running hours since overhaul (TSO) 
- 
Running hours since repair (TSR). 
- 
Original Equipment Manufacturer (OEM) 
 
 
 
- 
Air Intake and Filtration: 
 
 
 
 
The basic maintenance for the air intake and filtration system is very 
important for efficient and reliable engines. Poor air intake and 
filtration system will result in rapid deterioration to the performance 
of the engine, will reduce the lifetime of internal engine components 
and will have a negative impact on repair/overhaul cost. 
 
 
 
 
 
Increased attention must be taken to ensure the good performance of 
the system. 
 
 
- 
Air Inlet Duct Trash Screens (if fitted): 
 
 
 
Trash screen (safety screen) is the last defence against failure of air 
filter elements. However, there is no defence against the failure of 
the trash screens. 
 
 
 
Therefore, the inspection for the trash screen should be planned to 
coincide with the other scheduled maintenance. This is to ascertain 
early signs of deterioration and perform remedial actions.

## Page 89

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 3 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
 
 
- 
Compressor Cleaning: 
 
 
 
a) 
Crank Washing: 
 
 
 
 
Crank washing is an important activity on the gas generator.  
A clean gas generator operates with higher efficiency, reduced 
exhaust gas temperature and thus extends the life of 
combustion components, optimises on fuel consumption and 
avoids to some extent the corrosive sediments on the various 
components.  However if misapplied it might contribute to the 
corrosion of the gas generator components and might affect 
the driven power turbine. 
 
 
 
 
It is imperative to crank wash overhauled engines when 
installing it on a berth to remove the preservation material. 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
Periodicity:  washing should be applied 'on condition', i.e. 
whenever the gas generator becomes dirty which should be 
established utilising rather simple methods of condition 
monitoring. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The opportunity of scheduled shutdowns (e.g. boroscopic 
inspection or high efficiency air filter replacement or trips)  
should be utilised, to the possible extent, to perform crank 
washing. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
If conducting crank washing would result in production 
losses then Plant Management to evaluate the situation and 
present a recommendation to the Assistant General 
Manager (Operations) for his decision. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
In every case crank washing is to be performed at least 
once a year. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
Application:  the cleaning solution with the application 
method should conform to vendor’s recommendation with 
the adjustments gained via GASCO’s experience. 
 
. 
Precautions:  special attention shall be made to ensure 
that the compressor is properly rinsed from the washing 
effluents and dirt.  This is to prevent any adverse effect on 
the engine components and the power turbine.  In addition, 
the Avon must be started to dry after the crank wash to 
prevent compressor blade corrosion.  
 
 
 
 
. 
The Preventive Maintenance checklist tasks should be 
performed during the crank wash.

## Page 90

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 4 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
b) 
Avon Preservation 
 
. 
After Crank Soak Wash:  if the Avon, after completing 
the above crank soak wash tasks along with the dry out fire 
run of approximately 5 minutes, is not required for 
operation for more than 24 hours, then the following is 
applicable: 
 
1) Up to 3 days  
: fit covers & blanks 
4 to 10 days  
: insert V.P.I/C.H.C paper 
and fit covers & blanks 
10 days to 3 months 
: inhibit the compressor; 
insert V.P.I/C.H.C paper 
and fit covers & blanks 
 
The inhibited engine should be crank soak washed 
every 3 weeks followed by a dry out fire run and re-
inhibited again. This is due to the fact that the 
properties of the inhibiting fluid are time dependent. 
 
2) If the above procedure cannot be followed, the Avon 
must be crank soak washed followed by a fire run of 
approximately 5 minutes on a weekly basis. 
 
Shutdown Procedure: 
 
Shutdown duration up to 24 hours does not require 
preservation of Avon. However, if the shutdown is planned 
for more than 24 hours, it is recommended to first crank 
soak wash the Avon along with a dry out fire run, and then 
the procedure to be followed as indicated in the above item 
#1. 
 
For unit equipped with Turbine Inlet Cooling Fogging 
System: the fogging system shall be shut-off before turbine 
stop by about 10 minutes for drying the air inlet plenum 
and turbine early stages for avoiding any adverse effect 
from the water mist. 
 
Removal for Overhaul or Repair: 
 
 
 
 
A crank soak wash followed by a fire run and inhibiting 
should be carried out prior to Avon removal. If, due to the 
operational reasons, the crank washing / firing is not 
possible, then the engine is to be sent in its condition.

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 5 of 54 
Issue No: 6
