:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
 
 
 
 
c) 
On-Line Cleaning: 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
Soft Blasting:  the carbo/soft blast compressor cleaning 
method is no longer approved by the OEM. 
 
. 
Fired Fluids Wash:  it will not eliminate or be as effective 
as crank washing. The performance recovery after each 
fired wash is a little less, until the point is reached where 
the only solution is to carry out crank wash.  Fired Fluid 
Wash should be started on a clean compressor (preferably 
new or overhauled engines).  The frequency of application 
should be once per week (minimum).  It may increase, to 
twice weekly or daily, depending on the airborne 
contamination.  In all cases, crank washing must be carried 
out at regular intervals.  This method has never been used 
in GASCO because of the high frequency application. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
However, should any berth specific operating conditions 
justify using this method, it can be used by installing a 
special kit that includes spray nozzles, tank and pump.  
The correct and suitable washing fluid type and mixture 
must be used according to the OEM recommendation. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Flame Tubes and Discharge Nozzles: 
 
 
 
 
 
Due to the susceptibility of flame tubes and discharge nozzles 
to burn out/crack possibly due to liquid carryover or gas 
composition, all OEM recommended modifications which 
provide a potential improvement to the life of flame tubes and 
discharge nozzles shall be evaluated and incorporated. 
 
 
 
 
 
In addition, the replacement of the flame tubes and the 
discharge nozzles will be guided if possible by the indication 
made by the OEM that they should have a recommended finite 
life of 60,000 hours. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Burners Check and Cleaning: 
 
 
 
 
 
Dirty burners affect the homogeneous pattern of the flame that 
causes overheating, buckling or burning of the flame tubes, or 
in other words, it leads to a rapid deterioration or damage of 
the flame tubes.

## Page 92

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 6 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
 
 
 
 
Burners checking and cleaning should be performed along 
with the boroscopic inspection.  Cleaning shall be made for the 
assembled burners following the latest procedure set by the 
OEM. No attempt shall be made to disassemble the burners on 
site since a specialised calibration device is required. 
 
It is imperative to ensure that the fuel burners are of the same 
configuration (Pepperpot or Pintle type) during maintenance/ 
replacement. No attempt shall be made to mix fuel burners of 
different configuration during replacement and/ or storage. 
  
 
 
 
 
The burners calibration and adjustment is normally performed 
at the repair workshop for the complete set.  This is to ensure 
that the fuel flow is equally distributed to reduce the deviation 
in the exhaust cone temperature.  
 
 
 
 
- 
Boroscopic Inspection: 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
Boroscopic inspection being an important part of the 
condition monitoring should be conducted thoroughly.  It 
is required as minimum on a yearly basis (starting from the 
date of installation on berth) or 8,000 hours whichever 
comes first. However, in view of the fast deterioration that 
could occur to the flame tubes, the boroscopic inspection 
intervals should be planned according to flame tubes 
condition which can be predicted during previous 
boroscopic inspection or from condition monitoring data. 
 
 In addition, the following is recommended: 
 
 
- 
To carry out a boroscopic inspection before the end 
of the warranty period for any new, overhauled 
and/ or repaired engine. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
- 
To carry out boroscopic inspection between 27,000 
to 28,000 running hours after overhaul or at the 
first nearest shutdown. This is to assess the 
condition of the Avon and to confirm if it can 
continue in operation until its planned removal for 
overhaul. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
Boroscope inspection shall preferably be carried out with 
expert assistance to assess the internal condition of the hot 
gas path, turbine blades and nozzles, as well as a general 
visual inspection of front compressor casing, blades and 
accessories.

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 7 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
. 
The Preventive Maintenance checklist tasks should be 
performed during the Boroscope inspection. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Flexible Hoses Storage/Inspection/ Replacement: 
 
 
There is no mandatory instruction from the OEM to replace 
flexible hoses after specific operating hours. However, 
GASCO has experienced unplanned shutdowns due to 
problems faced with such hoses. Therefore, the following 
guidelines and recommendations are to be implemented: 
 
 
. 
Properly identify and store the flexible hoses (e.g. part 
number, specification, usage, test date, storage as 
specified, etc.). 
 
 
. 
Replace flexible hoses every 30,000 hours or every 4 years 
since installation, whichever comes first (this could be 
matched with the Avon removal for normal overhaul). 
 
 
. 
Follow Rolls Royce installation procedure under Service 
Bulletin No. 55 to avoid twisting, improper bends, etc. 
Also, attach tags indicating installation date. 
 
 
. 
Inspect the flexible hoses during the planned maintenance 
activities as highlighted in the Avon Task Force 
Maintenance Checklists. 
 
- 
Lube and Hydraulic Oil System: 
 
.  Check the scavenge pump performance during the 
baroscopic inspection and at each replacement.  
 
.     If the Avon engine has suffered from flooding with oil, the 
engine should be removed from service and to be sent to 
an Overhaul Base for disassembly and cleaning. Restarting 
of engine Flooded with oil is prohibited. 
  
- 
Check the accumulators charging pressure during the  
boroscopic inspection.

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 8 of 54 
Issue No: 6

:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
 
 
 
- 
Repairs and Overhauls: 
 
 
 
Avon Overhaul 
 
 
 
 
 
As Avon engine is not basically a modular engine, AVONS 
are replaced and sent for overhaul as a complete unit: 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
AVONS first life was determined whenever operating 
conditions or inspection results did not reveal any 
deterioration in the condition of the engine initiating a 
premature overhaul/repair, the engines were sent for 
overhaul between 30,000 and 35,000 running hours. 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
AVONS second life was determined based on the actual 
running condition and the findings during the first 
overhaul. The engines were sent for overhaul between 
29,000 and 33,000 running hours TSO. 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
However, the experience gained during the previous lives, 
the completed overhauls and the current fourth life shows 
that it is reasonable to send the AVONS for overhaul 
between 28,000 and 31,000 running hours TSO. 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
Similarly the recommendation for the fifth life is to send 
the AVONS for overhaul between 28,000 and 31,000 
running hours TSO providing that the boroscope 
inspection and condition monitoring do not reveal any 
deterioration in the condition of the engine which would 
initiate a premature overhaul. 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
During AVONS first and second lives the tendency was to 
reduce overhaul cost by utilising serviceable parts.  As 
GASCO AVONS are exceeding the 100,000 hours TSN, 
the tendency for parts replacement will increase.  Future 
attempts shall be made to utilise new parts rather than 
serviceable parts. 
 
. 
During Avons overhauls, apply the rejuvenation heat 
treatment process on turbine blades as per the following: 
 
- For turbine blades accumulated up to 60,000 hours 
TSN: Perform creep life assessment on sample blade. If 
passed, perform normal overhaul process. If failed, 
apply rejuvenation process.

## Page 95

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 9 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
- 
For turbine blades accumulated above 60,000 hours 
TSN: apply the rejuvenation process. 
 
 
- 
For rejuvenated turbine blades: During their next 
overhaul (i.e. after 30,000 hours of rejuvenation) 
perform creep life assessment on a sample blade; if 
passed perform normal overhaul process. If failed re-
apply rejuvenation process. 
 
 
 
 
 
Avon Repair: 
 
 
 
 
 
An Avon being not basically a modular engine, repair on Site 
in case of failure is limited to the so called 'Avon heavy 
maintenance'. 
 
Based on the above, in case of any failure not repairable in-
house, the complete engine shall be replaced and sent to an 
overhaul base for evaluating the extent of damage and advice 
the recommended repair/ overhaul work scope. An economical 
evaluation of a repair versus complete overhaul shall be made 
taking into consideration the accumulated running hours.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
- 
Start Up Precaution: 
 
 
 
 
The correct operation of the bleed valves and IGV ram are set 
and recorded by the repair/ overhaul workshop during the 
performance test.  As the correct settings are very important to 
prevent surge, they must be rechecked on Site to ensure 
settings have not been changed during the transportation to the 
Site and/or installation on the berth. 
 
 
 
 
 
It is recommended that the vendor specialist should perform 
this check. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Reporting: 
 
 
 
 
Since Avon gas generators are being interchanged within 
GASCO Plants, it is recommended that the following reports 
be dispatched to the Head of Rotating Equipment Engineering 
Department for a coordination point of view: 
 
 
 
 
 
- 
monthly digest sheet 
 
 
 
 
- 
inspection report 
 
 
 
 
- 
failure report 
 
 
 
 
- 
Site modification report 
 
 
 
 
- 
Avon Task Force Maintenance checklists 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
A copy of the history file shall remain with the engine.

The following terminology is observed:

-

-          Hot section  running hours since new (HTSN) 
-          Hot section  running hours since overhaul (HTSO)

- 
Air Intake and Filtration: 
 
The basic maintenance for the air intake and filtration system is very 
important for efficient and reliable engines. Poor air intake and filtration 
systems will result in rapid deterioration to the performance of the engine, 
will reduce the lifetime of internal engine components and will have a 
negative impact on repair/overhaul cost. 
 
 
 
 
Increased attention must be taken to ensure the good performance of the 
system. 
 
- 
Air Inlet Duct Trash Screens: 
 
 
Trash screen (safety screen) is the last defence against failure of air filter 
elements. However, there is no defence against the failure of the trash 
screens. 
 
 
Therefore, the inspection for the trash screen should be planned to coincide 
with the other scheduled maintenance. This is to ascertain early signs of 
deterioration and perform remedial actions. 
 
 
- 
Compressor Cleaning: 
 
 
 
a) 
Crank Washing: 
 
Crank washing is an important activity on the gas generator.  A clean 
gas generator operates with higher efficiency, reduced exhaust gas 
temperature and thus extends the life of hot section components, 
optimises on fuel consumption and avoids to some extent the corrosive 
sediments on the various components.  However if misapplied it might 
contribute to the corrosion of the gas generator components and might 
affect the driven power turbine. 
 
 
 
 
It is imperative to crank wash new or overhauled engines when first 
installed on berth to remove preservation material, if any.

This is to prevent any adverse effect on the engine components and 
the power turbine.  In addition, the engine must be started to dry 
after the crank wash for approximately 5 minutes to prevent 
corrosion of the compressor components. 
 
 
 
b)     On-Line Cleaning: 
 
 
  
. Fired Fluids Wash:  it will not eliminate or be as effective as crank 
washing. The performance recovery after each fired wash is a little 
less, until the point is reached where the only solution is to carry out 
crank wash.  Fired Fluid Wash should be started on a clean 
compressor (preferably new or overhauled engines).  The frequency 
of application should be once per week (minimum).  It may 
increase, to twice weekly or daily, depending on the airborne 
contamination. This method has never been used in GASCO 
because of the high frequency application. 
 
 
However, should any berth specific operating conditions justify 
using this method, it can be used by installing a special kit that 
includes spray nozzles, tank and pump.  The correct and suitable 
washing fluid type and mixture must be used according to O.E.M. 
recommendation. 
 
 
 
 
 
. Soft Blasting:  the carbo/soft blast compressor cleaning method is 
not approved by the OEM.

c) 
LM2500 Preservation: 
 
After Crank Soak Wash:  if the engine, after completing the crank 
soak wash tasks along with the dry out fire run of approximately 5 
minutes, is not required for operation for more than 15 days, then 
the engine should be preserved either by motoring every 15 days or 
by oil wetting of engine bearings every 60 days as per the OEM 
technical procedures ref. WP 501 00. 
  
Shutdown Procedure: 
 
Shutdown duration up to 15 days does not require preservation of 
engine. However, if the shutdown is planned for more than 15 days, 
it is recommended to first crank soak wash the engine followed a 
dry out fire run for approximately 5 minutes, and then the procedure 
to be followed as indicated above. 
 
For unit equipped with Turbine Inlet Cooling by Fogging System: 
the fogging system shall be shut-off before turbine stop by about 10 
minutes for drying the air inlet plenum and turbine early stages for 
avoiding any adverse effect from the water mist. 
 
Removal for Overhaul or Repair: 
 
The engine should be crank soak washed followed by a fire run for 
approximately 5 minutes prior to engine removal. If, due to the 
operational reasons, the crank washing / firing is not possible, then 
the engine is to be removed from berth in its condition, 
transferred/installed and preserved in its shipping pressurised 
container (refer to OEM WP 503 00, WP 505 00 & SWP 503 01). 
 
Spare Engine Preservation: 
 
After the initial preservation and installation into the pressurised 
shipping container, the container pressure and humidity indicator 
should be periodically monitored between 30 to 60 days (refer to 
OEM WP 500 00). 
 
 
- 
Fuel Nozzles Check and Cleaning: 
 
Dirty fuel nozzles affect the homogeneous pattern of the flame that causes 
overheating, buckling or burning of the combustor/hot gas path 
components, or in other words, it leads to a rapid deterioration or damage 
of these components.

Fuel nozzles checking and cleaning should be performed along with the 
Boroscopic inspection.  Cleaning shall be made for the assembled nozzles 
following the latest procedure set by the OEM. No attempt shall be made 
to disassemble the fuel nozzles on site since a specialised calibration 
device is required. 
 
The fuel nozzles calibration and adjustment is normally performed at the 
repair workshop for the complete set.  This is to ensure that the fuel flow 
is equally distributed to reduce the deviation in the exhaust temperature.   
 
- 
Boroscopic Inspection: 
 
· 
Boroscopic inspection being an important element of the condition 
monitoring and should be conducted thoroughly.  It is usually 
performed semi-annually  or 4,000 running hours (starting from the 
date of engine installation on berth), whichever comes first. However, 
in view of the fast deterioration that could occur to the engine hot 
section in some locations, the Boroscopic inspection intervals should 
be planned according to the condition which can be predicted during 
previous Boroscopic inspection or from condition monitoring data.  
 
In addition, the following is recommended: 
 
- 
To carry out a Boroscopic inspection before the end of the 
warranty period for any new, overhauled and or repaired engine.  
  
      - 
To carry out Boroscopic inspection between 22,000 to 23,000 
running hours HTSN or HTSO or 46,000 to 48,000 running hours 
ETSN or ETSO or at the first nearest shutdown opportunity. This 
is to assess the condition of the engine to confirm if it can 
continue in operation up till its planned removal for hot section 
overhaul (at 25,000 hours) or engine major overhaul (at 50,000 
run hors). 
 
· 
Boroscope inspection shall preferably be carried out with expert 
assistance to assess the internal condition of the compressor vanes and 
blades,  combustor, turbine blades and nozzles guide vanes,  as well 
as a general visual inspection of front compressor section and 
accessories. 
 
· 
The Preventive Maintenance Check Sheet tasks should be performed 
during the Boroscope inspection.

- 
Package Flexible Hoses Storage/Inspection/ Replacement: 
 
There is no mandatory instruction from the OEM to replace flexible hoses 
after specific operating hours. However, GASCO has experienced 
unplanned shutdowns due to problems faced with such hoses. Therefore, 
the following guidelines and recommendations are to be implemented: 
 
. 
Properly identify and store the flexible hoses (e.g. part number, 
specification, usage, test date, storage as specified,  etc.). 
 
. 
Replace flexible hoses every 50,000 hours during the engine 
removal for major overhaul. 
 
. 
Follow OEM guidelines for the installation procedure to avoid 
twisting, improper bends, etc. Also, attach tags indicating 
installation date. 
 
. 
Inspect the flexible hoses during the planned maintenance activities 
as highlighted in the LM2500 Maintenance Checklists. 
 
 
 
- 
Repairs and Overhauls: 
 
 
LM2500 Gas Generator equipped with the upgraded extended life hot 
section and running at EGT 835 ºC, shall be kept running up to its normal 
overhaul i.e. after 50,000 hours TSN/TSO without intermediate hot 
section overhaul after 25,000 hours. However, if the engine has been 
running at EGT 849 ºC, the hot section intermediate overhaul is to be 
planed every 30,000 hours TSN/TSO. 
 
LM2500 Hot Section Overhaul: 
 
The hot section should be replaced and sent for full overhaul as per the 
OEM guidelines after accumulating 25,000 running hours HTSN or 
HTSO, providing that the Boroscope inspection and condition monitoring 
do not reveal any deterioration in the condition of the engine which would 
initiate a premature overhaul. 
 
 
 
 
As per the OEM overhaul standard guidelines, the HP turbine rotor blades 
and nozzle guide vanes have a finite life limit. Therefore,  the total 
accumulated running hours of these components should be considered 
during the overhaul. The HP turbine rotor blades and the second stage 
nozzle guide vanes should be replaced after accumulating 50,000 running 
hours TSN (maximum of one repair is allowed) and the HP first stage 
nozzle guide vanes should be replaced when accumulating 75,000 
running hours TSN (maximum of two repairs are allowed).

The replacement components should be made with new. If serviceable 
parts are to be utilised, they should have accumulated a maximum of

LM2500 Major Overhaul 
 
The engine is to be replaced and sent for full overhaul as a complete unit 
after accumulating 50,000 running hours ETSN or ETSO, providing that 
the Boroscope inspection and condition monitoring do not reveal any 
deterioration in the condition of the engine which would initiate a 
premature overhaul. 
 
As per the OEM overhaul standard guide lines, the HP turbine rotor 
blades and nozzle guide vanes have a finite life limit. Therefore,  the total 
accumulated running hours of these components should be considered 
during the overhaul. The HP turbine rotor blades and the second stage 
nozzle guide vanes should be replaced after accumulating 50,000 running 
hours TSN  (maximum of one repair is allowed) and the HP first stage 
nozzle guide vanes should be replaced when accumulating 75,000 
running hours TSN (maximum of two repairs are allowed). The 
replacement components should be made with new.  If serviceable parts 
are to be utilised, they should have accumulated a maximum of 25,000 
hours TSN  and subjected only to one repair. 
 
Concerning the overhaul of the engine air compressor section assembly, 
attempts shall be made to utilise new parts rather than serviceable parts 
for up keeping the engine performance.   
 
 
- 
LM2500 Repair: 
 
As the LM engine is considered a modular engine, on site repair is 
possible, depending on the extent of damage and consequences, via 
replacing the defected module by a rotable module, if available in house.  
 
Failure Related To The Hot Section: 
 
The complete engine will be replaced by the spare engine. The defected 
hot section could be repaired in house, if possible. If not, it will be sent 
abroad to the overhaul base for repair or overhaul depending on the extent 
of damage and the accumulated running hours.  
 
Failure Related To The Air Compressor Section: 
 
 
 
 
The complete engine will be replaced by the spare engine. The defected 
section could be repaired in house, if possible. If not, the complete engine 
might be sent abroad, depending the down stream consequences, to the

6.1.1c Solar Centaur Gas Turbine 
 
 
 
The following terminology is observed:

-

- 
Air Intake and Filtration: 
 
The basic maintenance for the air intake and filtration system is very 
important for efficient and reliable engines. Poor air intake and filtration 
systems will result in rapid deterioration to the performance of the engine, 
will reduce the lifetime of internal engine components and will have a 
negative impact on repair/overhaul cost. 
 
Increased attention must be taken to ensure the good performance of the 
system.

- 
Air Inlet Duct Trash Screens (if fitted): 
 
 
Trash screen (safety screen) is the last defence against failure of air filter 
elements. However, there is no defence against the failure of the trash 
screens. 
 
 
Therefore, the inspection for the trash screen should be planned to coincide 
with the other scheduled maintenance. This is to ascertain early signs of 
deterioration and perform remedial actions. 
 
 
- 
Compressor Cleaning: 
 
 
 
a)      On -Crank Cleaning: 
 
Water Washing/Solvent Cleaning 
 
Crank washing is an important activity on the gas generator (GG).  A 
clean GG operates with higher efficiency, reduced exhaust gas 
temperature and thus extends the life of hot section components, 
optimises on fuel consumption and avoids to some extent the corrosive 
sediments on the various components.  However, if misapplied it might 
contribute to the corrosion of the gas generator components and might 
affect the driven power turbine. 
 
It is imperative to crank wash new or overhauled engines when first 
installed on berth to remove preservation material, if any. 
 
· Periodicity:  washing should be applied 'on condition', i.e. 
whenever the gas generator becomes dirty which should be 
established utilising rather simple methods of condition monitoring 
(at least 96 run hours between each cleaning cycle is allowed). 
 
The opportunity of scheduled shutdowns (e.g. Boroscopic 
inspection or high efficiency air filter replacement or trips)  should 
be utilised, to the possible extent, to perform crank washing. 
 
If conducting crank washing would result in production losses then 
Plant Management to evaluate the situation and present a 
recommendation to the Assistant General Manager (Operations) for 
his decision. 
 
 
 
 
· Application:  the cleaning fluid with the application method should 
conform to vendor’s recommendation with the adjustments gained 
via GASCO’s experience.

· Precautions:  special attention shall be made to ensure that the 
engine is properly rinsed and drained from the washing solution and 
dirt to prevent any adverse effect on the engine components and the 
power turbine.  In addition, the engine must be started to dry after 
the crank wash for approximately 5 minutes to prevent any side 
effect on engine components. 
 
 
 
  
Abrasive Cleaning: 
 
The abrasive compressor cleaning method is an approved method by 
the OEM. However, it should not be applied indiscriminately because 
blockage of cooling holes can occur. It must be applied only if the 
crank washing method does not restore engine performance. The 
engine should be stopped for preparation, normally started for cleaning 
ingestion process and stopped again after completion to return the 
engine to normal operating mode.  In addition, the GG speed should 
not exceed the idle speed (62 to 65 percent speed) during the cleaning 
process.  
 
 
 
b)     On-Line Cleaning: 
 
 
  
On-Line Cleaning “Fired Fluids Wash” will not eliminate or be as 
effective as crank washing. The performance recovery after each fired 
wash is a little less, until the point is reached where the only solution is 
to carry out crank wash.  Fired Fluid Wash should be started on a clean 
compressor (preferably new or overhauled engines).  The frequency of 
application should be once per week (minimum).  It may increase to 
twice  per week or once per day (at least 24 run hours between each 
cleaning cycle is allowed) depending on the airborne contamination. 
This method has never been used in GASCO due to high frequency of 
application. 
 
However, should any berth specific operating conditions justify using 
this method, it can be used by installing a special kit that includes 
spray nozzles, tank and pump.  The correct and suitable washing fluid 
type and mixture must be used according to OEM recommendation. 
 
-  
Preservation: 
 
After Crank Soak Wash:  
 
 
 
 
If the engine, after completing the crank soak wash tasks along with the dry 
out fire run of approximately 5 minutes, is not required for operation for 
more than 15 days, then the engine should be preserved by motoring every 
15 days. During this period, the auxiliaries shall be kept on Auto position 
(such that oil heater, oil pump, … etc.).

Shutdown Procedure: 
 
Shutdown duration up to 15 days does not require preservation of engine. 
However, if the shutdown is planned for more than 15 days, it is 
recommended to first crank soak wash the engine followed by a dry out fire 
run for approximately 5 minutes, and then the procedure to be followed as 
indicated above. 
 
For unit equipped with Turbine Inlet Cooling by Fogging System: the 
fogging system shall be shut-off before turbine stop by about 10 minutes for 
drying the air inlet plenum and turbine early stages for avoiding any adverse 
effect from the water mist. 
 
Removal for Overhaul or Repair: 
 
The engine should be crank soak washed followed by a fire run for 
approximately 5 minutes prior to engine removal. If, due to the operational 
reasons, the crank washing / firing is not possible, then the engine is to be 
removed from berth in its condition, transferred/installed and preserved in 
the shipping container.  
 
 
Spare Engine Preservation (if any): 
 
The overhaul base preserves the overhauled/repaired engine for short-term 
storage unless specified by User.  For long-term storage and after the initial 
preservation, the engine should be installed in a pressurised container. The 
container pressure and humidity indicator should be periodically monitored 
between 30 to 60 days. 
 
 
 
Fuel Injectors Check and Cleaning: 
 
Dirty fuel injectors affect the homogeneous pattern of the flame that 
causes overheating, buckling or burning of the combustor/hot gas path 
components, or in other words, it leads to a rapid deterioration or damage 
of these components. 
 
Fuel injectors checking and cleaning should be performed along with the 
Boroscopic inspection and shall be made following the latest procedure 
set by the OEM.

Boroscopic Inspection: 
 
· 
Boroscopic inspection being an important element of the condition 
monitoring and should be conducted thoroughly.  It is usually 
performed semi-annually  or every 4,000 running hours (starting from 
the date of engine installation on berth), whichever comes first. 
However, in view of the fast deterioration that could occur to the 
engine hot section in some locations, the Boroscopic inspection 
intervals should be planned according to the condition which can be 
predicted during previous Boroscopic inspection or from condition 
monitoring data.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
In addition, the following is recommended: 
 
  
 
- 
To carry out a Boroscopic inspection before the end of the 
warranty period for any new, overhauled and or repaired engine.  
  
      - 
To carry out Boroscopic inspection between 32,000 to 33,000 
running hours TSN or TSO or at the first nearest shutdown 
opportunity. This is to assess the condition of the engine to 
confirm if it can continue in operation up till its planned removal 
for engine major/standard overhaul. 
 
· 
Boroscope inspection shall preferably be carried out with expert 
assistance to assess the internal condition of the engine (compressor 
IGV’s & OGV’s,  combustor, turbine blades and nozzles guide vanes, 
..etc) as well as general visual inspection of the engine and 
accessories. 
 
· 
The Preventive Maintenance Check Sheet tasks should be performed 
during the Boroscope inspection. 
 
 
- 
Package Flexible Hoses Storage/Inspection/ Replacement: 
 
There is no mandatory instruction from the OEM to replace flexible hoses 
after specific operating hours. However, GASCO has experienced 
unplanned shutdowns due to problems faced with such hoses. Therefore, 
the following guidelines and recommendations are to be implemented: 
 
. 
Properly identify and store the flexible hoses (e.g. part number, 
specification, usage, test date, etc.). 
 
. 
Replace flexible hoses every 35,000 hours or every 4 years since 
installation, whichever comes first. This could be matched with the 
engine removal for normal overhaul.

- 
Overhauls & Repairs: 
 
Solar Centaur Gas Generator is not basically modular engine. Therefore, 
the engine is to be replaced and sent for overhaul/repair as a complete 
unit:   
 
 
  
Overhaul 
 
Two types of overhauls are available: “standard overhaul” and “major 
overhaul”. 
In a “major overhaul” the engine is returned to current production 
standards for mechanical configuration and aero thermal performance via 
incorporating the latest product improvement and performance testing.  
On the other hand, in a “standard overhaul” the product and durability 
improvements are incorporated only if specifically requested at additional 
cost. These improvements should be evaluated prior to implementation. 
 
The engine is to be removed/replaced and sent for overhaul as a complete 
unit every 35,000 running hours TSN or TSO, providing that the 
Boroscope inspection and or condition monitoring do not reveal any 
deterioration in the condition of the engine which would initiate a 
premature overhaul. 
 
During the overhaul work, attempts shall be made to utilise new 
replacement parts rather than serviceable parts for up keeping the engine 
performance.   
 
 
- 
Repair: 
 
The on site repair is limited to the external components (e.g. accessory 
drive gear box replacement, air bleed valve inspection/repair, IGV ram 
replacement, variable guide vanes operating mechanism inspection and 
setting, etc).  
 
Based on the above, in case of any internal failure the complete engine 
will be replaced and sent abroad to the overhaul base for evaluating the 
extent of damage and advise the recommended repair/overhaul work 
scope. An economical evaluation of a repair versus complete overhaul 
will be made taking into consideration the accumulated running hours.

6.1.2    Heavy Frame Gas Turbines 
 
6.1.2a General Electric Frame V Gas Turbines 
 
 
 
- 
The following terminology is observed: 
 
 
 
 
CCI  
 = combustion chamber inspection 
 
 
 
CCI E 
 = extended combustion chamber inspection 
 
 
 
HGPI 
 = hot gas path inspection 
 
 
 
MAJOR  = major overhaul 
 
 
 
Conventional Parts = Original Hot Gas Path Parts 
 
 
 
Hi-Tech Parts        = Upgraded Hot Gas Parts 
 
- 
GASCO has operated Frame V gas turbines both single and twin 
shafts types for a number of years during which time four major 
overhauls (160,000 to 170,000 hours) of all process units (twin 
shafts) have been completed, whereas the programme for the second 
major overhauls (90,000 hours) of all power generation units (single 
shaft) has just been completed. All process units (twin shafts) are 
currently upgraded using Hi-Tech Hot Gas Parts. A wealth of 
operating and maintenance experience has been gained and will be 
used for planning of future overhauls. 
 
 
 
 
The requirement for replacement of aged components as per the 
maintenance strategy will be based on a life assessment. This

Twin Shaft Configuration 
 
 
 
- 
Variable Inlet Guide Vane (VIGV's) Inspection: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
This inspection shall be performed to coincide with the planned 
inspections: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
Every 12,000 to 15,000 hours at the same time for CCI, 
HGPI,CCIE or major overhaul. 
 
 
 
The Carpenter 450 type vanes and Chemloy bushings are used in 
GASCO. This new blade has a superior corrosion resistance and is 
expected to achieve a greater operational life. However, full 
inspection of the VIGV assembly is still required to be performed 
on a regular basis as detailed above to avoid potential failure. The 
inspection will consist of checks for bushing wear, thrust washer 
corrosion, blade corrosion pitting and erosion, etc. 
 
 
 
- 
Combustion Chamber Inspection (CCI): 
 
 
 
 
This inspection shall be done within 12,000 to 15,000 hours starting 
from the completion of CCIE or MAJOR for units without 
combustion chamber life extender kit. This inspection shall be 
eliminated after the introduction of the CC life extender kit. 
 
The CCI requires the change out fuel nozzles, combustion liners, 
retainers and crossfire tubes. 
 
 
 
 
Removed combustion parts shall be sent to GASCO approved 
contractor for refurbishing and shall be replaced by new or 
refurbished parts to minimise downtime. It is also recommended 
that two transition pieces be removed for assessment of their 
condition by dye penetrant test and to perform a visual inspection of 
the leading edge of 1st stage nozzle portion and buckets to note any 
wear or deterioration of these parts. The variable inlet guide vanes, 
inlet & exhaust ducts & other relevant systems shall be inspected. 
 
 
 
- 
Extended Combustion Chamber Inspection (CCI E): 
 
  
 
For Units having The Conventional Combustion Components ( i.e. 
Without CC Life Extender Kit): 
 
 
 
 
The CCIE shall be done with run lengths between 12,000 to 15,000 
hours from the previous CCI.  This inspection includes all items 
covered by the CCI and: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
- 
transition pieces inspection

expert assistance for this inspection.

Major Overhaul: 
 
 
 
 
The Major Overhaul shall be performed at 48,000 to 60,000 hours 
after completion of the previous major overhaul. 
 
The Major Overhaul shall include the following main items: - 
 
 
 
 
- 
Replacement of all combustion parts. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Removal of all compressor top half casings, cleaning and 
inspection. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Remove 1st stage nozzle and replace with a new or 
reconditioned set. 
 
 
 
 
- 
HP Rotor Assembly and Stator Blades:

Extended Combustion Chamber Inspection (CCI EXT): 
 
 
 
 
The CCI EXT shall only apply to those units utilising hot gas

inspection shall be done with run lengths between 10,000 and 
12,000 run hours from the previous CCI. The scope of work is the 
same as the twin shaft units. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
- 
Hot Gas Path Inspection (HGPI): 
 
 
 
 
The HGPI shall only apply to those units utilising conventional 
hot gas path parts, which accumulated more than 90,000 run 
hours. The HGPI shall be carried out at 20,000 to 24,000 hours 
after completion of the previous major overhaul. This inspection 
shall include the CCI as earlier described and in addition, a detailed 
inspection of the turbine section as outlined below: 
 
 
 
 
- 
Removal of the upper half of the turbine shell casing. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Remove transition pieces and 1st stage nozzle assembly and 
replace with refurbished components. 
 
- 
Remove 1st stage support ring (upper half). 
 
- 
NDT inspect 1st stage buckets (replace if required). 
 
Inspect the following: 
 
 
 
 
. 
1st stage shroud blocks, replace if required. 
 
. 
Turbine labyrinth seals (check integrity of wheel space 
thermocouples). 
 
. 
2nd stage buckets. 
 
. 
NDT inspect and dimensional check 2nd stage nozzle & 
diaphragm, replace if required. 
 
. 
Inspect 2nd stage shroud blocks, replace if required.

6.1.2b General Electric Frame 6001B Gas Turbines 
 
 
The following terminology is observed: 
 
 
 
VIGV  
= 
variable inlet guide vane inspection 
 
 
CCI 
 
= 
combustion chamber inspection 
 
 
HGPI  
= 
hot gas path inspection 
 
 
MAJOR 
= 
major overhaul 
 
 
The 
maintenance 
schedule 
and 
following 
sequence 
of 
inspections/overhauls shall remain in force for each power generation unit 
CCI - HGPI - CCI - MAJOR  
 
 
Based on previous experience, inspections and overhaul outages should 
be planned to minimise downtime and to achieve the most cost-effective 
solution. Lack of on-site adequate spares can have a major effect on plant 
availability; therefore prior to schedule disassembly type of inspection 
adequate spares should be on-site. 
 
 
It should be emphasized that the requirement for replacing aged 
components as per the maintenance strategy is based on life assessment. 
This assessment is made by a metallurgical destructive test on the 
components during the refurbishment process.  Nevertheless NDT checks 
are still required to be performed on all the primary parts i.e. combustion 
components/nozzles/blades/discs etc during a planned inspection. 
 
Note:  
 
Visits to refurbishment shops will be made to discuss and evaluate new 
repair techniques and the type of coatings that are necessary to various 
components. 
 
 
- 
Maintenance: 
 
- 
Air intake and filtration, air inlet duct trash screens, air 
compressor crank washing and on-line fire fluid wash shall 
follow the same approach as for the Avon gas generator in 
6.1.1. However cleaning agents and application should follow 
General Electric recommendations. 
- 
As the control system has TMR, the mechanical over speed 
Trip shall be tested at every HGPI and Major Overhaul.  
 
 
 
- 
It is recommended to utilise the manufacturer/approved vendor 
experts for the following inspections. Copy of all reports to be 
dispatched to the Rotating Equipment Engineering Department 
for coordination point of view.

- 
Variable Inlet Guide Vane (VIGV's) Inspection: 
 
If it’s determined that VIGV assemblies are fitted with the original 
Garlock type bushing and/or 403SS vanes, which are susceptible to 
cracking, the following actions will prevail: 
 
 
 
 
Full inspection will continue every 5,000 running hours and will 
consist of checks for bushing wear, thrust washer corrosion (if 
fitted) and blade NDT inspection as recommended by the 
manufacturer.  
At the 1st Major Overhaul period the Garlock bushing must be 
replaced with the Chemloy type bushing which will increase the 
period of inspection intervals to every 8,000 running hours.  
Utilisation of the 403SS vanes shall continue however, in the event 
of blade abnormality/rejection the procurement of the new C450 
blade set will be considered after the appropriate evaluation. 
 
 
 
The new C450 blade has a superior corrosion resistance and is 
expected to achieve a greater operational life. The vanes however, 
are still subjected to the same operating conditions, which cause 
them to flutter with subsequent wear on the bushings.  Excessive 
bush clearances will result in an exaggerated fluttering of the vane; 
this in turn could lead to component fracture and consequential 
catastrophic damage. It is prudent therefore to continue a full 
inspection of the VIGV assembly, which will consist of checks for 
bushing wear, thrust washer corrosion, blade corrosion pitting and 
erosion, etc. on a regular basis to avoid potential failure. 
 
In the case that new C450 vanes and Chemloy bushings have been 
installed, the inspection criteria shall be at every 14,000 to 16,000 
run hours or at each scheduled outage CCI, HGPI, Major etc. 
However, based on the actual future findings and the deterioration 
rate of bushing wear for each particular machine, the inspection 
intervals can be reduced accordingly. 
 
 
 
- 
Combustion Chamber Inspection (CCI): 
 
This CCI inspection shall be performed at every 10,000 to 12,000 
run hours. (As per sequence of the maintenance schedule CCI - 
HGPI - CCI - MAJOR) 
 
 
 
 
The inspection shall include the following items: 
 
 
 
 
- 
Fuel nozzles

6.1.3 AVON/ LM Driven Power Turbines 
 
 
 
Starting from 1989 up to May 1997 the first major overhaul was 
completed for all mechanical drive and power generation power turbines: 
 
. 
GEC power turbines between 84,000 to 99,190 running hours 
 
. 
Dresser power turbines between 70,000 to 86,000 running hours 
 
. 
Cooper power turbines between 68,700 to 86,200 running hours 
 
 
In general the power turbine parts were found in good condition without 
any abnormalities. 
 
 
The only exception was a premature overhaul due to the catastrophic 
damage on BAB 14K-101 GEC power turbine, which failed in 1994 and 
replaced. 
 
 
- 
Maintenance 
 
Due to the extensive intervals between hours achieved throughout 
GASCO when compared to the manufacturer recommendation, the 
vendor expertise /assistance is highly recommended to assess the 
internal mechanical condition and performance of the unit and 
define major overhaul target running hours. 
 
 
 
- 
Visual Inspection 
 
 
 
 
This inspection should be repeated on a yearly basis and planned 
with the Avon gas generator boroscope inspection. 
 
 
 
 
- 
For turbine nozzle the inspection is done via removing the 
transition piece connecting the gas generator and the power 
turbine. 
 
 
 
 
- 
For the turbine blades, exhaust diffuser, volute and other 
exhaust stationary parts including Z-struts, the inspection can 
be done via the exhaust duct.  In Cooper Bessemer 
configuration and due to the two stage arrangement of the 
power turbine, this inspection is supported by a boroscopic

inspection. (for Cooper power turbine 1st stage tip clearance 
check should  be considered). 
 
 
 
 
Note: 
 
 
 
 
If Avon gas generator inspection reveals damage of the turbine 
section (loss of material), then an evaluation for the extent of the 
power turbine inspection should be made. 
 
 
 
- 
First Bearings Inspection (for new installations): 
 
 
 
 
The inspection for the power turbine, disc end and load end bearings 
should be planned at intervals between 30,000 to 40,000 total 
running hours and should be planned, if possible, with the 
concerned package module inspection. 
 
 
 
 
Opportunity should also be taken to perform thorough inspection to 
the exhaust diffuser, volute and other exhaust stationary parts.  This 
inspection is important to check the welding of parts and to repair 
any cracks at early stages. In addition, for Cooper power turbine 
check tip clearance of the first stage turbine blades as far as the 
access allows. 
 
 
 
- 
GEC Power Turbine Intermediate Inspection: 
 
 
 
 
This is a specific inspection for the GEC Power Turbine units 
which are fitted with insurance parts of total running hours less 
than 100,000. 
 
 
 
 
GEC (Siemens)  agreed that if intermediate inspection between 
65,000 to 75,000 running hours was carried out then a ceiling of 
100,000 running hours is achievable before the rotor would be 
replaced provided that no abnormalities are detected at the 
inspection.  The intermediate inspection is a full power turbine 
inspection to be performed on site.  It would include bearings and 
seals inspections, removal of the nozzle and rotor assembly to have 
full access for proper inspection.  It is recommended to perform dye 
penetrant check for the blades and their fir trees (blades removed if 
possible). It is also recommended to replace two turbine blades and 
perform destructive test and metallurgical analysis on the removed 
blades to assess their remaining life.

- 
First Major Overhaul: 
 
 
 
 
This inspection is applied to the power turbine units which are fitted 
with insurance parts with total running hours less than 100,000. 
 
 
 
 
Based on the O.E.M. recommendation the first major overhaul of 
the power turbines must be carried out between 70,000 to 80,000 
running hours. 
 
 
 
 
There is a possibility to extend this limit on a GEC power turbine to 
100,000 running hours if the intermediate inspection (detailed 
above) is carried out between 65,000 to 75,000 running hours and 
revealed no abnormalities.   
 
 
 
 
The scope of work for the Major Overhaul consists of the following: 
 
 
 
 
- 
Bearing inspection. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Removal of the rotor and nozzle and replacement with new or 
refurbished parts. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Refurbishment of the removed parts at vendor facilities and 
perform destructive test and metallurgical analysis on two 
blades to assess their remaining life. 
 
 
 
 
Note: 
 
 
The removed GEC Power Turbine parts which will be sent for 
refurbishment from a BUHASA Power Generation package shall be 
reinstalled in the same unit. 
 
 
 
- 
Intermediate Inspection: 
 
 
 
 
This inspection is applied on all power turbine types. It should be 
carried out at approximately 25,000 to 30,000 running hours after 
the first major overhaul for the units which are fitted with insurance 
parts that have been refurbished at a vendor facility after being 
removed from another unit during a major overhaul. 
 
 
 
 
The scope of work consists of the following: 
 
 
 
 
. 
Bearing inspection.

. 
Complete visual inspection to examine the 1st stage disc for

. 
Visual inspection for nozzle vanes to ensure they are in good 
condition and no cracks have developed. 
 
 
 
 
.

they are in good condition and that no cracks have developed.

.

- 
Second Major Overhaul: 
 
 
 
 
 
This overhaul should be planned at approximately 50,000 to 60,000 
running hours after the first major overhaul for the units which are 
fitted with used insurance parts that have been refurbished at a 
vendor facility after being removed from another unit during a 
major overhaul. 
 
 
 
The scope of work consists of the following: 
 
 
 
 
. 
Bearing inspections. 
 
 
 
 
. 
Removal of the rotor and nozzle and replacement with new or 
refurbished parts. 
 
 
 
 
. 
Refurbishment of the removed parts at vendor facilities and 
perform destructive test and metallurgical analysis on the rotor 
and nozzle blades to assess their remaining life.  A third party 
assessment (research establishment) shall be obtained for a life 
assessment of the blades. 
 
 
 
- 
Subsequent Inspections 
 
 
 
The above intermediate inspections and second major overhauls 
should be repeated, guided by the findings of the metallurgical 
destructive tests, until such time as the discs, rotor blades and nozzle 
vanes are replaced/scrapped. 
 
 
After replacing by new parts, inspection intervals for the new parts 
should be resumed.

6.1.4 Centrifugal Gas Compressors 
 
 
The first major overhaul campaign for the gas compressors had started in 
1986 and is still in progress. The completed overhauls were made 
between 41,000 and 118,000 and showed heavy scoring and fretting on 
bearing, seal and coupling taper areas. All other parts were in a general 
satisfactory condition depending on the application: 
 
 
 
- 
Process Gas Compressors: Minor cracks to the impellers. 
 
 
 
- 
Boot Gas Compressors/Blowers: Light to severe cracks to the 
impellers and sign of wear on gear teeth meshing. 
 
 
 
- 
Air Compressors: Extensive wear and pitting corrosion to the 
impellers and the rotating elements.  
  
 
 
- 
BAB Acid Gas Plant: GASCO has limited experience with 
equipment running in this application. The first major inspection for 
the electric motor driven compressors mounted in this plant has 
been made after the first 3 years of operation based on vendor 
recommendation. Parts were in good condition. Therefore, 
maintenance program will follow the normal policy for process 
compressors. 
 
 
 
- 
Maintenance: 
 
 
 
 
Due to the extent of running hours achieved throughout GASCO 
when compared to manufacturer recommendation, the vendor or 
third party expertise is highly recommended to assess the 
mechanical condition, together with the performance parameters, to 
support our on-condition maintenance philosophy. 
 
 
 
- 
Bearings Inspection: 
 
 
 
 
Bearings inspection and/or replacement shall be based on condition 
providing that condition monitoring does not reveal any 
deterioration in the condition that would initiate a premature 
intervention.. However, an opportunity might be planned at intervals 
between 30,000 to 40,000 total running hours in line with the 
concerned package other module inspection. . During major 
overhaul, bearing shall be replaced

- 
Seal Replacement: 
 
 
 
 
Seal changes are basically done on-condition only. Oil consumption 
will be periodically recorded. Trend analysis will be used to plan 
next replacement. During compressor major overhaul, seal 
replacement shall be mandatory. 
 
 
 
-    
Major Overhaul 
 
Exception: 
 
The following HABSHAN centrifugal compressors are 
exception to this policy and will be overhauled every 6 years 
or 48,000 running hours: 
-    Vapour recovery compressors 24C-101 & 27C-101 
-   ETB compressors 12C-101, 13C-101A, 13C-101B &  
13C-102 
 
Note:  
 
However this exceptional requirement will be reviewed to 
bring it inline with the policy. All other HABSHAN Gas 
compressors will be treated as process compressors.  
Air compressors & Air blowers will follow the policy for Air 
compressors.   
 
 
 
 
First Major Overhaul: 
 
 
 
 
It is recommended to plan the first major overhaul between 
70,000 to 80,000 total running hours since new but not 
exceeding 9 to 10 years. This overhaul should be planned to 
match the major overhaul of the driver.   
 
 
 
 
Since GASCO is provided with insurance rotor and spare parts 
and in view of the operating requirements to minimise 
shutdown downtime, it is recommended to replace the rotor 
during the major overhaul. 
 
 
 
 
In addition, the overhaul activities shall include: 
 
 
 
 
- 
Cleaning and flushing of seal, degassing and lube oil tanks.  
 
 
 
- 
Replacement of the bearing, seal and lubrication oil. 
 
 
 
 
Removed rotor shall be sent for inspection to a GASCO 
approved repair facility.

Prior to installing any of the insurance spare rotors, if the end 
user has any doubt of the rotor storage condition, preservative 
measures and/or integrity of rotor balance, it is recommended 
to carry out run out and balance checks before installation. 
 
 
 
Subsequent Major Overhaul: 
 
 
 
 
The subsequent major overhaul should be planned depending 
on the application: 
 
 
 
- 
Process 
Gas 
Compressors 
(including 
boot 
gas 
compressors): To match with the package inspection but 
not exceeding 70,000 to 80,000 running hours or 9 to 10 
years since last overhaul. 
 
 
 
- 
Air Compressors / Air Blowers: Between 40,000 to 48,000 
running hours or 6years since last overhaul, which ever 
comes first. 
 
 
 
 It is preferable to replace the air compressor / blower by 
the spare unit assembly and to send the removed one for 
complete overhaul. 
 
 
 
The scope of work will include the activities of the first major 
overhaul as earlier described in addition to:  
 
 
 
Diaphragms removal to allow for a complete visual and NDT 
inspection on the casing and diaphragms for any sign of 
cracks, voids, corrosion or erosion. 
 
 
 
This overhaul should be repeated until such time as rotor, 
diaphragms and casing are replaced.

6.1.5 Turbo Expander 
 
 
 
Operation past experience has shown that the type of turbo expander, 
used in GASCO, is very durable and reliable. However, shearing of 
nozzle vane pins is a possible cause to initiate failures. The following 
maintenance programme is recommended: 
 
 
Maintenance: 
 
 
Due to the extent of running hours between the maintenance intervals, the 
vendor assistance is highly recommended during the following 
inspection:  
 
 
- 
Intermediate Inspections: 
 
 
 
It is recommended to perform detailed inspection to the Rotoflow 
turbo expander at intervals not exceeding 5 to 6 years of running. 
The scope of work includes removal of the MCS, the nozzle vanes 
assembly casing from position, complete disassembly, inspection 
and replacement of the nozzle pins if sign of wear is evident. 
 
 
- 
Major Overhaul: 
 
 
 
It is recommended that complete overhaul be made after 90,000 
running hours or 10 years of operations whichever occurs first, 
providing that condition monitoring does not reveal any 
deterioration in the condition which would initiate a premature 
intervention. 
 
 
 
The scope of works includes the activities of the intermediate 
inspections detailed above in addition to the replacement of the 
MCS and Nozzle Vanes assemblies for complete overhaul. 
 
 
6.1.6 High Voltage Electrical Motors 
 
 
All electrical checks related to maintenance of high voltage 
motors are included and described in Electrical section of 
this document. 
 
 
 
- 
Bearings Inspection: 
 
 
 
 
- 
Electrical Motors Running On Oil Lubricated Journal

6.1.10 GENERAL PURPOSES STEAM TURBINES: 
 
 
6.1.10.a.   Steam Turbine in Power Generation Units 
 
The following terminology is observed: 
 
Min O/H 
= 
Minor Overhaul 
Int. O/H 
= 
Intermediate Overhaul 
MOH 
= 
100,000 hrs Major Overhaul (Rotor out)

= 
Baroscopic Inspection 
 
The second major overhaul cycle is completed (120,000 berth running 
hours) on December 2001 for the Power Generation unit Steam Turbine 
in HABSHAN plant. The significant problems noticed in both the 
overhauls are corrosion and erosion of LP stage buckets. The major 
contributor for this problem was shortcomings in both on & off the berth 
preservation. Fouling or Salt deposits were not observed so far indicated 
by drop in capacity or increased thrust bearing load. 
 
Fixed time schedules are to be followed as mentioned below until such 
time Modern Supervisory devices are used to reveal operating trends 
towards serious situations with clarity and in time. 
 
Since GASCO machines are of single casing construction, Modular 
overhaul is not applicable.

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY

The recommended overhaul cycle is Minor-Intermediate-Minor - Major 
Overhaul with each having 25,000 hours or 3 years operating span, 
which will be repeated for subsequent overhauls. 
 
Minor Overhaul: 
 
Two Minor overhauls shall be carried out during the overhaul cycle: one 
after first installation or after major overhaul and one in between the 
intermediate and major overhauls. It is recommended to be carried out 
every 25,000 hours or in maximum 3 to 4 years. The turbine casing is not 
opened up for this type of overhaul. The checks and inspection works to 
be carried out are normally restricted to random items of turbine 
peripheral equipment and individual jobs on the turbine itself, like 
bearing inspection, coupling and alignment checks, visual inspection of 
gearing, servicing of the control system, adjustment of the governing and 
protection and safety systems, etc. A Baroscopic inspection is 
recommended to assess the condition of the internals. 
 
The following activities shall be carried out on a Minor Overhaul. It is 
recommended to utilize the manufacturer’s expertise/approved vendor 
for this Overhaul inspection: 
 
 
 Journal bearings and Thrust bearings: It is strongly recommended to 
inspect the journal bearings and thrust bearings for Mechanical damage, 
corrosion, bearing currents, deposits bedding pattern, cracks and 
clearances. Record the findings. 
 
- Main Stop Valve: Inspect the main stop valve for   free movement by 
actuating the solenoid operated test valve. Inspect the stem seal for any 
sticky deposits. 
 
- Over speed/Emergency Trip System: Inspect the Over speed trip 
assembly. Inspect Emergency Trip system by simulation of all the trip 
conditions. 
 
- Control Valve: Clean the linkages. Visually inspect the arms and stems 
for free movement. Apply OEM recommended lubricant to the Main 
control valve actuator mechanisms. 
 
- Coupling: It is recommended to inspect coupling and fasteners for 
tightness. 
 
- Shafting earthing system: Co-ordinate with Electrical section to check 
the earthing system components and record the readings.

- Check the pipe support systems, check, and attend external 
oil/water/steam leaks. Check high temperature area stud bolts for stress 
relaxing.  
 
- Oil system: Check the oil filters, remove the elements, clean and 
examine and replace if required. Inspect the coolers and shut off 
devices. 
 
 
Intermediate Overhaul: 
 
An Intermediate Overhaul shall be carried out during the overhaul cycle 
at 50,000 run hours or 5 to 6 years of running since new or last major 
overhaul. The work is approximately the same as the minor overhaul 
although the percentage of random checks is greater. The turbine casing 
is not opened up similar to minor overhaul.   
 
The following activities shall be carried out on the Intermediate 
Overhaul. 
It 
is 
recommended 
to 
utilize 
the 
manufacturer’s 
expertise/approved vendor for this Overhaul inspection: 
 
- All the activities prescribed under minor overhauls are to be carried out 
during the Intermediate Overhaul.   
 
- Turning Gear assembly: Carry out full strip down inspection on the 
turning gear assembly. 
 
-  Governing systems including Hydraulic oil Package: Inspect all control 
components used in Hydraulic systems. Check and calibrate the 
Governing system following OEM’s instructions. 
 
- Lube oil pumps & filters: Check the main and auxiliary lube oil pumps 
and their couplings. 
 
- Instruments & Measuring devices:  Calibrate the measuring devices in 
both Monitoring and Protection systems. 
 
- Front Standard: It is highly recommended to check the sliding support 
system for free movement and bearing housing mountings. Check the 
self-aligning bearings.  
 
- Alignment:  Check and record the Turbine to Generator alignment. 
Check the concentricity of seal and bearing mountings.

- Control Valve: Inspect the control valves and check / lap the seats if 
required. 
 
- Bearing housing seal: It is recommended to inspect the bearing housing 
sealing rings and replace if necessary. 
 
Major Overhaul (100,000 hours): 
 
Major overhaul shall be carried out at 100,000 running hours or between 
10 to 12 years. Rotor will be removed and sent to the OEM or GASCO 
approved repair facility for complete inspection and refurbishment. 
 
All the inspections and maintenance activities carried out in an 
Intermediate overhaul will be carried out in addition to the following: 
 
- Casing: Inspect casing for cracks, deformation, erosion, corrosion, and 
Mechanical damages. Check for crevice corrosion in the diaphragm 
fitment. Flange and joint bolts shall be inspected for permanent 
distortion, embitterment, surface cracks, and mechanical damage. 
 
- Rotor: Thoroughly inspect the rotor, discs, and buckets for visible 
corrosion, erosion, mechanical- damage, and permanent/residual bow, 
signs of rub, journal damage, and cracks. The rotor shall be sent for 
refurbishment. Spare rotor shall be checked for run out and balance 
condition before installing into the berth. 
 
- Diaphragms/Nozzles: Diaphragms of all the stages shall be removed 
and inspected for surface cracks, erosion, corrosion, deposits, rubbing, 
permanent distortion, and mechanical damage. Diaphragms shall be 
replaced if required. 
 
- Steam chest: Steam chest shall be fully disassembled to check for 
cracks, corrosion, erosion, and distortion. Individual valve discs shall 
be checked for correct fitment with the rack bar. 
 
- Labyrinth Glands: Inspect for surface cracks, erosion, corrosion, 
rubbing, permanent distortion, and mechanical damage. Labyrinths 
shall be replaced based on physical condition & clearance checks. 
 
 
 
Corrosion prevention and Protection during Turbine Outages: 
 
Due to machine shutdown in response to surplus capacity or any other 
reason, steam turbines are constantly being taken out of service for

## Page 134

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 48 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
 
 
 
varying durations. During such outages considerable direct damage can 
be caused by corrosion and subsequent secondary damage due to 
corroded components. Moisture in conjunction with salt deposits 
considerably increases the danger of corrosion fatigue or of a reduction 
in the endurance limit due to pitting. Therefore provision must be made 
for adequate internal corrosion protection even for short outages. 
 
For all methods of corrosion prevention it is imperative that neither 
vapour nor condensate can gain access to the turbine. Complete reliance 
should not be placed on isolation valves; spades/blank flanges are to be 
incorporated to separate the machine from the steam and condensate 
systems. 
Despite the connected pipe work being isolated by blanking, the 
possibility of condensation occurring inside the casing is still there 
because of turbine casing ‘breathing’ (The temperature changes over the 
course of the day causes outside air to enter through the shaft glands). 
Every effort should be made to reduce the relative humidity inside the 
casing below 50% during turbine outages. 
 
Following methods shall be used for protection against corrosion:  
 
1) A better and more economical method of protecting turbine casings 
and internals is the introduction of dehumidifies air (Instrument air) 
Or Filling the casing with Nitrogen. If the installation suffers from 
passing valves, the casing traps shall be maintained and the casing 
SHALL be kept under pressure with the LP steam. The OEM 
recommended operation of turning gears should be supplemented 
FOR this.    
2) To ensure those parts which operate in oil are wetted with a film of oil 
at all times, the oil system should be operated for several hours every 
two to four weeks with the rotor on turning gear. 
 
Oil System (inclusive of Hydraulic oil): 
 
- Oil sampling and analysis should be carried out every 6 months to 
check the Viscosity, specific gravity, flash point, acidity, Total Acid 
Number (TAN) colour, and water content. 
- Oil filter elements shall be changed once every 12 months of operation 
to avoid erosion of filter elements.  
- Oil coolers and pumps and transfer valves shall be inspected, cleaned 
and tested every 5-6 years at the time of Intermediate overhaul. 
- Oil reservoir shall be opened, cleaned and oil system flushed every 5-6 
years at the time of Intermediate and or Major Overhauls.

Washing off Salt Deposits 
 
Past overhaul inspections and operating experience do not suggest any 
washing requirements owing to the purity of the steam used in the plants.  
Casing will not be opened only to clean the salt deposits revealed during 
Boroscopic inspection. However washing off the deposits by wet steam 
to remove the soluble salts following the OEM’s instructions shall be 
carried out only if required and subject to plant conditions. Normally a 
normal shut down and restart or running at no-load for a while will help 
in washing away the salt deposits. If a low pressure external steam is 
used for this purpose, it is recommended to run the turbine at 10 to 15% 
of normal speed.  
 
Performance Test 
 
On line performance tests, Valves Wide Open (VWO) tests or Full Lift 
tests (ASME 1985) are not encouraged due to the fact that any 
fluctuation in the outlet steam pressure would affect the process streams 
seriously and non availability of two stop valves. The purity of steam 
and operational experience do not call for any performance tests.

100,000 hrs Overhaul 
 
The First Overhaul cycle has been initiated for HABSHAN plant process 
drive steam turbines. Warranty overhaul has been completed for AGD-I 
process drive Steam turbine.  
 
It is recommended to plan the first major overhaul (Rotor out) at 100,000 
total running hours since new but not exceeding 12 years. The 
recommended overhaul cycle is Minor-Intermediate-Minor-Major 
Overhaul within 100,000 hours with each having 25,000 hours or 3 years 
operating span. Subsequent Major overhauls shall be at 100,000 running 
hours since last major overhaul. The findings in the first Major overhaul 
shall form the basis for the future inspection intervals. Owing to the 
purity of the steam used in the plants, it is not expected to face serious 
problems of loss of efficiency or corrosion due to salt deposits in the 
steam path. Based on the current Operation and Maintenance data

alignment checks, visual inspection of gearing, servicing of the control

the internals if access provisions exist.

- Journal bearings and Thrust bearings: It is strongly recommended to

control valve actuator mechanisms.

Washing off Salt Deposits 
Past overhaul inspections and operating experience do not suggest any 
washing requirements owing to the purity of the steam used in the plants.  
Casing will not be opened only to clean the salt deposits revealed during 
Boroscopic inspection. However washing off the deposits by wet steam 
to remove the soluble salts following the OEM’s instructions is 
recommended to be carried out only if required and subject to plant 
conditions. Normally a normal shut down and restart or running at no-
load for a while will help in washing away the salt deposits. If a low 
pressure external steam is used for this purpose, it is recommended to 
run the turbine at 10 to 15% of normal speed.  
 
Performance Test 
 
On line performance tests, Valves Wide Open (VWO) tests or Full Lift

for any performance tests. 
 
6.2 
PROCESS AND UTILITY EQUIPMENT WITH STANDBY 
 
 
On-condition maintenance and monitoring of parameters (mainly vibration) shall 
be applied.  Scheduling of maintenance activities will be done in accordance with 
equipment condition and manpower availability. 
 
6.2.1 Diesel Engines 
 
 
 
As the diesel engines are very rarely operated in continuous modes the 
following procedures and intervals are vital to maintain a high standard of 
maintenance to suit this application. 
 
 
 
They shall be maintained under a weekly, monthly, half yearly and annual 
preventive maintenance programme to follow the driven equipment with 
the addition of long term maintenance planning such as described below. 
 
 
 
It is recommended to utilise the manufacturer expertise for the following: 
 
 
 
- 
Every 3 years: 
 
 
 
1. 
Remove, clean and calibrate injectors and fuel pump. 
2. 
Clean radiator. 
 
 
 
3. 
Check injection timing. 
 
 
 
4. 
Inspect injection valve springs. 
 
 
 
5. 
Check the starter mechanism (air, hydraulic, motor). 
 
 
 
6. 
Check fan hub, idler pulley assembly and back side idler. 
 
 
 
7. 
Check cooling water pump (mechanical seals and oil seals).

## Page 140

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 6 - MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 54 of 54 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
:  8 
Effective Date:01.05.2006 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
8. 
Check and disassemble turbocharger (if fitted). 
 
 
 
9. 
Overhauls the starting air compressor, if applicable. 
 
 
 
10. Check and calibrate the governor / control system.   
 
 
 
- 
Every 9 years: 
 
 
 
1. 
Repeat above 3 year inspection. 
 
 
 
2. 
Carry out major inspection which will include the inspection and 
possible replacement of liners, pistons, bearings, gaskets, etc. 
 
 
 
3. 
Check/ clean and pressure test coolers. 
 
 
6.3 
OTHER EQUIPMENT 
 
 
For all other equipment not mentioned in this Chapter, scheduling of maintenance 
activities shall be guided by the manufacturer recommendations and in accordance 
with equipment condition and manpower availability. 
 
6.4 
OIL CONSUMPTION AND LABORATORY ANALYSIS 
 
 
- 
Major Rotating Equipment: 
 
 
 
As part of the condition monitoring programme a periodic check for the 
consumption and laboratory analysis for lube and seal oil shall be made.  The 
other auxiliary oil systems, unless otherwise specified by the O.E.M. will also 
be checked on a periodic basis. 
 
 
 
The oil consumption will be checked on a monthly basis, while the duration 
between two laboratory analysis should never exceed 3 months. 
 
 
- 
Other Equipment: 
 
 
 
The oil consumption and analysis check for all other equipment such as 
pumps, diesel engines, etc. shall be left to each plant to decide the period 
between checks based on how critical is the equipment and the O.E.M. 
recommendation, but this shall never exceed one year duration. 
 
 
In all above cases the periodic checks will be performed in site laboratories and 
compared to the limitations set by the O.E.M.  It will cover the following 
requirements in addition to any other specific requirements set by the O.E.M.:  
viscosity at 40oC and 100oC, specific gravity, flash point, acidity (total acidity 
number -TAN), colour and water content. 
 
 
A third party laboratory shall be requested on annual basis to perform a complete 
analysis as back-up to the on-site periodic checks.

CHAPTER 7.0 
 
 
ELECTRICAL MAINTENANCE 
===========================

## Page 142

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 7 - ELECTRICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : Page 2 of 62 
Issue No: 6 Addendum 
Rev. :  7 
Effective Date:01.12.2010 
 
The maintenance of electrical installations will follow the same basis as laid down in the 
GASCO Maintenance Policy for other equipment. 
 
It is obvious that the whole operation of a plant depends on electrical supplies from every 
point of view, whether it is for production or domestic services. 
 
In order to meet the maximum efficiency of equipment and the statutory obligations of safety 
of personnel and plant, inspection and testing of the electrical equipment on a regular basis 
will be the main part of the electrical maintenance policy. 
 
The existence of standby or spare facilities will allow for maintenance according to the 
standard activities as laid down in the policy. 
 
The target of the electrical maintenance shall be to keep the electrical equipment in good 
working and safe condition and to ensure that protective circuits are healthy and available 
when needed. 
 
To achieve this required level of highly efficient equipment, the electrical maintenance policy 
shall be based on, but not limited to: 
a) 
Testing and inspection of the electrical equipment to ensure correct functioning and 
safe working conditions. 
b) 
Cleaning, repair, adjustment or replacement to restore equipment to satisfactory 
working conditions. 
c) 
Modifications and additions to improve performance or to increase reliability or 
protection level. 
 
In order to facilitate the implementation of an efficient maintenance programme, it is the 
policy of GASCO that the priorities for maintenance of electrical equipment shall be set 
according to the Equipment Classification types as described in Chapter 4.1(Vital-HSE, Vital-
Production Loss, Critical & Non-Critical). 
 
VITAL is where: 
 
- 
Continued operation of the plant is wholly dependent upon this equipment (e.g. 
motors driving gas compressor in a single train plant). 
 
- 
Standby equipment is not available. 
 
- 
Failure of the equipment may lead to unsafe working conditions and life or health 
may be in jeopardy. 
CRITICAL is where: 
 
- 
Continued operation of the plant may be affected after a certain period of time 
(e.g. single equipment which is not on-line all the time due to process 
requirements like heaters, dryers). 
 
- 
Standby equipment is available but its failure will affect the operation and/or the 
production (e.g. instrument air compressors, NGL delivery pumps, power 
generation units). 
NON-CRITICAL is where: 
 
- 
Alternative equipment or supplies are available. 
 
- 
Safe operation may continue though less efficiently. 
 
- 
Working/living conditions may be less than desired but not untenable.

7.1 
MAINTENANCE CATEGORIES 
 
 
Maintenance can be categorized under scheduled, routine, unscheduled, or 
modification maintenance as determined in MAXIMO 
 
 
It is the policy of the company to MINIMISE UNSCHEDULED WORK in connection 
with maintenance of equipment classified as vital and critical by implementing 
maintenance on a planned routine basis.  This shall be achieved by proper trouble-
shooting procedures which should include: 
 
- 
Written procedure or flow charts for trouble-shooting based on previous 
experience. 
 
- 
Availability of the relevant drawings and documents nearby the equipment. 
 
- 
Analysis and recording of the trouble. 
 
- 
Updating the trouble-shooting procedures accordingly. 
 
- 
On condition monitoring of motors, transformers, chargers, etc. 
 
 
 
7.2 
ENGINEERING REGULATIONS FOR PERFORMING ELECTRICAL 
MAINTENANCE 
 
 
In order to ensure the maximum safety for the persons involved in an electrical job and 
to avoid any unwanted shutdowns, the following are the minimum requirements for the 
working procedure. 
 
 
1. 
Only the authorized persons nominated by the Electrical Section Head/Senior 
Elect. Eng. in charge are allowed to work with the electrical system. 
 
 
2. 
If work is required on high and low tension the Electrical Section Head or the 
authorized person nominated by him shall determine the necessary precautions to 
be taken to perform the job safely and shall clearly state them in writing. 
 
 
3. 
Forms of isolation and de-isolation are to be signed by the Electrical Section 
Head or the authorized person nominated by them. 
 
 
Every plant could establish more precautions that it deems necessary to avoid any un-
required shutdowns.

7.3 
MAINTENANCE ACTIVITIES 
 
 
The electrical maintenance shall cover different areas which could be summarized as 
follows: 
 
 
1) 
Generation, distribution and switchgear. 
 
2) 
Motors and motor accessories. 
 
3) 
Transformers. 
 
4) 
Protection systems. 
 
5) 
D.C. systems and inverters. 
 
6) 
Emergency supplies. 
 
7) 
Small Power and Lighting Systems. 
 
8) 
Cathodic protection. 
 
9) 
Electrical Heat tracing system. 
 
10) Grounding network. 
 
11) Telecommunication Equipment (If under Elec. Section). 
 
12) Building services. 
 
13) Trouble-shooting. 
 
14) Test equipment checking. 
 
15) Modifications. 
 
16) Drawings and documentation updating. 
 
17) Area classification. 
 
18) Testing of cables. 
 
19) Camp electrical survey. 
20) Periodic checking of electrical equipment of other sections. 
21) Field Warning Controllers (Siren, Hooters and Flashers). 
22) Field EX- Junction Boxes. 
23) HVAC. 
24) Active Lightning Protection System. 
25) Filed Sockets and Welding Outlets (Refer Appendix 7.18, Appendix 7.19 and 
Appendix 7.21) 
26) Field LCP  (Refer Appendix 7.18, Appendix 7.19 and Appendix 7.21) 
27) Classified Overhead Cranes (Refer Appendix 7.18, Appendix 7.19 and Appendix 
7.21 as well as section 7.3.20) 
 
 
The magnitude of the maintenance task will be determined by the nature of the 
application, vendors’ recommendation and may be continuously reassessed as a result 
of experience with the equipment.  However the frequencies of maintenance given in 
this document should be considered as a guideline only. 
 
 
The above electrical activities, depending upon the overall plant maintenance planning 
or the impact on the plant performance, can be categorized under scheduled, routine, 
unscheduled, or modification types of maintenance. 
 
 
They could be defined as follows:

7.3.1 Generation, Distribution and Switchgear 
 
The GASCO Maintenance Policy is to ensure a CONTINUOUS SUPPLY of 
electricity capable of meeting the demand at all times and to have stand-by 
equipment and emergency services in good working condition to meet 
unscheduled and scheduled shutdown situations. 
Routine maintenance system in addition to the condition monitoring should be 
adopted for generators. 
 
 
  
- 
Generators Inspection: 
 
 
 
- 
Special attention shall be given to the HV generators windings.  In 
order to assess their condition on-line PDA should be performed 
regularly with a recommended interval of 6 months to 1 year. Data 
may be sent to Vendor's specialist for detailed analysis in case of 
abnormal data collected. Whenever the HV generator is 
continuously operated at 100% capacity , on-line PDA should be 
performed regularly every maximum 6 months, its respective data 
should be sent to vendor specialist for detailed analysis and further 
consideration of his recommendations.  
 
 
 
For the emergency generators which are not PDA tested a thorough 
visual inspection, insulation resistance check and polarization index 
measurement should be done on a regular basis with maximum 
interval of three years. 
 
- 
Generator protection system inspection, testing and calibration (if 
needed) to be done on a regular basis with a minimum interval of 12 
months and maximum interval of three years. 
 
- 
Excitation, brushes and battery banks should be maintained and 
checked on a regular basis with maximum intervals of 12 months. 
 
- 
Further specific inspection requirements are detailed as follows: 
 
 GE 11kV Generator Minor & Major Inspection (Ref. to 
Appendix 7.1). 
 GEC 11kV Generator Minor & Major Inspection (Ref. to 
Appendix 7.2). 
 GE 11kV Generator Minor & Major Inspection for HABSHAN 
only (Ref. to Appendix 7.14). 
 GEC ALSTOM 11kV Generator Minor & Major Inspection for 
HABSHAN only (Ref. to Appendix 7.15). 
 
BRUSH 11kV Generator Minor & Major Inspection for 
HABSHAN only (Ref. to Appendix 7.16).

- 
Switchgear Bus bars Inspection: 
 
 
 
- 
Where the IR-Thermography windows are installed, images should 
be analyzed once a year and any problems to be fixed as per the 
report. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Where the IR windows are not installed, bus bars inspection should 
be scheduled to conform with the plant scheduled partial 
shutdowns. The frequency of inspection shall be determined 
according to the previous inspection results, but should not exceed 
48 months. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Switchgear Inspections:(Ref. to Appendix 4,7,8 & 9) 
 
 
 
 
 
High and low voltage switchgear and its related protection system for 
motors and transformers shall be maintained and tested for reliability 
along with corresponding equipment with recommended intervals of 
minimum one year and maximum of two years for 3.3kV and 11kV, and 
one year for LV switchgear.  However in case of microprocessor based 
relays, 
these
