5 
years 
maximum. 
 
 
 
 
- 
Auto Synchronizing System: 
 
 
 
 
 
The auto synchronizing system should be tested and maintained 
regularly along with generator protection relay and also after any major 
wiring modifications (if any) in the generator unit control panel or in the 
generator breaker cubicle. 
- 
Annunciation system: 
 
Annunciation panel shall be maintained (cleaning,  lamp test and 
inspection for over heating traces of PC cards) in the same interval of its 
associated switchgear. 
 
7.3.2 Motors (Ref. to Appendix 3, 5, 6, 18 & 20) 
 
 
 
A routine maintenance system shall be adopted for all motors, however special 
attention shall be given to HV motors. 
 
 
 
Condition monitoring shall be the basis for the HV motors overhauling. 
 
 
 
This condition monitoring shall be performed as follows: 
 
a) For the 11kV motors which are equipped with on-line PD condition 
monitoring system, analysis will be performed every 6 months to 1 year. 
Data may be sent to Vendors specialist for detailed analysis in case of 
abnormality.

b) For the 11kV motors which are not equipped with On-Line PDA system, 
Off-Line PDA to be performed in order to assess the condition of the 
windings, a Partial Discharge analysis and a loss tangent characteristic 
testing shall be done for the  
11kV motors classified as vital and could be optionally done for the critical 
11kV  motors. 
 
 
 
Such tests shall be performed by a vendor specialist with a maximum 
interval of 3-4 years for the vital and critical HV motors. This interval can 
eventually be reduced to 2 years for the vital drives if an abnormal 
degradation has been detected and/or major corrective action has been done 
which justifies an earlier retesting. 
 
During this period regular inspections with a recommended minimum 
interval of 12 months and maximum interval of two years can be performed 
to cover also the winding insulation resistance and polarization index 
measurements. 
 
 
 
c) For the 415V & 3.3kV motors which cannot be are not PDA tested, due to 
PD activity phenomenon applies only at above 3.3 KV, the condition of 
their windings should be assessed as a result of insulation resistance, 
polarization index measurement and through visual inspection to check for 
loose wedges, contamination and weak bracings. 
 
 
 
 
With proper conditioning monitoring as afore- defined, it is possible to 
predict well in advance the requirement for maintenance /overhauling of the 
windings of the electrical machines and therefore plan for the overhauling 
activities. 
 
 
 
Note: 
 
 
 
Motors’ accessories like push button station, emergency stop, etc. will be 
maintained along with the motors. 
 
7.3.3 Distribution Type Transformers 
a) Conservator Type 
 
Visual checks including leaks check, cleaning, tightening of all 
connections, cable boxes inspection, testing and setting the On-Tank 
protective instrument systems, Oil sample analysis, dielectric testing and 
neutral earthing resistance should be done on regular basis with a 
recommended minimum interval of one year and maximum two to three 
years. 
For transformers where IR windows are installed on the junction boxes, hot 
spots thermal image to be performed once a year utilizing the IR 
Thermography camera, any problem to be fixed as per the report results. 
 
Transformer winding insulation value, polarization index by Megger and 
resistance test by Ductor, and oil moisture content should be  
monitored on a regular basis with a recommended minimum interval of one 
year and maximum two to three years.

Predictive maintenance through in-depth transformer oil analysis shall be 
performed for each transformer by an independent specialized Transformer 
Oil DGA laboratory.  Analysis shall be done with a recommended interval 
of four years.  Unscheduled analysis may be required in case of heavy 
surges on the transformer or the network, which could affect the integrity of

b) Hermetically Sealed Type (Ref. to Appendix 10) 
 
 
Visual checks including hot spots thermal image check using thermal

Transformer winding insulation resistance and polarisation index by

For transformers where IR windows are installed on the junction boxes, hot 
spots thermal image to be performed once a year utilizing the IR 
Thermography camera, any problem to be fixed as per the report result. 
 
 
7.3.4 Protection Systems (Ref. Appendices 4, 7, 8 & 9) 
 
 
 
 The recommended frequencies of testing the protection systems are included in 
appendices 4, 7, 8 & 9 to ensure that it is functioning properly. 
 
 
 Vendor specialist assistance is recommended if found necessary. 
 
 
7.3.5 D.C. System and Inverters 
 
 
 
 - 
NiCad Batteries:  (Ref. Appendix 7.11) 
 
Voltage of each battery cell shall be measured and recorded at a maximum 
interval of three months. For ultra low maintenance type, specific gravity to 
be checked if the electrolyte level goes to the low level mark. Comparison 
with previous records is important in identifying any degradation taking 
place in the cells.  Battery cell temperature and battery room temperature 
shall be captured using the IR thermometer and recorded. 
Battery impedance test should be carried out on semi-annual to determine 
the condition of the batteries and connections. 
It should be noted that in most stationary battery application, the electrolyte 
would retain its effectiveness for the lifetime of the battery. However, if the 
electrolyte is found to be carbonated, the battery performance can be 
restored by changing the electrolyte.

CHAPTER 7 - ELECTRICAL MAINTENANCE

Electrolyte levels shall be checked every 3 months and adjusted when 
necessary. 
Cells showing weakness in voltage, and/or having higher than normal 
electrolyte consumption rate or high impedance shall be closely monitored 
and controlled with a maximum interval of one month. 
 
Performance testing should be carried out as follows: 
a) If SAT was not carried out, a performance test of battery capacity should 
be made within the first two years of service. For comparison purposes, 
having the performance test be similar in duration to the battery duty cycle 
is desirable. 
b) Additional performance tests should be made at five-year intervals until the 
battery shows signs of excessive capacity loss. Excessive capacity loss is 
indicated when the battery capacity drops more than an average of 1.5% per 
year of rated capacity from its capacity on the previous performance test. 
c) Annual performance tests of battery capacity should be made on any 
battery that shows signs of excessive capacity loss. 
 
- 
Lead Acid  Batteries:  (Ref. Appendix 7.11) 
 
Voltage of each battery cell shall be measured and recorded at a maximum 
interval of three months. 
Recommended float voltage  2.23 - 2.25 + .05 VPC 
Electrolyte levels shall be checked every 3 months and adjusted when 
necessary. 
 
Battery impedance test should be carried out on quarterly basis in order to 
determine and record the condition of the batteries and connections. 
 
Additional Performance Test for Lead Acid Batteries:  
 
a) Valve Regulated Lead Acid Batteries (VRLA): 
 
Batteries should undergo a performance tests periodically. When 
establishing the interval between tests, factors such as design life and 
operating temperature should be considered. It is recommended that the 
performance test interval should not be greater than 25% of the expected 
service life or two years, whichever is less (For sites where a very large 
number of Battery sets are existing and the full discharge test could not be 
practically applied in two years interval the following will be applied: 
 
1 Battery impedance on line test will be applied on quarterly basis, to 
verify battery condition. 
2 In case of satisfactory battery condition the test interval between 
discharge tests could be extended to the maximum allowed by the 
original battery manufacturer. 
3 In case of unsatisfactory battery condition, partial and full discharge 
tests should be alternatively applied at 2 years interval.

The expected service life may be significantly less than the warranty period. 
The recommended interval assumes that an on-site acceptance test was 
performed with acceptable results. 
Battery discharge test acceptable results are defined as the capacity of each 
cell exceeding 90%, and the capacity of all cells are within 10% of the 
average cell performance. For batteries that were not acceptance tested on 
site or had unacceptable results, the first performance test should be given 
within one year of installation. 
Annual performance tests of battery capacity should be made on any battery 
that shows signs of degradation or has reached 85% of the service life 
expected for the application. Degradation is indicated when the battery 
capacity drops more than 10% from its capacity on the previous 
performance test or is below 90% of the manufacturer’s rating. 
 
b) Vented Lead Acid Batteries (VLA): 
 
b.1) IF SAT was not carried out, a performance test of the battery capacity 
should be made within the first two years of service. It is desirable for 
comparison purposes that the performance tests be similar in duration to the 
battery duty cycle. 
 
b.2) Batteries should undergo additional performance tests periodically. 
When establishing the interval between tests, factors such as design life and 
operating temperature should be considered. 
It is recommended that the performance test interval should not be greater 
than 25% of the expected service life. 
 
b.3) Annual performance tests of battery capacity should be made on any 
battery that shows signs of degradation or has reached 85% of the service 
life expected for the application. Degradation is indicated when the battery 
capacity drops more than 10% from its capacity on the previous 
performance test, or is below 90% of the manufacturers rating. If the battery 
has reached 85% of service life, delivers a capacity of 100% or greater of 
the manufacturer’s rated capacity, and has shown no signs of degradation, 
performance testing at two-year intervals is acceptable until the battery 
shows signs of degradation.  
- 
Chargers and Inverters (Ref. Appendices 7.12 & 7.13): 
 
 
 
 
Complete check up and adjustment shall be conducted  6-12 Months on the 
chargers and inverters. 
 
 
 
All the electrolyte capacitors (AC & CD) to be replaced at the end of their 
lifetime based on the Manufacturer data, if Manufacturer is not specifying 
they have to be replaced on a regular interval of 5-7 years. 
 
 
 
Assistance from a vendor representative could be considered for the 
chargers.  It is recommended for the inverters.

7.3.6 Emergency Supplies  
 
 
 Generation and distribution of the emergency supplies are the main and only 
power sources available in case of black-out. They must be tested and inspected 
regularly to ensure their correct operation. Loading of the emergency 
generators is essential to maintain and guarantee their capability to cope with 
the emergency situations. It is recommended to load these generators by 
synchronizing on a regular basis during the operation tests if it is possible, with 
interval of one month.  Otherwise generator load tests to be done with external 
load banks at the interval of 1-2 years. 
 
7.3.7 Small Power and Lighting Systems 
 
 
 
 Regular maintenance to be carried out every 6-12 months (as per Site 
experience), this includes cleaning with vacuum cleaner , visual inspection for 
traces of overheating/improper condition and function check for RCD’s  
and MCCB trip (where applicable), Ammeters, Voltmeters and selector 
switches.  The illumination level measurements by LUX meter shall be 
performed to ensure at all times the proper illumination level as per the area 
and the operational requirements at the interval of 4-5 years.  Any lighting 
failure which could be a source of hazard in a particular area shall be 
immediately corrected.  Note that the interval 4-5 years is adequate as the 
polycarbonate diffusers get yellowish over time of 4-5 years and they require 
replacements. 
 
7.3.8 Cathodic Protection  
 
 
 
 As the cathodic protection is vital to the life of equipment buried, or installed 
underwater, periodic surveys shall be carried out to determine the status of 
cathodic protection and the related equipment at each plant, on the NGL , Gas, 
Condensate, Crude, Water & Multi product pipelines and on the jetty structure 
(RUWAIS only). 
 
For the NGL , Gas, Condensate, Crude, Water & Multi product pipelines 
cathodic protection system survey reading intervals refer to Appendix 4.5 (Item 
2.4-Pipelines Division List of  Inspection Activities & Frequencies). 
The frequency of maintenance for cathodic protection equipment such as 
Rectifiers, Transformers, Test Points shall be yearly 
 
7.3.9 Electrical Heat Tracing System 
 
 
 
 Electrical heat tracing system is essential in maintaining process pipe 
temperatures and protecting against ground freezing (NGL tanks). 
Each heating element shall be controlled and thermostats (if any) recalibrated 
once a year by a joint team of instrument/electrical staff. 
The current reading of individual horizontal heater and group of vertical heaters 
shall be taken every 3 months.  
The heater with very high deviation in current from the reference value is to be 
pulled out for repair/replacement.

The maintenance of the associated switchgear and control panel should be 
performed once a year preferably during hotter periods on the process lines to 
avoid the possible formation of condensates in the gas during the time the 
system is switched off. The activities shall be as per the job plan in Maximo. 
It can be performed at any time on the ground protection freezing system but 
power supply shut down shall be reduced to the minimum. 
 
7.3.10 Grounding Network 
 
 
 
 Earthing systems is dependent on the state of the mud well earth electrodes.  
The depth of penetration into the water table, the physical nature of the water 
table and the bonding of the electrode to the main plant earthing system 
determine the relative effectiveness of each mud well.  As the water table is 
highly saline, the active portion of the mud well electrodes is susceptible to 
corrosion. Seasonal variations, or a gradual change of water table level, may 
lead to efficiency drop of the electrodes.  Although loss of effectiveness of a 
single electrode, or even of two electrodes, will not result in potentially 
dangerous conditions, changes should be identified and  
rectified.  In addition effective plant equipotential bonding and earth return 
paths are reliant on the maintenance of metallic earthing conductors between 
items of plant. The “two-point method” is a simple and effective method of 
testing mud well electrode resistance and should be adopted on a routine basis.  
This method involves taking measurements between the isolated electrode and 
the main connecting cable(s) using an earth test megger.  The measurements 
taken to be tabulated and to form the benchmark for future routine maintenance 
results. Frequency of 30 months would be most appropriate. 
 
7.3.11 Telecommunications Equipment (If under Elec. Section) 
 
 
 
 To carry out maintenance tasks every 6 months (visual inspection, cleaning and 
functionality checking)  for CCTV, Radio Repeater, WP/FLP/PABX field 
telephones and Public Address Systems to ensure a proper operation and good 
communication. 
 
7.3.12 Buildings Services 
 
 
 
 The maintenance of electrical equipment for Buildings, offices and support 
facilities as well as Gate Barriers, Sliding Doors and Exhaust fans shall be 
carried out in the best interests of safety, economy and acceptable living, 
working and social conditions. 
 
7.3.13 Trouble-Shooting 
 
 
 
 The diagnosis of faults, malfunctions or failure of equipment affecting the 
normal operation of plant will be carried out to determine the required 
maintenance level, or any modification required to achieve better operation and 
performance.  This could be achieved by proper analysis for the causes of the 
trouble and locating the troublesome components which need frequent 
maintenance.

7.3.14 Test Equipment Checking 
 
 
 
 Where standard equipment is used for inspection, calibration or adjustment, it 
should be checked periodically and stamped with the date of test.  Calibration 
may be carried out at site or alternatively, by an external recognized calibration 
authority. 
 
7.3.15 Modifications 
 
 
 
 Continuous assessment of the performance of equipment and systems will 
indicate where replacement or modifications are necessary to reduce 
maintenance, increase efficiency or reliability (refer to Plant Change 
Procedures).  
 
 
7.3.16 Drawings and Documentation 
 
 
 
 Drawings and documentation shall be kept up to date and amended when any 
changes or additions are carried out. All new installations and modifications’ 
drawings and documentation shall be kept in electronic CAD Format and to be 
copied to HQ Central Archive. 
7.3.17 Area Classification 
 
 
 
 A review and updating to the area classification maps, based on present 
operating conditions should be done by specialized Consultant team consisting 
of Process and Safety engineers then the field electrical equipment are to be 
checked by the Electrical Section Head to ensure that certification for present 
field installed equipment are still valid. Any new installation should match with 
the updated area classification map. 
 
7.3.18 Testing of Cables 
 
 
 
 Testing of cables should be carried out occasionally whenever possible and 
according to each plant condition.  However, motor cables shall be tested 
during the motor checks. 
 
 
 
 This activity should receive more attention due to ageing of the cables, it 
should be planned during the scheduled shutdowns.  The recommended 
frequency is once every two years. 
 
 
 
 If, for any reason, jointing work is performed on any of the high voltage cables 
or re-termination effected, they should be tested with appropriate test voltage 
after the completion of work. 
 
The test voltage and duration shall follow the DEP recommendation in this 
regard.

7.3.19 Residential Complexes and Contractors Camps Electrical Survey (ASAB, 
BAB & BUHASA) 
 
 
 
 Annual check for the camp electrical installation should be done to ensure that 
the installations are safe and comply with the GASCO standards. 
 
7.3.20 Periodic check of electrical equipment related to other sections 
 
 
Electrical equipment related to other sections such as overhead cranes, 
lathe, cutting, drilling & punching, welding machines, portable light, 
extension cords …etc., are to be maintained by Electrical section in 
accordance with OEM recommendations as applicable.  The general checks 
are required for cranes including electrical, mechanical & ropes, by 3rd 
party inspector for certification purposes.  Equipments accompanied by 
contractors for S/D, new construction activities; services…etc are to be 
inspected by Electrical and Safety Sections on mobilization to site. 
 
 
Inventory of equipments covered by this clause are: 
 Equipments under Plants’ custody: 
Welding machines, Drilling machines, Union grinders…etc. 
 Equipments accompanied by contractors for S/D and new 
construction activities: 
Pipe Threading machines, Portable lights, Extension cords…etc. 
 
7.3.21 Field Warning Controller (Sirens, Hooters and Flashers) 
The Field-warning controller (FWC) shall be grouped along with Battery 
charger and UPS. The frequency of battery maintenance and visual inspection 
of the FWC batteries shall be carried out every six months. 
The maintenance of Battery Charger / inverter units of the FWC (Sirens, 
Hooters and Flashers) shall be carried out every two years. This frequency of 
two years for Battery Charger / inverter is same as that for other Battery 
charger / UPS. 
 
7.3.22 Field Ex-Junction Boxes 
 Ex-Junction Boxes to be checked every 2-3 years (depends on site 
condition) as follows.  Visual check for corrosion and cracks (replace when 
necessary). 
 Hand check for proper gland tightness and earthing lug if provided (tight 
when necessary). 
 Internal visual check (after removal of cover) for possible accumulation of 
dust and condensation or improper condition (clean/repair when necessary). 
 Unscrew Cover’s bolts and/or nuts and clean them from dirt/rust/old grease 
(replace if necessary). Put thin film of silicon grease on threads, screw 
again and put silicon grease on bolts heads and nuts to protect them from 
the environment conditions for easier removal of box cover afterwards.  
 For Junction box with threaded cover, use oil instead of grease. 
 In addition to the above, all inspection schedules in appendices 7.18 and 
7.19 and 7.21 shall be conducted as applicable.

7.3.23 HVAC Systems 
 
The HVAC System is maintained by Specialists through maintenance contract 
under supervision of concerned section. However, the contractor should carry 
out the preventive maintenance of HVAC panels, Bus bars, and relay 
calibration under close coordination and supervisory of the electrical 
department and method statement, as per signed HVAC contract. 
 
7.3.24 Active Lightning Protection System 
 
Maintenance of Lightning protections systems is necessary for different 
reasons:  
1-With the installation ageing, it is important to verify that all devices and 
connections are in good working state.  
2-When a lightning strike is intercepted by a lightning protection system, it is 
possible that some devices become damaged.  
3-Sometimes, some works around the installations (mainly around the ground 
system) can induce damage to the lightning protection systems  
4-New installations that don’t take in account the existing lightning protection 
can decrease the safety of the protection.  
For all these reasons, two types of maintenance operations have to be 
completed:  
 
a) 
 Periodically operations (generally once by year) to cover the first case, 
these operations have to take in account all the installations and systems of 
all plants. The choice of the operation date will be preferably before the 
storm season. 
 
b) Punctual operation to cover the other cases, this operation will be 
completed after each event, but only the concerning installation have to be 
checked.  
 
Each result of checking to be recorded on a form and compared with the 
previous measurement of the same installation (Ref. to Appendix 7.23  
“Maintenance Sheet Form for lightning Protection system”).  
 
All maintenance for Lightning Protection System including the necessary 
test equipment shall be in accordance with the maintenance procedure 
(Ref. to Appendix 7.22)

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 7 - ELECTRICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : Page 16 of 62 
Issue No: 6 Addendum 
Rev. :  7 
Effective Date:01.12.2010 
 
7.4 
REPAIR POLICY 
 
In the event of a breakdown the time taken to repair is normally a vital factor.  Where 
spare or standby items have been installed, repairs can be carried out at leisure but this 
is not normally the case with single installed items. 
 
 
It is therefore the policy wherever possible to REPAIR BY REPLACEMENT - to cut 
down the time taken to diagnose faults within a system by replacing a unit/printed 
circuit board/module or even a complete unit. 
 
 
Wherever possible it is advisable to find the fault and repair such modules in the 
correct environment of the workshop rather than in the field. 
 
 
Repair policy will vary according to one or two train plant configuration, installation of 
spare units, or applications on common facilities and essential services. 
 
7.5       RECORDS 
 
Fault analysis can play an important part in reducing maintenance either by pointing 
the way to unsatisfactory equipment, faulty installation, or even lack of skill or 
perception. 
 
Maintenance activities records, combined with major equipment and area log books 
which record events and Work Order numbers are the minimum requirements for 
useful records which all should be included in MAXIMO in order to facilitate 
retrieving of data. 
 
Defect reports for all trips or critical problems should be prepared by the electrical 
engineer and should be considered in filling the reason for trips in History File in 
MAXIMO. 
 
However, major defect report should be sent to Engineering & Technical Support 
Division who will act to exchange the experience between sites (refer to 4.6). 
 
7.6 
RESOURCES 
 
The electrical equipment is a vital parameter in the plant operation.  At the same time 
the maintenance policy is based upon the MAXIMUM USE OF AVAILABLE 
RESOURCES. 
 
 
1. 
TECHNICIANS WITH TRAINING AND EXPERIENCE COVERING THE 
COMPLETE RANGE OF ELECTRICAL EQUIPMENT, with a sound 
knowledge of the plant process and the area classification principles. 
 
 
 
HIGHLY SKILLED PEOPLE, like engineers, supervisors and technicians will, 
as a matter of policy, be part of the GASCO ESTABLISHMENT.  Where 
LOWER SKILLS are required, i.e. on the craftsmen level, CONTRACTING of 
required manpower may be considered if economically attractive. 
 
2. 
Adequate spare UNITS OR MODULES so that the need for components is 
reduced and the choice can be made to REPAIR OR RETURN to manufacturer. 
 
3. 
Mobility to reduce the time spent moving around where plants cover large areas. 
 
4. 
Careful attention to the updating of all relevant drawings and information. 
 
5. 
Good communication. 
 
6. 
Adequate and reliable test equipment.

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY

Effective Date:01.12.2010 
 
7.7 
INTERFACE BETWEEN ELECTRICAL, INSTRUMENT AND MECHANICAL 
SECTIONS 
 
 
In order to achieve a well defined interface and to ensure that maintenance is covering 
the overall system, the following guidelines are defining the division of responsibilities 
between each section.  The following are only guidelines for some typical examples. 
 
 
1. 
Instrument Section shall be responsible for the power generation turbines control 
systems, while Electrical Section shall be responsible for the generation system 
(e.g. excitation, protection system). 
 
2. 
Mechanical Section shall be responsible for the monitoring of all electric motor 
bearings.  Electrical Section shall be responsible for all electrical motor 
maintenance, however when overhaul and/or repairs are required, assistance of 
Mechanical Section shall be sought. 
 
 
Each plant shall define all the areas of interface and the responsibilities between the 
sections according to plant conditions.  The definitions of all the plants will be the base 
for a common GASCO definition. 
 
 
Note: 
 
Reference should be made to the interface between Instrument and Electrical Sections. 
 
 
 
7.8 
PROCEDURES 
 
 
The procedures to carry out the maintenance (e.g. the extent of a certain check or 
inspection) shall be prepared in coordination with the plants during regular Electrical 
Engineering meetings. 
 
After finalizing all the procedures, a GASCO Electrical Maintenance Procedure shall 
be issued.

7.9 
REPAIR OF EX-MOTORS  In compliance with standard (IEC60079-19), 
Ex- motors certification will be still valid and there is no need to re-certify motors as 
far the scope of repair is done in line with below table: 
 (Y: to do ; N: Don't do)  
 
Mechanical Repairs (Reclamation) 
Refer to Appendix 7.24 “Ex Motor Data to be Repaired” 
1. Any mechanical repair (reclamation) shall be carried out by original 
manufacturer or his approved repair facility. 
2. In circumstances where: 
a. Original manufacturer repair facility is not available locally, or 
b. Increased down time due to logistics, custom clearance to ship the 
motor to repair facility outside UAE., or 
c. The motor is obsolete or OEM support no longer exists “Authorized Ex 
Motor Repair Facility” can be appointed provided that they can provide 
a declaration that the repair has been done in accordance with IEC 
60079-19. In such case marking for repair shall be (R inside inverted 
triangle). 
If marking R inside square (according to original manufacturer/ certification) is 
required, original manufacturer specialist shall be mobilized to supervise the repair/ 
testing process. All cost shall be borne by Gasco. 
 
ON HOLD 
Per MMM 4/2010

Electrical Repairs (Re-winding) 
Refer to Appendix 7.24 “Ex Motor Data to be Repaired” 
Re-winding represents the majority of repairs of the motors. As such, it is proposed to 
use “Authorized Ex Motor Repair Facility” directly for re-winding of any Ex- motor 
for the following reasons: 
1. Re-winding can use the accepted “copy winding” technique by using the same 
material and insulation class or better. 
2. Increased down time due to logistics, custom clearance to ship the motor to the 
manufacturer or an authorized repair facility outside UAE. 
Retroactive Works of Already Mechanically Repaired Ex-Motors in service at site  
 Prepare an inventory of already reclaimed motors. 
 For ExN 415V & 3.3kV motors located in Zone-2 Area – do nothing.  However 
for any future repairs, ensure that it is in line with above and IEC60079-19.   
 For any Exd or Exp motor re-certification is required or replace the motor.  
 11kV Ex(N) motors in zone-2 shall be replaced with Ex(d) or Ex(p) in 
accordance with DGS-1630-003 
 If re-certification costs exceed 50% of the replacement cost, then replace the 
motor.  
ON HOLD 
Per MMM 4/2010

Appendix 7.1: GE Generators Detailed Inspection Jobs 
 
- 
6 to 12 Monthly 
 
Stator Winding Partial Discharge Analysis. 
N.B. In case of HV generator put in service for continuous operation at 100% full 
capacity, it is recommended to perform on-line PDA every maximum interval 6 
months. 
 
- 
Minor Inspection Every 20,000 - 22,500 Running Hours 
 
Remove generator end covers. 
 
Inspect condition of end windings, blocking, lashing, etc. 
 
Clean winding using dry air/vacuum. 
 
If winding is oily/greasy, wipe with electrical cleaning fluid, do not spray onto 
winding. 
 
Carry out electrical tests to stator & field windings. 
 
- 
Major Inspection Every 80,000 - 90,000 Running Hours (along with alternative 
power turbine overhauls) 
 Dismantle generator and remove field. 
 Stator Windings Cleaning 
 Check tightness of stator wedges 
 Check all stator end winding for any looseness of blocking, lashing etc; 
 Spray winding with approved anti-tracking varnish 
 Carry out electrical test that includes; 
o Insulation Resistance test  (IR)  
o Polarization Index test (PI) 
o Rotor, Stator and RTD's Impedance test 
o R.S.O (Repeated Surge Oscilloscope) test  
o Tan Delta test 
o TVA (Tennesy value assessment) or Peak pulse probe test  
 Set up and remove field retaining rings 
 Inspect rotor windings for any distortion, loose blocking etc 
 Carry out NDT (non destructive testing) inspection of retaining rings. 
 Refit retaining rings using insulation (Scanning for cracks)  
 Re-alignment measurements and checks  
 High voltage tests 
 Rotor Cleaning  
 Inspect brush holding assembly and change polarity of DC cables 
 Inspect and test Pedestal bearings 
 Check air gap and adjust as necessary 
 Inspect and clean of NER (neutral earth resistor) 
 Inspect Generator R T D’s  
 Loading tests with vibration and temperature measurements 
 
Note: All these checks and tests are to be performed in accordance with G.E 
procedures acceptance criteria and specifications

Appendix 7.2: GEC Generators Detailed Inspection Jobs 
 
 
- 
6 to 12 Monthly Check:  
 
 
Stator Windings Partial Discharge Analysis 
N.B. In case of HV generator put in service for continuous operation at 100% full 
capacity, it is recommended to perform on-line PDA every maximum interval 6

- 
Annual / 8,000 Running Hours

 
Visually inspect integrity of winding 






 
Inspect lubrication system for leaks 
 
Check all connections including terminal box etc. 
 
Check diodes and varistors. 
 
Check all fixing including holding down bolts. 
 
Check all ventilation ducts and passages as necessary. 
 
 
- 
40,000 Running Hours 
 
 
All above checks. 
 
Check alignment to gearbox.

3.0

As required 
 
 
Capacity Check 
a) Discharge the battery at the rate of 0.1C5 to 0.2C5 amperes (10 to 20 amperes for 100 
Ah battery) to a final average voltage of 1.0 volt per cell 
b) Charge 200% (i.e. 200Ah for a 100Ah battery at the same rate used in a). 
c) Discharge at the same rate used in a, measuring and recording current, voltage and 
time every hour, and more frequently towards the end of the discharge. This should be 
continued until the average voltage of 1.0 volts per cell is reached. The overall state of 
the battery can be seen, and if individual cell measurements are taken, the state of 
each cell can be observed.

## Page 179

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 7 - ELECTRICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : Page 39 of 62 
Issue No: 6 Addendum 
Rev. :  7 
Effective Date:01.12.2010 
 
Maintenance with POWER ON 
 
Sr. 
No 
Job task 
Frequency Remarks 
1.0 
Review safety procedures, instruction manual & previous 
records 
 
 
2.0 
The following test should be conducted 
 Evaluate the normal backup time required for the system. 
 Check that the normal load is connected to the charger and 
is in service. 
 Press the “Reset” button to test all LED’s & relays. 
 Connect a millivoltmeter across the battery shunt. 
 Connect a multi-meter across the battery.  
 Turn off the rectifier using the input C.B. Start a stopwatch. 
 Check that the charger O/P voltage measured across the 
load terminals is equal to the rated voltage ± 1% as 
indicated on the nameplate. 
 When the battery voltage is at 85% of the rated value, 
record the following: 
 The millivoltmeter reading across battery shunt ( US). 
 The discharge time in seconds (T). 
 Battery shunt current rating in ampere (IS) 
 Battery shunt voltage in millivolt (Um). 
 Use the formula {ID = (IS x Um)/ US} to determine the 
average battery current discharge. 
 From the discharge current and time, check that the battery 
capacity still confirms to the manufacturer specifications. 
 Turn on the rectifier and allow the batteries to charge for at 
least 10 minutes. 
 Press the “Reset” button if there is any red alarm LED’s 
on. If any of theses LED’s remain on after the reset button 
is pressed, 
refer to troubleshooting manual of the manufacturer. 
1 Year

3 Yearly PM 
 
 Remove the Stator end shields (D.E & N.D.E) 
 Inspect the end windings for deposits of oil and evidence of corona discharge or 
mechanical damage such as vibration, shafting, loose blocks, end ties etc. 
 Clean end windings 
 Measure IR & PI 
 Measure winding resistance 
 Check space heaters & their wirings 
 Check RTDs & their wirings 
 Cleaning of retaining rings and portion of end windings 
 Visual inspection of rotor end winding from sides of retaining rings. 
 Check air gap & readjust if required  
 Check AVR 
 Test Protection Relays 
 
Note: - 3 Yearly frequencies shall be linked with minor inspection of turbine.

## Page 183

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 7 - ELECTRICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : Page 43 of 62 
Issue No: 6 Addendum 
Rev. :  7 
Effective Date:01.12.2010 
 
Major Inspection 80,000 – 90,000 running hours 
(Frequency will be linked with major Gas Turbine overhaul) 
 
A. Dismantle generator & remove rotor. 
 
1. STATOR: 
1.1 Stator Frame: 
 
 Check for fatigue cracks in welding positions, distortion of supporting ribs and air 
diffusers. 
 Check end shields and other parts for cracks and rust. 
 Check base hold down bolts for tightness. 
 Check inlet plenum for cleanliness. 
 Check base transverse keys for tightness. 
 
1.2 Core Laminations: 
 
 Check for overheating, hot spots, looseness, distortion, corrosion and rubbing. 
 Check slot teeth for looseness or mechanical damage. 
 Check vent spacers for shift movement. 
 Check cooling vents for any blockage. 
 Check ends clamping rings/arrangement for looseness or mechanical damage. 
 
1.3 Stator Winding: 
 
 Check slot wedges and spacers for deterioration/looseness/shift. 
 Check lashing of winding overhangs. 
 Check stator coils for movements and for signs of abrasion where coils are supported. 
 Check looseness of insulation tapes, especially on end winding, leads and in stator 
cooling vents. 
 Check for overheating/hot spots/tracking/charring/pitting/corona discharge. 
 Check winding leads, cables for crack/overheating/insulation condition. 
 Check varnish for discolouration. 
 Check for cracks and mechanical damages on insulating material, especially on end 
winding. 
 Clean winding and spray with approved anti-tracking vanishes. 
 Check healthiness of RTD. 
 Check star point of winding and current transformers. 
 Measure insulation resistance and polarization index. 
 Measure winding resistance with low resistance bridge. 
 Carryout HV DLA tests. 
 
1.4 Anti-Condensation Heaters: 
 
 Check healthiness (Heater resistance, insulation resistance & mechanical condition). 
 Check thermostat control.

## Page 184

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 7 - ELECTRICAL MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : Page 44 of 62 
Issue No: 6 Addendum 
Rev. :  7 
Effective Date:01.12.2010 
 
2. ROTOR: 
 
 Clean the rotor & retaining rings. 
 Visual inspection of rotor end winding from sides of retaining rings. 
 Check wedges specially end wedges for sparking. 
 Check cooling ducts. 
 Carryout NDE inspection of retaining rings. 
 Apply thin coat of varnish. 
 Carryout electrical tests. 
 Check rotor fans for damage/cracks on blades, fixing keys and locking arrangement. 
 
3. Bearings: 
 
A. Inspect bearings. 
B. Replacement of bearings, if required. 
C. Inspection and measurement of bearing insulation (collector end bearing only). 
 
B. Reassemble Generator 
 
 Check air gap between stator and rotor for equispaced, if required, readjust. 
 Perform IR & PI test before taking in service. 
 
C. Test Protection Relays. 
D. Test AVR.

Appendix 7.23 Maintenance procedure for Lightning protection system 
 
Lightning protection systems are composed of main three (3) parts:  
A. Lightning Rods.  
B. Down conductors 
C. Grounding System.  
 
LIGHTNING PROTECTION SYSTEM 
LIGHTNING 
GROUND 
SYSTEMS 
DOWN 
CONDUCTORS 
LIGHTNING RODS 
Description 
Description 
Description 
Ground systems have to leak 
off the lightning current from 
the down conductor to the 
ground with a minimum of 
potential elevation between 
Lightning systems and 
buildings. 
They are constituted with 
copper rods buried in the soil 
connected together with 
tinned copper strip (crowfoot 
or triangle connections 
depending of the building).  
This ground system is 
connected to the electrical 
ground of the building. 
All connections inside the 
ground are aluminothermy 
connections and it is not 
necessary to check these 
connections, excepted if 
resistance value measurement 
of the ground system is very 
different of the previous 
measurement. 
 
 
The purpose of down 
conductors is to leak off 
lightning current from the 
lightning rod to the ground 
system properly. To 
assume this function they 
must be in proper state and 
correctly fixed along the 
structure.  
They are constituted of:  
 One or two tinned 
copper strips (30 x 2 
mm or 25 x 3 mm) fixed 
all along the structure 
building with 3 fixing 
by meters. The type of 
fixings depends of the 
type of the support.  
 One test joint for each 
down conductor  
 Equipotential 
connection with metallic 
parts inside the safety 
area of the down 
conductor.  
 
The lightning rod have to 
intercept the lightning 
stoke 
at 
a 
distance 
depending of the type of 
the device and the high 
of its top then the current 
is routing through the 
down 
conductor 
from 
which it is connected.

Tests & Checks 
Tests & Checks 
 Test resistance ground 
value measurement 
(every 4 year - 20%),  
 as per Shell DEP 
recommendations) 
a) Measure the ground 
resistance value with the 
ground building connected 
with recording the value 
on the form and compare 
with the previous

b) Disconnect the connection 
between lightning ground

of the lightning ground

(See annex I). 
 
 Checking of down 
conductors (every 
year - 5% )  
All the length of down 
conductors will be 
visually checked to 
verify the following:  
a) The strip is in a good 
state (not bended and 
not drilled, Remove 
the bends or change 
all the faulty parts of 
the conductor if 
necessary).

To change a part of a 
down conductor, 
remove all fixings of 
the faulty parts, cut this 
one and make an 
aluminothermic 
connection at each

part. Use new fixing for

b) All fixings 
accessories are in 
place (Add a new 
fixing accessories if 
necessary)  
 Checking of test joint 
(every year - 5% )  
Verify if the test joint 
is correctly closed (use 
a spanner), tighten as 
necessary  
 Equipotential 
connections (every 
year - 5%)  
c) Inspect & verify if 
connections are well 
screwed and if 
equipotential 
conductor is not 
damaged (Tighten or 
change the conductor 
if necessary). 
 Visual and mechanical inspection 
(every year - 5%): 
The purpose of this inspection is to 
verify that the general aspect of 
Lightning rod is correct and that 
fixations or connections are not 
faulty, particularly: 
a) Check visually the general aspect 
of all installations.  
b) Lightning rod and elevation mast 
must be vertically and straight. No 
important rusted marks will be 
visible.  
c) If it is not the case, concerned 
pieces will be removed and 
changed with similar pieces.  
d) Check all mechanical and 
electrical connections.  
e) Lightning rod and down conductor 
clamp (use a spanner to tighten as 
necessary),  
f) Lightning rod and mast elevation 
fixing (use a spanner to tighten as 
necessary),  
g) Mast elevation and supports (use a 
spanner to tighten as necessary).  
h) Equipotential connections with 
other metallic parts of the building 
(when existing).  
i) Check the connections 
(mechanical or welded)  
j) Tighten (or make another weld) as 
necessary.  
k) Check conductor(s) and fixation(s)  
l) Changed or repair with appropriate 
device as necessary. 
 
(See annex I). 
 
 Electrical test (every four years 
all)  
This checking requires using of 
specific tester. It consists to verify that 
the lightning rod is in the electrical 
manufacturer range. Use the notice of 
the tester to complete the test (see 
annex II).

Appendix 7.24 Ex- Motor Data To Be Repaired 
 
1. Availability of certification/design/repair documents shall be discussed and if it is found 
that these documents are not available at either parties (Gasco end user and Repair 
Facility), then the action shall be mutually agreed. 
2. After repair and testing, Reports/ Documentation shall be prepared and recorded as per 
clause 4.4.1.5 and its sub clauses of IEC60079-19:2006. 
3. Repair facility shall pay through attention to additional requirements for repair of different 
types of Ex equipment repair as detailed in IEC60079-19:2006 (main clauses 5 to 12). 
4. In case of motor brands which the repair facility has no authorization particularly for 
reclamation/ mechanical repairs other than re-winding (where copy winding technique 
may be adopted), Gasco may request marking R inside square. 
In such case, Repair Facility shall mobilize OEM specialist to Repair Facility to supervise 
the repair activities. 
The following shall be obtained: 
• 
Quotation for supervision/ replacement parts. 
• 
Agreement on repair procedures. 
• 
List of tests to be conducted and acceptance criteria. 
• 
OEM specialist approval on test results after conducting them by Repair facility 
under his supervision.

CHAPTER 8.0 
 
 
INSTRUMENT MAINTENANCE

## Page 204

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 8 - INSTRUMENT MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 1 of 23 
Issue No: 6 Addendum 
Rev 
: 7 
Effective Date:01.12.2011 
 
 
8.0 
INTRODUCTION 
 
The maintenance of instrumentation for measurement, control and safeguarding 
systems will, in general, follow the guide lines laid down in this Maintenance 
Policy. 
 
The Instrument Maintenance Policy is to ensure valid measurements and optimum 
control on which the performance of the plant depends with respect to product 
quality, production efficiency and safety 
 
Safety of plant and personnel is also reliant on instrumentation control and ESD 
systems (Instrument Protective Functions), which should be checked and maintained 
in good condition to ensure its availability on demand. 
 
The continued performance of instrumentation in a condition comparable to new 
or the original performance level as per design, as well as in a safe working 
condition, shall be a continuous goal of GASCO Maintenance.  
Consequently, the objective of this maintenance policy is to ensure maximum 
availability of the instruments at minimal cost to achieve the required level of 
performance at safe working conditions and to integrate the same with optimum 
maintenance efforts. 
 
This shall include: 
 
a) 
Establish an effective Preventive Maintenance schedule based on the 
Instrument manufacturers’ recommendation and experience. 
 
b) 
Testing of the Instrument Protective Functional (IPF) loops for their 
availability on demand, at intervals determined by IPF classification, to meet 
the required SIL. 
 
c) 
Corrective Maintenance: Recalibration, repair, cleaning or replacement to 
restore equipment to satisfactory working condition. 
 
d) 
Modifications/Replacements and additions to improve plant performance, 
reduce maintenance and enhance simplicity and reliability, by identifying 
requirements based upon failure rates and non-availability of spares and 
recommend for upgrade/replacement for obsolete instrument systems. 
 
It should be noted that this policy does not include maintenance procedures. The 
procedures, which are meant for the implementation of the policy, need to be 
developed by the sites, specific for their respective equipment/Instrument and the 
vendor recommendation.

## Page 205

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 8 - INSTRUMENT MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 2 of 23 
Issue No: 6 Addendum 
Rev 
: 7 
Effective Date:01.12.2011 
 
 
8.1 
TYPES OF MAINTENANCE 
 
 
8.1.1 On-Line Maintenance  
As the operation of the plant does not always allow for frequent stoppage of 
equipment, instrument maintenance will be carried out on-line wherever 
possible to minimise disruption to plant availability, using maintenance over-
rides, which shall be in line with DEP 32.30.10.10-Gen. 
  
 
8.1.2 Scheduled Shutdown Maintenance  
Will be used for the instruments and control systems, which could not be 
accessed during normal operation of the plant (e.g. turbines control systems, 
some fire and gas detectors, ESD and EDP systems etc). 
 
 
8.1.3 Break-Down Maintenance  
Every effort will be made to avoid UNSCHEDULED SHUTDOWNS; 
however a proper established trouble-shooting procedure should be utilized 
in minimising the unscheduled downtime. 
 
 
         This should include the following activities: 
 
- 
Written procedure or flow charts for trouble-  
 shooting based on previous experience. 
 
- 
Availability of relevant drawings and documents  
   located nearby the equipment. 
    
- 
Analysis and recording of the problems. 
 
- 
To establish Maintenance & Failure History and  
Critical instruments. 
 
 
 
All the data on the Breakdown, Preventive Maintenance, Failure reporting, 
the test frequencies shall be registered in the MAXIMO system for Tracking 
and Auditing.

8.2 
ENGINEERING REGULATIONS FOR PERFORMING INSTRUMENT 
MAINTENANCE 
 
 
8.2.1 As the ON-LINE instrument maintenance will cover most of the critical 
loops in the plant and in order to avoid any upsets to the plant operation and 
to ensure the safety of equipment and personnel, a procedure should be 
established as below. 
 
 
8.2.2   The governing document for the procedure is the latest revision of the Permit 
to Work procedure 
 
 
 
8.2.3    The procedure should cover the following as minimum requirements: 
 
 
 
 - 
The Instrument Section Head or Area Instrument Engineer should specify 
the necessary precautions for performing the job (e.g. Trip override, 
putting system on manual control, etc), taking in to consideration the 
associated risks involved in performing the job. 
 
 
 
 - 
All precautions are to be written on the work permit, discussed with 
Operations Department and signed by all concerned sections as required 
by the procedure in effect. 
 
 
 
 - 
All the “protection system overrides and “return to normal” needed for 
performing the job are to be recorded as per PTW (Permit to Work), duly 
signed by the Instrument Section Head and the Operations Department 
responsible supervisor. 
 
 
- 
Jobs that need special care are to be performed /supervised by highly 
competent personnel. 
 
 
 
 - 
After performing the job, it should be made sure that all protection 
systems are back in normal position and witnessed by Operations 
Department. 
 
 
8.2.4 Every plant could include more precautions that are deemed necessary to 
avoid any un-required shutdowns or unsafe practices.

8.3 
INSTRUMENTS AND CONTROL SYSTEM REPLACEMENT POLICY 
 
 
In view of the increased number of obsolete instruments and the in-house 
obsolescence surveys, GASCO has established a program to upgrade existing 
instrumentation and control systems by modern technology. 
 
 
GASCO strives to continuously improve the performance and reliability of the 
instrumentation and control systems to improve plant safety and availability. Part of 
this effort is to replace the stand-alone obsolete control systems with new systems 
having proven technology. All the new systems shall also have the capability to be 
interfaced with a plant DCS. 
 
 
8.3.1 Integrated Control Systems Projects 
 
 
GASCO has undertaken many upgrade programs for the replacement of 
pneumatically based instrumentation and control systems by modern Control 
Systems (ICS) in ASAB, Bab& Buhasa. 
 
The program entails the instrumentation and control system upgrade in all 
GASCO plants as follows: 
 
- RUWAIS Plant 
 
 
Project completed in May 2001 
- BUHASA Plant 
 
 
Project completed in March 2005 
- ASAB (NGL) and BAB Plants 
Project completed in 2006 
 
The ICS upgrade projects scope broadly comprise the installation and 
commissioning of the following main items: 
- 
Electronic field equipment such as transmitters. Note that ESD and 
control valves are not replaced. 
- 
Field junction boxes and cabling. 
- 
Distributed Control System for plant monitoring and control. 
- 
Safety Systems to handle the plant safety critical loops. 
- 
Interface with other subsystems such as Compressor anti-surge control 
and shutdown system as required.  
 
 
  
 
Also in the scope of work of the ICS upgrade projects is to carry out an IPF 
exercise in which the plant loops are classified and the test intervals 
determined according to DEP 32.80.10.10.The IPF classification & testing 
interval methodology is aimed at optimising maintenance effort with a 
reduction of risk of “failure on demand” for the service to be in line with the 
SIL requirements.

8.3.2 HABSHAN Plant   
In the case of HABSHAN plant, DCS was initially implemented in 1980, 
upgraded in 1995(OGD I) and extended in 2000(OGDII). 
IPF (SIL) classification exercise has been carried out for OGD I in 2004. For 
OGD II, the IPF (SIL) classification exercise was done along with DCS 
implementation in 2000. For Habshan 0, the IPF (SIL) classification was 
completed and implemented in 2005. 
However, until such time the IPF (SIL) classification and Test intervals are 
finalised and implemented, the maintenance policy in section 8.5, 8.6 and 8.7 
will be followed in Habshan 0. 
 
8.3.3      ASAB (AGD-I)  
In the case of ASAB Gas Plant, the IPF (SIL) classification exercise was 
done along with DCS implementation in January 2000. 
 
8.4 
MAINTENANCE POLICY STRATEGY 
 
Until such time that the ICS system and associated IPF classification are 
implemented in all plants, the Maintenance Policy Strategy for the near future will 
depend upon whether  
- 
ICS implementation done  
- 
IPF classification carried out   
 
Accordingly, the frequency of testing intervals will be appropriately determined. 
 
      
The Maintenance Policy strategy is therefore drawn on the following principles: 
 
- 
Current revision (Section 8.5 & 8.6) is applicable to ASAB (NGL) and BAB as 
described below. As the ICS is implemented in these plants, the DCS 
maintenance policy (8.7) along with the IPF based (8.8) test intervals will be 
additionally applicable. 
- 
For HABSHAN plant, the ICS (DCS) is already implemented. Hence, in 
addition to the section 8.5 & 8.6, DCS maintenance policy 8.7 will be presently 
followed. For OGD I & II as the IPF (SIL) classification was completed, the IPF 
based Test intervals (section 8.8) will be additionally applicable. 
- 
For ASAB (AGD-I) and RUWAIS plants, in addition to the section 8.5 & 8.6, 
DCS maintenance policy as described in section 8.7 will be followed. The new 
test intervals (section 8.8) will be based on the IPF (SIL) classification already 
done. 
- 
Section 9.0 gives some guidelines on the interface between the Instrument and 
Electrical sections and it is applicable to all Plants. Similarly, each plant shall 
define all areas of interface and the responsibilities with other engineering 
sections.

8.5   
PREVENTIVE MAINTENANCE POLICY 
 
8.5.1 Equipment Classification 
 
The degree of instrumentation involved in GASCO plants is considerable 
and covers a wide range of equipment.  In order to determine the most 
effective and economical methods of maintenance, PRIORITIES for 
instrument maintenance activities to be considered in the light of Equipment 
Classification types as described in Chapter 4.1(Vital-HSE, Vital-Production 
Loss, Critical & Non-Critical) . 
 
 
 
   VITAL(HSE) : 
- 
Where it is unsafe, uncontrollable to continue to operate without 
instrumentation (e.g. alarm and trips of main equipment, safety control 
loops),which are identified as INSTRUMENT PROTECTIVE 
FUNCTIONS and classified based on the required SAFETY 
INTEGRITY LEVELS. 
 
 
VITAL (PRODUCTION) : 
-  These are the instruments , the failure of which affects 
      the production and the quality of the products. 
 
 
  -   Where measurements are essential to the correct operation of the plant, 
with respect to product quality, quantity, production efficiency. 
 
 
CRITICAL : 
 
- 
Where continued operation of the plant may be affected after a certain 
period of time (e.g. single equipment which is not on-line all the time 
due to process requirements like heaters, dryers). 
 
- 
Where standby equipment is available but its failure will affect the 
operation and/or the production (e.g. instrument air compressors, NGL 
delivery pumps, power generation units). 
 
NON-CRITICAL : 
 
- 
Where optimum production or product specifications are not affected 
either because there is manual facilities or other means to control without 
affecting the plant performance (e.g. control valves with bypasses, 
equipment with standby in the same train). 
 
- 
Where instrumentation is helpful but other less convenient indications 
and controls are available (e.g. normal measurement and control loops).

8.5.2 Maintenance Activities 
 
 
The scope of instrument maintenance may be sub-divided as follows: 
 
 
 1) Calibration. 
 
 2) Instrument impulse and signal lines. 
 
 3) Instrument air supplies. 
 
 4) Alarm and trip settings. 
 
 5) Emergency shutdown system. 
 
 6) Intrinsic safe & Explosion Proof Instruments. 
 
 7) Valve and valve actuators. 
 
 8) Fire and gas detecting systems. 
 
 9)   Package control systems. 
 
10)  Fiscal or custody transfer measurements. 
 
11)  Environmental protection. 
 
12)  Measurement standards (test equipment). 
 
13)  Modifications 
      14)  Drawings and documentation. 
      15)  Analyzers (QMI) 
      16)  Auxiliary Systems (PA, Radio, Telephone, CCTV) 
 
17)  SCADA System                
      18)  Instrument Grounding  
      
 
 
The magnitude of the maintenance task will be determined by the nature 
(criticality) of the application, their failure rate and may be continuously 
reassessed as a result of experience with the equipment and the process. 
 
     Generally, the determination of the frequency of maintenance will be 
according to the plant experience and vendor recommendations.  However, 
the frequencies given in this document should be considered as a guideline 
only. 
 
      However, whilst categorisation may be useful for the overall plant 
maintenance and performance assessment, instrument maintenance will be 
based on the ACTIVITIES listed above. 
 
 
It is the policy of the Company to implement such maintenance activities as 
to MINIMISE UNWANTED SHUTDOWNS AND UNSAFE OPERATION 
caused by failure of instrumentation classified as vital (Types 1 and 2).

8.5.2.1 Calibration 
 
 
 
 
Zero and span checks will be carried out at intervals determined by 
experience and the application.  Standard samples shall be used on 
analytical devices.  However, full loop checking including 
transmitters, impulse lines, booster relays and control room 
instruments are to be carried out regularly (recommended frequency 
is twice a year)  
 
8.5.2.2 Instrument Impulse and Signal Lines 
 
 
 
 
Tappings and impulse lines shall be checked for leaks, plugging and 
corrosion of fittings whenever the instruments connected to these 
impulse lines are under maintenance.  However the maximum period 
between inspections should not exceed one year. 
 
8.5.2.3 Instrument Air Supplies 
 
 
 
 
Filter regulators shall be checked for leak, moisture and dust and 
correct pressure setting.  This shall be done along with any 
maintenance for the corresponding instrument.  However the 
maximum period between inspections should not exceed one year. 
 
 
8.5.2.4 Alarm and Trip Settings 
 
 
 
 
Checks with maximum intervals of SIX MONTHS shall be made to 
ensure that alarm and trip settings are in accordance with the design 
requirements, with reference to the documents. Trip settings shall be 
checked with certain precautions (Overrides) and shall include 
checking of the field switch calibration and the actuation of the 
corresponding tripping relay/output module. (i.e. the whole loop from 
field to panel). 
 
8.5.2.5 Emergency Shutdown and Safeguarding System 
 
 
 
 
Inspection and testing of emergency shutdown system shall be carried 
out on the complete system or parts of it (zones), with reference to 
the CAUSE & EFFECT.      
  
 
 
The system should be tested for the complete loop including the 
ESD/EDP Push buttons.  The testing of the system shall be carried 
out during the scheduled shutdowns of the plants.

The following activities can be checked as below: 
1) System diagnostic checking and recovery – monthly. 
- Routine check (from the console)  
2) Replacement of modules – based on diagnostic & failure reports. 
3) Check the system cabinet power supply for proper voltage – 6 
month. 
 
8.5.2.6 Intrinsic Safe & Explosion Proof Instruments 
 
 
 
 
Intrinsically safe circuits must be treated with special care.  
Particular attention shall be paid to ensure that the maintenance on 
these circuits does not in any way invalidate the intrinsic safety. 
 
 
 
 
Tests on intrinsic safety earth connections shall be made to ensure 
recommended resistance values are not exceeded (recommended 
frequency is once per year). 
 
Explosion Proof Instruments: Any maintenance on the EExd 
instruments shall be carried out with all necessary safety precautions 
and the checking to be carried out with Approved/certified instruments. 
    
8.5.2.7 Valves and Valve Actuators 
 
 
 
 
A) CONTROL VALVES 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Control valves should be planned for inspection according to service, 
performance and availability of bypass and spares. 
 
 
 
 
 
Control valves that show a repeated failure or trouble should be 
serviced at the next earliest opportunity (shutdown). 
 
 
 
 
 
However, valve air connections, limit switches, stems, positioner 
links should be inspected, cleaned and maintained during the 
preventive maintenance or intervention work related to these valves. 
 
 
 
 
B) ESD VALVES 
 
 
ESD valves are categorized as IPF valves, non-IPF valves and EDP 
valves. The testing requirements include stroke test (partial stroke 
test, full stroke test) and seat leakage test.  
 
While carrying out the Full Stroke test, the time taken for the full 
closure of the valve shall be recorded to ensure that they meet the 
specified closure time as per the valve design data which is based on 
the process safety time of the respective protective function

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY

Effective Date:01.12.2011 
 
Seat leakage test shall be applicable only for the valves identified 
with Tight Shut Off (TSO) requirement and shall be carried out in the 
workshop. 
 
TSO requirement for the IPF valves shall be decided as a part of SIL 
review. For non-IPF valves or valves not verified during SIL study, a 
separate process safety/QRA review shall be carried out to identify 
TSO requirement based on application criticality.  
 
Category wise testing requirements are summarized as follows. Any 
deferment of the test from the above schedule requires management 
approval: 
 
1) IPF valves (Part of the safeguarding function and initiated by 
process upsets) 
 
•  Stroke testing (PST, FST) and leakage testing requirements shall 
be followed as per the SIL study report 
 
•  Where SIL studies have not been done, then the following criteria 
shall be applicable: 
- Valves with PST: If PST is done once in 6 months, FST 
once in 3 years 
- Valves without PST facility or valves in which PST is not 
done once in 6 months - FST shall be carried out at least once 
in 2 years  
- Valves identified with TSO requirement shall be leak tested 
once in 5 Years 
 
 The opportunity of interim shutdowns should be availed to test IPF valves. 
 
2) Non-IPF Valves (Operator Initiated, Used for primary/secondary 
isolation in case of external events such as F&G) 
 
• 
Valves shall follow the same testing criteria as that of “valves 
where SIL study have not been done” as mentioned in category 1 
above. 
 
 
3) EDP Valves (Normally closed, Used for de- pressurisation) 
 
• 
EDP valves shall be stroke tested for full opening once in every 2 
Years. The opportunity of interim shutdowns should be availed to 
test the valves.

Generally from process safety point of view, leakage test is not 
essential for EDP valves. Critical valves related to product 
inventory loss need to be identified by operations and these to be 
leak tested during planned shutdown. 
 
C) PIPELINE ROVs  
 
As per the current practice followed, pipeline ROVs to undergo PST 
once in 06 months and full closure test once in 3 years.  
For the ROVs closer to CRS and the specific ones which can comply 
with the above, any opportunity shutdown shall be utilized to carry 
out the FC test. 
 
Valves identified as TSO during the process safety/ QRA review, 
leakage test to be carried out once in 5 years.  
 
Note:  
For all ESD valves and pipeline ROVs requiring leak test as per the 
above criteria and which cannot be removed from line due to specific 
reasons ( Welded valves, non-availability of  isolation valves ,etc. ) , 
on line testing facilities like Double Block and Bleed arrangement  
shall be implemented and until such time ,  portable ultrasonic 
meters for seat leak detection can be used .On-line Inspection during 
S/D of the valve internals  (Seat & Plug) without removing the valve 
body from line can be given credit for some test coverage . 
 
 
8.5.2.8 Fire and Gas Detection Systems 
 
 
 
 
A continuous programme for the testing and maintenance of 
detection and alarm systems for fire and gas shall be carried out 
preferably by personnel not concerned with normal instrument 
maintenance tasks (dedicated F&G team). 
The testing interval shall be based on the vendor’s recommendation 
and in line with DEP Standard 32.30.20.11(GEN). However, a 
minimum frequency of once in 6 months is recommended. 
 
 
 
 
For F&G detectors inside the enclosures of Turbine Control Units 
which cannot be accessed during normal operation, the same shall be 
tested during the Unit shutdown. 
 
Regular checking and inspection of deluge valves should be done 
with the involvement of Operations or Safety staff, to ensure its  
availability and proper operation when required. This should be 
carried out periodically once a year.

8.5.2.9 Package Control Systems 
 
 
 
 
Testing, calibration of Package control systems like Turbine Control, 
Anti-surge control systems shall be scheduled annually and should 
not exceed 18 months  
 
 
 
 
Vendor assistance can be considered as the systems are specialised in 
nature and Service Agreements can be entered into. 
 
It is preferred that turbine fuel gas control (throttle) valves to be 
inspected, calibrated or replaced at least once in every two years of 
service. 
 
8.5.2.10 Fiscal and Custody Transfer Measurements 
 
 
 
 
Where quantitative measurements are made for the purpose of 
transferring products, instruments shall be checked at regular 
intervals against known and accepted standards, local requirements 
(weights and measures) and requirements of contract parties.  The 
frequency of testing shall be determined by experience with the 
equipment and the vendor recommendations. The meters should be 
calibrated at least once in 3 months. Instruments (Master gauges) 
used for the calibration shall be checked and certified by the 
Manufacturer once in a year. 
The recommended frequency for Inspection of Meters for Mass 
balance / Custody transfer applications shall be as follows: 
 
i) Orifice meters shall be inspected during scheduled shut down.  
 
ii) Turbine meters and Coriolis meters shall be calibrated every 2 
years. 
 
DEP 32.32.00.12 is to be referred for (additional) guidelines. 
 
 
8.5.2.11   Protection from the Environment 
 
 
 
 
In the U.A.E. plant conditions for instrumentation are severe with 
HIGH 
AMBIENT 
TEMPERATURES 
AND 
AGGRESSIVE 
ATMOSPHERES.  Where protection has been provided this must be 
properly used and maintained.  Junction box covers with seals 
properly fitted should be bolted in place.  Consideration should be

given to added protection where instrumentation is affected by

Junction boxes, especially on the gas compressor packages, should be 
planned for inspection during the scheduled shutdowns to ensure that 
they are free of water, moisture, dirt, etc. and properly sealed. They

8.5.2.12 Measurement Standards (Test Equipment) 
 
 
 
 
Where standards are used for calibration, i.e. standard gauges, digital 
meters, test boxes, these shall be checked periodically and 
STAMPED WITH THE DATE OF TEST, at least once a year .The 
calibration and certification to be carried out by approved third party 
agencies against international standards (NIST etc). 
    
8.5.2.13  Fault Analysis and Modifications 
 
 
 
 
The analysis of faults, malfunctions or failure of instrumentation 
affecting the normal operation of plant and the recording failure 
reports and the periodical review of the failure history will facilitate 
prevention of occurrence of such repeated failures. MAXIMO 
system facilities shall be used to the fullest extent possible to 
record the historical data and maintain the test interval 
frequencies. 
            Continuous assessment of the performance of individual items of 
instrumentation or complete systems will indicate where replacement 
(obsolescence) or modifications are necessary to reduce maintenance, 
increase availability, or reliability. Any change or modification to the 
existing instrument and control system should be done only through 
an approved PCR (refer to Plant Change Procedures). 
 
 
 
 
However, standardisation of the types of the instrument used for such 
modification should be carefully considered to minimise spare parts 
inventory and enhance inter-changeability. 
 
 
8.5.2.14 Drawings and Documentation 
 
 
 
 
Drawings and documentation shall be UPDATED and As-built ones 
recorded when any changes or additions are carried out as per the 
PCR procedures. The updated drawings shall be available on the 
respective locations for immediate reference as well as in Central 
Record office.

8.5.2.15  Analyzers (QMI) 
               
               Analyzers are quality-measuring instruments that include on-line and 
Laboratory instruments .The maintenance of these instruments are 
vital 
for

operation 
to

and            
compliance to process design/safety requirements. 
               The periodical maintenance activities and the intervals shall be

instruments are very delicate and requires specific skills, the               
job is to be assigned to adequately experienced and trained personnel. 
                
8.5.2.16  Auxiliary Systems (PA, Telephone , Radio and CCTV) 
                             
These are auxiliary facilities not directly related to the production. 
However as they are essential facilities for communication and plant

has to be carried out for the proper working of the units. 
Depending on the existing site practices, the maintenance of the

                                    All the above system shall be tested for their Operational

8.5.2.17 SCADA System  
    
                              
At present, HABSHAN plant mostly incorporates SCADA system for 
monitoring and control of wells. SMC system is used for monitoring 
and controlling the Sales gas Pipeline network. The main equipments 
to be covered for preventive maintenance are RTUs, MTUs and I/O 
modules, communication links etc. The log of all these information 
shall be maintained for future reference.  
 
              
 
Daily routine check of the SCADA/SMC systems shall be carried out 
through standard system diagnostic displays, alarms and events. The 
servers and the networks shall be monitored for performance as per 
the system specification requirements. The log of all these 
information shall be maintained for future reference. 
                            
It is recommended to carry out the complete functional check of the 
RTUs once in 3 months and MTUs once in a year. 
              
 
Any modification on the system configuration shall follow the 
“software modification” procedure. 
                              
Other   maintenance activities on the system console and software 
shall be as per vendor recommended procedure and intervals.

8.5.2.18  Grounding 
 
Grounding of the various Instrument systems shall be checked at least 
once a year. A checklist to be developed which shall cover but not 
limited to the following. 
Control room System panels shall be checked for AC ground, Signal 
ground and Intrinsic Safe ground wherever applicable .The ground 
wires shall be visually inspected for damage to the cables, for any 
corrosion, continuity and tightness of the connections. 
 
While checking on the main Earthing rod on line, care should be 
taken to provide temporary earth points before disconnection. The 
earth rod on the field shall be checked for firm fixing. 
All the field junction boxes to the local earthing point shall be 
visually checked for tightness at the terminating points. 
 
Field instruments shall be periodically checked (once a year) for 
continuity and firm connection of the signal and shield grounding at 
the control room equipment end. It should be ensured that that the 
signal and the shield are not grounded at the instrument (field) end. 
When using special tools such as instruments for calibration/service, 
special care shall be taken to avoid discharge of static charge and the 
instruments shall be checked for proper earthing before the job is 
carried out.         
 
8.6       BREAKDOWN MAINTENANCE POLICY 
 
In the event of a breakdown the time taken to repair is normally a vital factor.  
Where spare or standby items have been installed, repairs can be carried out at 
leisure but this is not normally the case with instrumentation and control systems. 
 
It is therefore the policy wherever possible to REPLACE and REPAIR, to cut 
down the time taken to diagnose faults within instruments by replacing a 
unit/printed circuit board/module or even a complete instrument. 
 
In many electronic instruments, printed circuit board replacement is preferable to 
component replacement because of possible damage to sensitive circuit elements. 
 
 
For items, which should be returned to manufacturer for repairing, manufacturer 
should submit to GASCO a detailed inspection report with all the findings and the 
possible causes.  This should help in either avoiding similar problems or in deciding 
to change the type of module of better design.

8.7 
DCS MAINTENANCE 
 
The following DCS Systems have been implemented in GASCO’S various plants 
 
HABSHAN 
- ABB BAILEY INFI 90   
RUWAIS  
- YOKOGAWA CENTUM CS 3000   
BUHASSA 
- HONEYWELL TPS   
ASAB (AGD-I) 
 
- SIEMENS TELEPERM XP   
All the above systems have their own system hardware architectures and different 
software features, the maintenance requirements and activities are basically the 
same. The maintenance policy is generally applicable to all DCS systems. 
The activities that need to be carried out as a part of the DCS maintenance are 
broadly classified as follows: 
8.7.1  System Diagnostic Checking and Recovery 
 
The daily routine check of the DCS system shall be carried out through 
system diagnostic displays and System alarms from the Engineering console. 
Individual modules shall be checked for any error messages and corrective 
action taken. The same should be updated in the system failure history. 
 
8.7.2  System Performance & Network Monitoring 
 
The system and the network shall be monitored for the error-free 
performance as per the system specification requirements and a footprint 
recorded for reference. 
A Performance Monitor Program shall be established in consultation with 
the system vendor. 
This shall include the monitoring of the following as applicable. 
1. CPU idle time (PCU/FSC) 
2. Network Loading 
3. HIS (HMI) Monitor-Processor idle time. 
4. Disk storage Capacity utilisation. 
5. Error counts on serial links. 
6. Performance of printers, Hard copy units etc. 
 
The recommended frequency is minimum once a month.

8.7.3 System Configuration Activities 
 
Any system configuration activities required for system enhancement or 
modification shall be carried out only after the approval of the Software 
modification request. 
 
 
8.7.4   System Back-Up & Restore 
 
The DCS system back-up activities consist of regular backup of the 
configuration of the operator stations (HIS/OIS), EWS, Report generation 
and plant data of Trends, alarms and Event archival tapes (if applicable). 
Any configuration change of the above shall be backed up immediately after 
any changes.  
      
EWS configuration shall be backed up every week and the Replacement of 
Trend and Archival tapes shall be typically once in 2 months or based on the 
specific system design. The backup details shall be entered in a Logbook. 
 
The back-up plant History data shall be available on Tapes for at least 12 
months. 
The restore operation of the Backup tape shall be tried at least twice a year 
for authenticity of Backup operation and Tape media validation. 
  
 
8.7.5  DCS System Records & History Database 
 
The following system records shall be maintained for future analysis and 
management reporting: 
 
    1. Modification details 
    2. System Activities Log  
    3. System Backup Log 
    4. History (Archive) Data base 
 
8.7.6. System Hardware 
All the system hardware modules shall be regularly monitored and 
replacement of the modules shall be carried out based on the Module Error 
diagnostic and failure reports.

The System cabinets shall be checked for proper Power supply voltages once 
a month. 
 
The performance of the system hardware modules in the cabinet will be

in the AC duct is checked and cleaned at least once in 2 months. 
 
The requirement and testing of System earthing is covered in the separate
