GASCO has established Maintenance Service contracts with the     
respective DCS system vendors covering Preventive Maintenance, Remedial 
Maintenance, Emergency Call-out, Telephone support and the Spares supply 
which should be best utilised for the continuous trouble free performance of 
the system. 
      
8.8 
TEST INTERVALS FOR IPF FUNCTIONS 
 
8.8.1 Instrumented Protective Functions Classification and Testing 
Frequency 
 
A quantitative assessment of the criticality of each loop shall be carried out 
by applying DEP 32.80.10.10 – Classification and Implementation of 
Instrumented Protective Functions.  
 
The methodology outlined in the DEP classifies the Instrumented Protective 
Function (IPF) according to the Safety Integrity Level (SIL) as recommended 
in the industry wide accepted standards IEC 61508 and IEC 61511  
 
 
The IPF classification is part of the Risk and Reliability Management (RRM) 
program by Shell and shall be supported by the RRM software. 
  
The IPF classification shall apply to all safety loops of the Plant including 
Process, Fire & Gas and Utilities. 
This policy does not deal with the implementation aspects of IPF. It is 
expected that the ICS projects, subsequent to the IPF classification exercise, 
implement the IPF in order to meet the SIL requirements.  
 
The testing frequencies shall be determined so as to maximise the test 
interval whilst meeting the SIL requirement of the IPF.

## Page 222

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 8 - INSTRUMENT MAINTENANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 19 of 23 
Issue No: 6 Addendum 
Rev 
: 7 
Effective Date:01.12.2011 
 
The calculated testing intervals are therefore defined on an IPF by IPF basis. 
This shall be rationalised and phased into a Maintenance Schedule 
considering 
practical 
constraints 
and 
Maintenance 
optimising 
requirements. 
The following guidelines shall be followed in determining the test frequency: 
 
- 
The minimum test interval shall be 6 months. Experience gained shows 
that it is not practical to have a shorter testing interval. In such cases, 
the established test frequencies shall be continued and the IPF 
implementation to be reviewed for implementation, to achieve the 
desired SIL. 
 
- 
Irrespective of the SIL classification, initiators shall be tested with a 
coverage factor of as close as possible to 1 during planned shutdowns 
(maintenance interval). Proper functioning of line monitoring shall be 
tested during every planned shutdown. 
- 
The maintenance interval for IPSs (Instrument Protective Systems) shall 
be set equal to the life of the IPS, which is usually 10 years. 
 
- 
Irrespective of the SIL class, final elements shall be tested with a 
coverage factor of as close as possible to 1 during planned shutdowns. 
The maintenance interval shall not be more than 4 years if the 
planned shutdown cycle exceeds 4 years. Proper functioning of line 
monitoring shall be tested during every planned shutdown. 
 
- 
If the valve action is monitored by the DCS by monitoring the effect of 
the valve movement to the safe position and the DCS generates a report, 
this report may be considered as a test. The valve closing time should 
also be checked so that incipient problems (valve becoming sticky) are 
also detected. The valve moving to a safe position is not necessarily 
detected by a position switch; the reduction of flows to zero, if a closed 
valve is the safe position, can also be considered as an indication of the 
same.  
 
- 
Automatic testing is one way to reduce the test interval and at the same 
time minimise un-revealed failures and nuisance trips caused by human 
errors during manual testing. Irrespective of the automatic tests 
performed, manual testing with a coverage factor of as close as possible 
to 1 shall be performed during planned shutdowns (maintenance 
interval). 
- 
 If for any operational reasons (non-availability of scheduled plant 
shutdowns), the testing cannot be carried out at intervals 
recommended by IPF, management approval should be obtained to 
defer the same and subsequently, the same need to be reviewed and 
re-evaluated.

8.8.2 Instrumented Protective Functions Management 
 
The management of the Instrumented Protective Functions shall be in 
keeping with DEP 32.80.10.12 – Management of Instrumented Protective 
Functions, specifically the sections that deal with operation, maintenance and 
modification aspects. 
 
The management of the IPF shall be aimed at providing a structural and 
practical approach to achieving and maintaining compliance with the 
relevant standards. In addition it should aim at the continuous improvement 
of the management of IPF. 
For existing installations, the objective is not to design IPFs but rather to 
confirm the suitability of the existing IPF implementations and to bring the 
required documentation up to a minimum standard.  
 
In addition, an adequate management of IPFs optimises the maintenance and 
inspection effort. 
  
 
The management of the IPF shall address the following aspects: 
- 
Competence management, human resources and manpower allocation 
- 
Testing methodology, schedule, recording and archiving of testing results 
- 
Preventive, predictive and corrective maintenance  
- 
Trip reporting and incident investigation 
- 
Failure rate data collection 
- 
IPF requirements review 
 
The Instrumentation department (ESX) in Abu Dhabi shall be the centralised 
point of control of the classification, implementation and management of the 
Instrumented Protective Functions, in coordination with the respective site 
Instrument Section Heads. 
 
8.8.3 Implementation of Modifications 
 
 
The requirement for modifications in the instrumentation and/or control 
systems can arise due to a variety of reasons. These can range from 
technology obsolescence, bringing an IPF to the required SIL level, fit for 
purpose requirements to economic grounds. 
 
Continuous assessment of the performance of individual items of 
instrumentation or complete systems will indicate where replacement or 
modifications are necessary to reduce maintenance, increase efficiency or 
reliability. 
The implementation of modifications is governed by the latest revision of the 
Plant Change Procedure.

8.9.      MAINTENANCE ORGANISATION  
 
8.9.1 Maintenance Resources 
 
Instrumentation covers a wide field from fairly substantial mechanical 
devices to sophisticated microelectronics.  At the same time the maintenance 
policy is founded upon the realisation of maintenance objectives with the 
optimum use of available resources. 
 
 
To achieve this end, the requirements are as follows: 
 
1. Technicians with experience covering the complete range of 
instrumentation together with a sound knowledge of the plants and 
processes, and associated control and safe guarding systems. 
      
2. Highly skilled people like engineers, supervisors and technicians will, as 
a matter of policy, be part of GASCO establishment.  
 
3. For specialised categories, contracting (outsourcing) of Manpower may 
be considered, based upon the number of equipment, staffing levels and 
site-specific constraints. 
 
4. Adequate and reliable testing equipments shall be made available and 
standard instruments shall be used for calibration and the same shall be 
checked periodically and certified by approved third party agencies. 
          
 
8.9.2    Maintenance Agreement and Vendor Support 
 
The company shall pursue the establishment of Maintenance agreements 
with the Vendors to optimise company manpower requirements and to 
ensure a cost-effective availability of skilled support from the Vendor. 
 
The Maintenance Agreements shall cover the following aspects: 
 
- 
Standards Callout Services -for corrective Maintenance Support and 
modification works 
- 
Emergency Callout Services  
 
 
Effort should be made to standardise on a Vendor for specific equipment as 
well as to establish Maintenance agreements with the Vendors across all 
GASCO sites. 
 
Replacement of a faulty instrument (rather than repairing it) shall be 
considered based upon the criticality and the availability requirements of the

equipment. In other cases, an optimum solution based on the maintenance 
and the repairing cost factors shall be adopted. 
 
For items, which should be returned to manufacturer for repairing, 
manufacturer should submit to GASCO a detailed inspection report with all 
the findings and the possible causes.  This should help in either avoiding 
similar faults. 
 
As the Maintenance Policy relies on replacing faulty parts, spare parts 
management becomes a key element. 
 
The company should establish a suitable spare parts management programs 
that can either: 
 
- 
be the direct purchase of spare parts from the Vendor. The Materials 
department in GASCO will then be responsible for managing the spare 
parts, or 
 
- 
engage the Vendor in the management of the spare parts. Under this 
program, GASCO does not own the spare parts and pay a fee to the 
Vendor for the spare parts availability. The spare parts management by 
the Vendor normally entails the storage of critical parts at site with a 
guaranteed replenishment time, and storage of non-critical parts at the 
Vendor’s location, preferably within the UAE with a guaranteed delivery 
time.  
The decision on the strategy to pursue shall be made on a case-by-case basis 
depending on the cost effectiveness of the options, warranty considerations 
as well as the Vendor performance in managing the spare parts. n either case, 
the Vendor shall recommend the critical spare parts.  
     
 
9.0 
INTERFACE BETWEEN INSTRUMENT AND OTHER MAINTENANCE 
SECTIONS 
 
 
In order to achieve a smooth interface and to ensure that maintenance is covering the 
overall system, the division of responsibilities between Instrument and other 
Engineering Sections in the areas of interface shall be clearly defined, in line with 
the practices in other GASCO sites, to have uniformity. 
 
 
The following are only guidelines for some typical examples: 
 
 
1. 
Electrical Section shall be responsible for the inspection and maintenance of 
AC/DC powering of instrument systems, including back-up and the AC/DC 
distribution network up to and including the fused take-off points.  Instrument 
Section responsibility shall start from, but not include the take-off points.

9.0 Introduction 
 
The objective of civil maintenance work is to maintain the integrity, enhance the reliability 
and prolong the life of the civil facilities and installations at GASCO plants & pipelines 
network.  For the purpose of this policy, the following definitions shall apply: 
 
Plant   
Operations Group Divisions at Habshan/Bab,Asab,Buhasa, 
Ruwais &   Pipelines  
TP 
Technical Projects Division (Abu Dhabi) 
TS 
Engineering & Technical Support Division (Abu Dhabi) 
RTS 
Request for Technical service 
DGS                                  Company Specification 
Shell DEP 
Shell Design & Engineering Practice 
 
Civil maintenance works will be carried out by experienced qualified contractors on a 
contract basis in accordance with a detailed scope of work, attachments and schedule of 
charges or to be carried out by GASCO in house, if possible as per the available 
manpower/resources of the particular plant 
 
Work will be designed and executed in accordance with the original design specifications 
suitably modified to comply with current European and U.S. standards and codes of practice, 
DGS and Shell DEPs.  Preparation of contract documents, budgetary and overall contract 
administration shall be the responsibility of either the Plant or Technical Projects Division 
(Abudhabi) based on the nature and size of works.  
 
The supervision and execution of major maintenance work shall be carried out by TP 
division or by the Plant ( in case the plant has Civil Engineer) as per the discretion of the 
concerned plant.  Routine and minor maintenance work shall be supervised and executed by 
the Plant. Technical support for specific technical issues can be requested from TS division, 
through RTS. 
 
The objective of the required civil work shall meet the main recommendations specified in 
the inspection report. 
 
The inspection of GASCO's plant civil items shall be planned and executed in accordance 
with the following main steps: 
 
9.1 
Inspection programme (major and minor items). 
9.2 
Inspection frequency. 
9.3 
Reports, records and filing. 
9.4 
Analysis of deficiencies. 
9.5 
Repair policy and schedule. 
9.6 
Scope of work, Execution, Supervision and Responsibility.

9.1 
INSPECTION PROGRAMME 
 
 
The objective of inspection is to detect and monitor deterioration/defects in the civil 
facilities such as settlement, underground or aboveground cracks, delamination and 
erosion which may affect the integrity/reliability of these facilities. The requirement 
of preventive maintenance for any structure/installation shall be also considered in 
the inspection. 
 
 
Civil facilities to be included in the inspection programme shall be identified and 
listed for each plant/site.  
 
The inspection shall initially be on a visual basis.  Non-destructive testing shall be 
applied only on special recommendation and for critical areas. 
 
 
The inspection of civil items will be carried out by the Plant Civil Engineer.  The 
services of a consultant can be engaged by the plant for this inspection. The planning 
and budget arrangement of consultancy services shall be the responsibility of the 
plant.  TS division assistance in the tendering activities of consultancy services can 
be requested through RTS.   
 
The inspection of civil items shall be followed up at site by the Plant Civil 
Engineer/Supervisor with frequencies stipulated by the severity of the cases.   
 
 
Civil items shall be classified in the following three categories: 
 
 
 
- 
Major items 
 
 
- 
Minor items 
 
 
- 
Emergency items 
 
 
- 
Major Items: 
 
 
 
Major items shall be treated as priority items from an inspection point of view. 
 
 
 
Major inspection items will consist in general of the following: 
 
 
Roads and Paving  
 
- 
Main roads, Process Area pavement and flooring. 
 
Buildings  
 
 
 
- 
Permanent buildings on site (control room, substations, administrative 
building, warehouse, safety building, workshops, PABX Building, 
residential complex   etc). 
- 
Recreation facilities/ swimming pools.

Concrete Structures  
 
- 
Cooling water structures (Sea water intake, outfall culvert, weir box,

-

pits/sumps, etc.). 
 
 
- 
Pipeline sleepers/supports. 
 
 
Concrete Foundations  
 
-

-

-

-

-

Fire Proofing  
 
-

      -     Fireproofing on external walls of process buildings

-

Pipeline Network  
 
- 
Right of way (ROW). 
 
- 
Embankment, soil stabilization, and slope protection. 
 
 
 
-      Track/Rig Crossings of the pipelines. 
 
 
 
 
Inspection of fireproofing shall be formally considered as part of the civil 
inspection/maintenance activities. Collaboration with Plant Inspection Engineer

Refractory works shall be inspected by the Plant Inspection Engineer.

- 
Minor Items: 
 
 
 
Inspection of minor items may be done only on spot check (random) basis and 
when deficiencies are reported by Plant personnel (e.g. Inspection Engineer on 
site, Plant Civil Engineer/Supervisor, etc). 
 
 
 
Reporting of minor civil items inspection could be done by exception. 
 
 
- 
Emergency Items: 
 
 
 
In the case of any emergency situation/exceptional conditions occurring on site.  
Plants shall carry out a preliminary inspection by the Plant Civil 
Engineer/supervisor ,then decide whether back-up assistance is required from 
TS/TP divisions or not  
 
 
9.2 
INSPECTION FREQUENCY 
 
 
Inspection of the major civil items is the responsibility of the Plant and shall be 
carried out as per the following frequencies. 
 
a) Inspection of the major civil items shall be carried out once every year by a Civil 
Engineer and report issued. 
 
b) Monitoring of the condition of the major civil items shall be carried out at 6 
months interval by a Civil Engineer or a Civil supervisor 
 
c) Periodic settlement survey, at 3 year intervals, shall be carried out by specialist 
contractor to check/monitor the stability of major/critical equipment foundations

and structures within the plants. Frequencies may be increased or decreased 
depending on the settlement rates recorded.  Special independent monitoring may

d) The remote area facilities of pipeline division (i.e. pipeline berm, right of way

9.3 
REPORTS, RECORDS AND FILING 
 
 
- 
Reports: 
 
 
 
Comprehensive inspection reports shall be prepared by the Plant Civil Engineer 
or by the engaged consultants as the case may be.  
 
These reports shall be prepared periodically in order to bring out the 
development trend and thus enable GASCO to plan repair works at an early 
stage. 
 
 
 
Inspection reports shall contain the following information: 
 
 
 
- 
Comprehensive description of the existing situation. 
 
 
 
- 
Trend of failure development. 
 
 
 
- 
Investigation made to assess the cause of failure. 
 
 
 
- 
Final recommendations (repair or investigation, or more observations). 
 
 
 
Photographs, sketches and marking on movements shall be considered as an aid 
to the failure records. 
 
 
 
One complete copy of the report shall be retained with the plant civil team for 
future record/reference. 
 
 
 
- 
Records: 
 
 
 
Inspection records will be mainly a record sheet for each structure which will 
contain all major items, findings and recommendations.

Record sheets will be classified by types as per the following: 
 Civil 1 - Roads and Paving

in order to establish historical data for the same. 
 
 
- 
Filing: 
 
 
 
Historical files such as final project reports and as-built documentation 
covering all information related to the inspection, repair method, cross 
referenced materials records, QA/QC tests result and cost implication shall be 
prepared for each major repair contract. Copies of these historical files shall be 
kept with custodian of the project and the plants for future references. 
 
Files and inspection records shall be prepared for all major civil items and shall 
be maintained and updated by the Plant.  For other items, files may be prepared 
when any deficiency is discovered. 
 
9.4 
ANALYSIS OF DEFICIENCIES 
 
 
Deficiencies and failures shall be properly monitored and investigated. 
 
Their causes and development trends shall be assessed before deciding on the method 
and schedule of repairs. 
 
 
The following are general guidelines for handling deficiencies and failures: 
 
 
- 
Initially, deficiencies shall be inspected visually.  If the situation is not serious 
enough to jeopardise the integrity and the safety of the structure, the 
phenomenon needs only to be recorded and visually monitored at regular 
intervals (6 month intervals). 
 
 
         A possible repair may be planned on a purely economic point of view. 
 
-    If the deterioration progresses/accelerates towards failure, then more positive 
means and methods shall be introduced for determining the causes, extent and 
development trend of failure, namely, checking conformity with drawings and 
specifications, verifying design parameters, performing field and laboratory 
tests, spot checks by exposing buried portions for observations, checking for 
settlement, etc.

- 
History of failure shall be adequately recorded, especially for those which are 
long-term. e.g. cracks and settlements. 
 
-       Based on the recorded data and experience with similar cases, the failure causes, 
development trends and consequences can be assessed. 
 
- 
The above analysis of deficiencies shall be carried out by either the Plant Civil 
Engineer or by the engaged Consultant (if any).  Assistance of TS division could 
be obtained for specific issues that require further study. 
 
- 
Seeking advice of Shareholders or independent consultants is advisable in 
complex situations particularly for matters related to soil mechanics, corrosion 
and concrete deterioration. 
 
- 
Repair schedule and procedures shall be developed based on findings of 
investigations. 
 
 
 
Complementary actions such as change in operating conditions, design 
improvement, etc. shall also be considered. 
. 
 
 
 
9.5 
REPAIR POLICY AND SCHEDULE 
 
 
Based on the inspection reports, main findings, analysis of deficiencies and 
recommendations, a general strategy of repair will be established considering the 
following: 
 
 
- 
Type of foundation/structure 
 
- 
Type and extent of required repair 
 
- 
Degree of deterioration/ repair priorities 
 
- 
Location of foundation/structure 
 
- 
Requirement of equipment shutdown 
 
- 
Estimated repair time 
 
- 
Estimated repair cost 
 
 
 
Accordingly an appropriate work schedule will be prepared by the plant .  Necessary 
coordination shall be made with TP division and their assistance obtained for the 
works to be implemented by them.

9.6 
SCOPE OF WORK, EXECUTION, SUPERVISION AND RESPONSIBILITY 
 
 
The Scope of Work shall cover all types of repairs which are classified as follows: 
 
 
 
- 
Minor Works: 
 
 
 
These works are related to any minor deficiency detected by routine inspection 
of Plant Civil Engineer/Supervisor or by any of the plant personnel. 
 
 
 
If the works cannot be carried out by the in house resources a local site 
contractor shall be appointed to carry out the work by a Plant Service Work 
Order. 
 
 
 
The preparation of Scope of Work and site supervision shall be the Plant 
responsibility. 
 
 
 
- Major Works: 
 
 
 
The major works items are related to all major items detailed in the inspection 
programme classification list.  These major items shall be compiled in one 
maintenance project (Civil Package) at each site and implemented either by TP 
division or by the plant (in case the plant has civil engineer) as per the plant 
discretion. 
 
 
 
Preliminary or detailed Scope of Work shall be prepared by the project 
implementer. 
 
 
 
Based on the above prepared scope, complete contract documents for the major 
repair works shall be prepared and implemented by either; 
 
 
 
 
   GASCO package 
 
 
 
or   Consultancy package 
 
 
 
- 
GASCO Package: 
 
 
 
 
This consists of the repair works which could be prepared completely in-
house. 
 
 
 
 
Engineering, tendering and contract supervision shall be handled by the 
project implementer.  The Plant Civil Engineer/Supervisor could assist in 
the preparation of the Scope of Work and the site supervision during the 
work execution.

10.1 
INTRODUCTION 
 
 
 This “Inspection Policy” of GASCO had undergone periodic revisions after the 
merger of “ATHEER” with GASCO and again after the integration of AGD-I. 
Efforts are made in this Policy to combine the best practices of GASCO, ATHEER 
and AGD-I.  
 
 
 The “Inspection Policy” is concerned with the principles governing the Asset 
Integrity management through Inspection.  Compliance with the GASCO HSE 
Policy has been considered while drafting this Policy. 
 
 
 Detailed procedures and strategies for successful implementation shall be updated 
based on this Policy. 
 
 
 This Policy is applicable to all the static equipment and pressure containing parts 
including transmission pipelines. Inspection of rotating parts of machinery is not 
part of the scope of this Policy. 
 
Updating of the Policy shall be coordinated by ESI department in the Abu Dhabi 
office 
 
   
10.2 
OBJECTIVE 
 
The objective of the Policy is to design and implement an Inspection Management 
System in an effective and efficient manner so as to assure the Company 
Management that the equipment and piping, including transmission pipelines, are 
maintained in safe operating condition and that off-stream time of static equipment 
is reduced to a minimum by implementing pro-active Risk Based Inspection (RBI) 
programs. 
 
Shutdown inspection frequencies shall be optimized by using a systematic Risk 
Based Inspection program to evaluate and reduce plant risk and to ensure 
reliability and availability of the plants and pipeline network meets or exceeds the 
plan.  
 
The objective of the on-stream and planned shutdown inspections shall be to 
minimize unplanned shutdowns. 
 
Identification of integrity threats and their categorization utilizing a risk based 
approach shall be adopted and timely remedial actions shall be implemented 
according to this risk categorization.  Experience has shown that the identification 
of integrity threats must be a very rigorous and structured process.  External

identified and mitigated.  All assumptions used in the RBI analysis must be 
recorded and validated.  These threats and assumptions must be re-visited on a 
regular basis and re-validated. 
 
Ensure that repairs and modification of existing equipment are executed in 
accordance with company procedures, International codes and engineering 
standards. 
 
Set-up and ensure control of inspection management utilizing computerized 
inspection and corrosion management systems using S-RBI and MAXIMO. Other 
user friendly software can be adopted if they have functionalities that are not 
available in MAXIMO. 
 
 
 
 
10.3 
INSPECTION STRATEGY 
 
In order to achieve the above objective, a broad based inspection strategy has been 
developed in accordance with the following Polices, procedures and requirements: 
 
· Company HSE policy 
· Root Cause Analysis (RCA) 
· Technical Integrity of the facilities 
· Fitness for Service Study (FFS) 
· High plant availability 
· Long length between shutdowns 
· Maximize On-Stream inspection 
· Risk Based Inspection using SRBI programmes  
· Critical Analysis of On-Stream and Off-Stream inspection Survey results. 
· Investigation of each incident related to equipment piping and transmission 
pipelines and implementation of action plan. 
· Consultation by Shareholders/external specialists to provide in depth 
knowledge and independent view as and when required. 
· Individual Strategies for specialised equipment i.e. Jetty structures, 
Refrigerated LPG tanks and associated structures.

STAGES OF INSPECTION 
 
Pre-commissioning Inspection 
 
The Project Group shall ensure that plant inspection personnel are actively 
involved in monitoring the contractors works during the pre-commissioning and 
commissioning activities such as snag / punch listing, initial thickness survey of 
piping and equipment, baseline intelligent pig survey of the pipeline, baseline 
RBI, cathodic protection systems, DCVG and CIPS surveys. A copy of the final 
documents pertaining to these activities shall be handed over to the Inspection 
Section. 
 
Warranty Inspection 
 
Warranty inspection is required to ensure that following an initial period of 
service, the overall design, construction and materials are satisfactory and any 
defects observed are thoroughly investigated.  Initial measurements are taken to 
form the basis for assessment of deterioration.  This is normally carried out after 
12-24 months of service and before final acceptance of the facility. 
 
Warranty Inspection of equipment shall be done as appropriates based on RBI and 
process requirements. 
 
Along with Operations Inspection Group, Project Engineering Div. shall actively 
participate to co-ordinate the remedial actions with the EPC Contractors. Any 
lessons learnt shall be incorporated in the GASCO’s DGS specifications for future 
projects. 
 
Subsequent Inspections 
 
These are required to ensure continued safety and reliability of the plant, 
equipment and transmission pipelines. Inspection must assure that all components 
are thoroughly examined and necessary measurements are taken to compare with 
previous values to enable calculation and assessment of deterioration and to 
establish remnant life and to set the date for next turnaround inspection. 
 
The frequency of inspection of equipment must be based on risk based inspection 
methodology by identifying the rate of deterioration and the risk of failure.  If RBI 
has still not established for any plant or part of the plant, the plant inspection 
section head shall propose the inspection frequency, which shall be endorsed by 
ESI. Optimization of inspection frequencies shall be done through S-RBI as soon 
as possible.

10.4 
RESOURCES 
 
The inspection activities are generally executed by the company’s own staff, but 
services that require specialized equipment and staff shall be done using approved 
outside resources, as follows: 
 
- HIC Survey by Ultrasonic Mapping. 
- IRIS (Internal Rotary Inspection System) inspection 
- Eddy Current Testing 
- Magnetic Flux Leakage Detection 
- Time of Flight Diffraction (TOFD) and Guided Wave Ultrasonic Techniques 
- Long Range Ultrasonic Techniques 
- Thermo graphic Survey using Infrared Imaging. 
- Radiography. 
- Failure Investigation. 
- In-situ Metallographic Surveys.  
- Intelligent Pigging of Pipelines. 
- DCVG/CIP Surveys  
- Corrosion Coupon Retrieval and Analysis. 
 
Specialized training and certification of company inspection personnel shall be 
arranged as necessary. 
Assistance from Shareholders/external agencies shall be sought time to time on 
requirement basis for consultancy type of work. 
 
10.5 
RESPONSIBILITIES 
 
The Inspection section heads are responsible for keeping the Technical Services 
Superintendents (Maintenance Superintendents in the case of BUHASA , ASAB 
and pipelines division) aware of the condition of equipment, piping and promptly 
report any deterioration or condition which may affect the integrity of the plant, for 
their respective Division. The same shall also be communicated to Integrity 
Coordinator in Head Office.  The Inspection section heads are responsible for 
notifying the Plant Division Managers directly of any urgent integrity issue that 
requires high priority and urgent attention. 
 
The Senior Corrosion Engineer and the Integrity Coordinator in the Head Office 
shall develop Inspection Policy, procedures and assist the Sites by providing 
solutions to plant corrosion and integrity issues, fitness for service studies and RBI 
review in co ordination with Plant Inspection Section Heads.

The Inspection section heads shall be responsible to organize the work to be 
executed by specialist inspection contractors.  The Division Managers shall ensure 
that there is close liaison between Inspection, Maintenance and the Operations 
functions and shall also ensure that the Inspection section heads maintain their 
independence with respect to integrity issues. 
 
The recommendations of RCA and FFS studies, including specialized studies and 
audits for materials and corrosion, shall be incorporated by the Plant Inspection

implementation. 
 
ESI, along with his specialist’s engineers, are responsible for providing services in

and also arrange for failure investigation and fitness-for-service assessments.   
 
Corrosion and Material Coordination Department of Project Execution Div., in 
liaison with Plant Inspection section heads, shall co-ordinate with major EPC 
Contractors regarding defects identified during warranty inspection. 
 
10.5A Base Line NDT / RBI for New Project 
 
It is required to include during Project Execution stage, base line survey such as 
initial ultrasonic thickness survey of plant and facility piping , base line corrosion 
survey (Intelligent Pig Survey), DCVG and CIPS for buried product pipelines 
during pre-commissioning by EPC contractor.  A RBI programme shall be 
established as part of new project utilizing the base line data. 
 
 
10.6 
RISK BASED INSPECTION 
 
The risk based inspection system shall be used for developing the inspection plans. 
A thorough Risk Based Inspection Programme shall be conducted at no longer than 
five 
years 
intervals 
or 
when 
there 
are 
changes 
in 
the 
operating 
envelopes/deviations at each plant.  Shareholders/contracted specialists acting as 
advisors and facilitators shall be involved in the RBI assessment. 
 
RBI provides a methodology for determining the optimum combination of 
equipment inspection methods, scopes and frequencies. With the risk factor 
determined for each component of a company's equipment, this information is then 
utilized to optimize and pinpoint the amounts and locations of subsequent 
inspections and maintenance processes. Inspection efforts can be concentrated on 
documented “problem areas” and inspection scopes reduced on more benign areas 
without affecting the plant's integrity and production

10.7 
SCHEDULING 
 
Though inspection frequency is determined by RBI methodology, consideration 
shall be given for the inspection of equipment involving production losses to 
coincide with upstream / downstream plants shutdown or the planned shutdowns 
for major overhaul of rotating equipment such as gas turbines / compressor etc. 
 
10.8 
EXECUTION 
 
Inspection section heads are responsible for the execution of the inspection 
programmes, planning and implementation, follow up and to inform the 
management about areas of concern related to the integrity of the plant. 
 
10.9 
ANALYSIS 
 
 
 
 The findings of inspection shall be properly analysed by the Inspection Section 
Heads so that corrective steps can be taken to improve the overall integrity of the 
equipment.  
 
 
 When failures or unexpected inspection findings are observed the Inspection 
Section Heads should consult the Integrity Department in the HQ. 
The recommendation of inspection findings shall be documented and the 
implementation shall be incorporated in the company’s 5-year business plan. 
 
Inspection Section Heads are required to keep themselves abreast and updated on 
new inspection/analysis techniques. These should be shared at the GASCO 
Inspection Section Heads Meetings. 
 
The quality of interpretation of inspection data will be examined via Level-2 and 
Level-3 Audits. 
 
10.10 FEEDBACK 
 
 
 
After inspection and maintenance activities, the inspection database shall be 
updated. These updates are essential for the periodic Risk Based Inspection 
Assessments. 
 
10.11 FAILURE INVESTIGATIONS 
 
In order to prevent recurrence, all significant failures shall be thoroughly 
investigated to establish their root causes and to understand the sources of the 
problem using the GASCO RCA procedure.

10.12 FITNESS-FOR-SERVICE 
 
In order to avoid unplanned shutdowns or unavailability of spares, equipment 
which has suffered some form of degradation beyond the acceptance criteria of the 
original design and construction codes, consideration should be given for Fitness-
For-Service (FFS) Assessments.  
This assessment should lead to a technically and economically feasible action plan 
that allows continued service of the equipment, without sacrificing integrity, for a 
given period of time. 
 
The Inspection section heads are responsible to ensure FFS assessments are 
conducted as and when required. They have the authority to perform Level 1 (API 
579 “Recommended Practice for Fitness-For-Service”) assessments (e.g. FFS of 
piping damaged by localised metal loss). Levels 2 and 3 assessments (e.g. FFS of 
stress corrosion cracking, and remaining fatigue life of turbine blades, 
respectively) should only be done by specialist companies knowledgeable in FFS 
assessment. The actions arising from the recommendations shall be incorporated in 
the GRIAT system with risk based priorities assigned to them. 
 
10.13 DEVIATIONS 
 
Inspection section heads shall notify the Plant Management of any deviations for 
their endorsement. Deviations shall be proposed and authorised using the Integrity 
Critical Activities Deviation Control procedure described in Section 4.15 of this 
document.  GRIATS shall be used to capture and track deviations. 
 
10.14 PROCEDURES 
 
Inspection procedures shall be revised periodically for the following critical 
integrity issues. The Frequency of review should be as per the GASCO Procedure 
of Control of Engineering & Maintenance Procedures & Policies: 
 
 
10.14.1 Corrosion Under Insulation (CUI) 
 
External corrosion of steel beneath thermal insulation on all types of 
static equipment is a major cause for concern because extensive metal 
loss of the wall thickness of insulated piping and equipment may result in 
an unexpected failure.

## Page 246

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY

All insulated process and utility piping and equipment shall be 
periodically inspected at frequencies in line with the S-RBI analysis and 
recent history record. 
 
The inspection procedure defines the inspection frequencies and the tools 
required to establish the condition of the insulated piping and equipment. 
Priority for inspection shall be given to cryogenic insulation, piping and 
equipment operating in dual temperature service and personnel protection 
insulation. Procedure shall also include QA/QC specifications for 
insulation work.  
 
Adequate inspection coverage using periodic thermography survey, 
including complete removal of the insulation may be required to achieve 
an acceptable level of confidence concerning the corrosion condition. 
 
Where CUI is significant, such as Mol Sieve Treaters and associated 
piping, consideration shall be given to pure aluminium thermal spray 
coating with an appropriate sealer. 
 
10.14.2 Underground Structure Corrosion Survey 
 
It is essential to clearly identify the company owned pipeline routes 
between the launcher and receiver, including the pipelines extending into 
upstream and downstream of the launcher and receiver into other 
companies’ areas. 
 
Metallic piping buried in soil may be subject to external corrosion upon 
failure of the protective coating. When the external coating is delaminated 
it acts as a shield thus preventing cathodic protection current to reach the 
surface. Such areas are found to be subject to severe pitting corrosion. In 
order to ensure the integrity, all the buried piping including the non 
piggable buried piping shall be subjected to periodic inspection by 
excavation followed by visual inspection.  Proven NDT techniques shall 
be used for determining the condition of the coating. 
 
The pipeline inspection procedure shall be established to define the 
method of inspection, inspection coverage, type of inspection tools to be 
used and the inspection frequencies. 
 
The buried piggable Gas, NGL, Crude and Condensate pipelines shall be 
periodically inspected by inline inspection pigs. The inspection intervals 
shall depend on various factors such as condition observed in the last 
survey, corrosive nature of the soil along the pipeline route, type and 
condition of coating and also the cathodic protection performance. Due to 
business criticality, the NGL and acid gas pipelines shall be pigged using

exposed and the joint is fully tight. 
inline pigs every four years. Other pipelines shall be pigged between 5-8 
years based on historical data, coating deterioration and risk ranking. 
 
CP system of pipelines in a common corridor shall be integrated. Stand-
alone systems shall be avoided. 
 
A comprehensive pipeline integrity management system shall be 
established to incorporate design, maintenance and inspection data 
(including the data collected during various inspection surveys). 
 
The management system shall provide facility for RBI and risk ranking of 
pipeline sections and shall be based on GIS coordinates. 
 
An approved procedure for monitoring of cathodic protection system, 
CIPS and DCVG surveys shall be established. 
 
A comprehensive procedure to inspect the launcher and receiver 
including all the appurtenances shall be established. 
 
A standard procedure for pipeline rehabilitation shall be prepared for 
execution soon after intelligent pig or DCVG/CIP surveys. The procedure 
shall include comprehensive plans to address all the defects and coating 
repairs in a prioritised manner. 
 
10.14.3 General External Corrosion 
 
General external corrosion of steel on all types of static equipment is also 
a cause for concern.  There have been several instances where not paying 
due regard to this general corrosion threat has given rise to high potential 
loss of containment incidents. 
 
The Inspection section head must have in place a procedure and plan for 
the regular inspection and rehabilitation of protective coatings for all 
static equipment.  See Section 10.22 below on Paint Maintenance. 
 
 
10.14.4 Threaded Connections 
 
Threaded connections shall not be used in hydrocarbon services. Every 
effort shall be made to eliminate the existing threaded connections.  The 
DGS, DEP and Industry standards do not allow threaded connections in 
hydrocarbon service.  Existing threaded connections are potential sources 
of leakage due to failure from fatigue, corrosion or improper fit-up. It is 
necessary to make sure that the threaded joints are welded up to first 
block valves. Before welding, it is essential to ensure that no threads are

Threaded connections in the sour gas system and other toxic service shall 
be prohibited in all the piping classes. 
 
A detailed inspection procedure, defining the inspection frequencies and 
methods of inspection, should be available. 
 
10.14.5 Socket Weld Connections 
 
Socket weld connections shall not be permitted in wet sour gas and toxic 
service in all piping classes. 
Socket welds shall be avoided in 600# and above rated piping in 
hydrocarbon service. 
 
10.14.6 Prevention and Monitoring of Degradation Mechanism due to H2S 
 
The H2S limits and inspection frequencies established in this policy are 
applicable to the existing plants that were constructed with high sulphur 
steels (S>0.01%), i.e. non-sour spec and non-HIC resistant steels. 
 
With the exception of HABSHAN & BAB plant, there are no written 
statements available that the other GASCO plants are designed and 
constructed to operate under sour environment as defined by NACE MR-
0175. However, they have been operating with H2S levels higher than 
original design and, as a result, there could be a moderate risk of sulphide 
stress corrosion cracking (SSC) and hydrogen induced cracking ( HIC). 
Therefore, these units require close and careful survey for hardness level 
of weld joints to ensure they are within HRC 22 to avoid SCC.  
 
For new requirements or projects, only NACE / HIC resistant steel shall 
be used, for which neither H2S limits nor periodic inspection for 
HIC/SOHIC detection are required. However such equipment may suffer 
general type of corrosion, especially when H2S and chloride are present 
and they need to be inspected periodically, as per the RBI Analysis. 
 
Corrosion prevention methodologies including inhibitor injection, 
installation of various types monitoring systems for general corrosion, 
stress corrosion, etc. shall be considered.  Such corrosion prevention 
methods shall be regularly reviewed and up-dated at least every 5 years. 
Inspection Section Heads need to review PCRs with regard to their 
potential impact on corrosion prevention methodologies.

10.15 INSPECTION FREQUENCY OF SPECIALISED EQUIPMENT 
 
 
 
The required inspection methods and frequency for most items of static 
equipment are derived from the RBI assessment.  However, RBI may not 
cater for the specialised static equipment below: 
 
10.15.1 Fired Steam Generation & Waste Heat Boilers 
 
The internal full inspection and hydro testing shall be carried out after 
every two years of operation. The complete thermal insulation shall be 
removed after 10 years of operation and detailed inspection to be carried 
out for all the components and connected piping. 
 
10.15.2 Unfired Waste Heat Boilers (SRU) 
 
The maximum internal inspection & hydro testing interval shall be after 
4-years.   
 
10.15.3 Reaction Furnaces (SRU) 
 
 
 
 
The internal inspection interval shall be every 4 years, in case RBI study 
is not carried out. The frequency can be increased or decreased as per 
RBI study. 
 
10.15.4 SRU Stacks 
 
The internal inspection of stacks shall be on opportunity basis. On stream 
and stack verticality survey to be carried out  every 6 months. 
 
10.15.5 Cold Boxes (Stacked Aluminium Brazed Heat Exchangers) 
 
The internal inspection of such stacked exchangers is practically not 
possible. Hence, pressure leak test to be carried out every 10 years. 
 
 
10.15.6 Testing of Fully Welded Equipment (where hydro- static testing is not 
permitted due to operational reasons) 
 
Such equipment (e.g. Gas Compression Injection After-Coolers) shall be 
inspected by non- intrusive inspection techniques to assess their integrity. 
10.15.7 Fired Process Heaters     
 
The fired heaters generally consist of Radiant and Convection zone. The 
radiant zone tubes and fittings are normally fully accessible. Detailed NDT

o The convection zone tubes of such heaters are partly accessible (shock tubes 
and top row of tubes only) and NDT inspection of entire section is difficult. 
In such circumstances, the shock tubes, top bank tubes and bends shall be 
inspected along with radiant zone tubes every four years.  However, 
inspection of the tube bends is not necessarily representative of the finned 
tube condition and therefore its value must be carefully considered.  Efforts 
shall be made to carry out to carry out NDT inspection of finned /studded 
tubes in the convection zone (such as by breaking the fins at 
approachable locations, etc.). Intelligent pigging of the tubes should be 
considered.  Installation of removable viewing panels may also assist with 
access to the convection bank for inspection. . The inspection frequency for 
heater inspection can be altered as per RBI recommendations. 
  
However, the fired heaters convection and radiant zone tubes shall 
under go integrity test for the entire furnace by carrying hydro-static 
test at 1.5 times the design pressure every 10 years. 
 
 
10.16 REPAIRS 
 
A detailed procedure shall be developed for any repair work differing from the 
original specification.  That procedure shall be based on sound engineering 
practices and take into consideration the requirements of the original design codes 
and construction specifications.  The Plant Change Request (PCR) procedure 
should be applied to all repairs that are not as per the original design codes and 
construction specifications. 
 
Because of the eventual non-availability of materials or specialized manpower or 
equipment and tools, or due to operational considerations, the repair works may 
require to be postponed or planned during more appropriate time, a temporary 
repair may be acceptable. In such case a risk assessment shall be conducted. Any 
temporary repair shall be made as per the Plant Change Request  (PCR) procedure. 
 
 
10.17 WELDING WORKS 
 
- 
Welding Works Carried Out By Contractors: 
 
Contractors shall provide welder qualification certificates issued by a reputable 
third party inspection agency, prior to commencement of any welding works. 
 
GASCO Inspection section heads shall review the certificates and check the 
respective validity against the relevant code requirements applicable to the 
specific welding works to be carried out by the contractor. The contractor shall

for which he was qualified. However, if the certificate is more than six months 
old and there is no documentary evidence that the welder was in continuous

In case of no qualification certificates are provided by the contractor or those

Welding Works Carried Out By GASCO Welders: 
 
GASCO Inspection Section Heads shall ensure that the welders are fully 
qualified to carry out the required welding works by evaluating GASCO 
welders performance through the execution of welding performance tests 
according to ASME Section IX and API 1104 requirements. Welding 
Inspection & Control sheets are part of the requirement. 
 
 
10.18 SAFETY RELIEF VALVES 
 
10.18.1 Testing and Inspection 
 
The frequency of testing and inspection of Safety Relief Valves should be 
as per Appendix 4.5- 2.1 of this document. 
 
These testing frequencies are administered in MAXIMO.  
 
 
Valves shall be dismantled for overhaul and thorough examination under 
the following conditions: 
 
- When found passing during normal operation or scheduled testing. 
 
- If the valves keep passing during service and dismantling is difficult 
(such as absence of isolation block valves), then thorough 
examination may be deferred depending on design, operations and 
environmental concerns, up to a maximum of 6 months. 
 
10.18.2 Valve Setting/Calibration of Pressure Gauges 
 
Incorrect or lack of calibration of pressure gauges is a frequent cause of 
improper valve setting. Thus, pressure gauges used in the test block shall 
be calibrated every 6 months to ensure accuracy.

The pressure tests should be carried out with fresh water. 
 
10.18.3 Valve Misapplication 
 
When service experience indicates that the selected type of valve or 
material is not suitable for the service conditions, a change shall be made 
to ensure dependable operation of the safety relief valve.  Such changes 
shall be made using the PCR procedure. 
 
 
 
10.19 FLEXIBLE HOSES  
 
10.19.1 Scope 
 
During their useful life, flexible hoses should be inspected and tested 
periodically to ensure safe transfer of hydrocarbons.  Moreover, during 
idle periods, the flexible hoses should be properly protected against the 
ingress of foreign matter, particularly sand. The hoses shall be flushed, 
dried, coiled and stored in covered shed. 
 
  
10.19.2 Useful Life of Flexible Hoses 
 
The useful life of flexible hoses should be four years counted from the 
date of their first use. Whatever is the frequency of use when reaching the 
end of their useful life, flexible hoses should be scrapped and removed 
from the site. 
10.19.3 Frequency of Inspection 
 
During their useful life, flexible hoses should be subjected to a thorough 
visual examination every six months. 
 
10.19.4 Frequency of Testing 
 
During their useful life, flexible hoses should be hydrostatically tested 
twice at 18 months intervals. A colour coding shall be devised such that 
the users can confidently use only tested hoses. The colour shall be 
changed during subsequent tests. All concerned users of the hoses shall 
be made aware of the colour code applicable during that period of time. 
 
The pressure should be raised in increments up to the test pressure of 1.3 
times the operating pressure of the hose.

Effective Date01.05.2006 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Following the test, the water should be immediately drained and the hose 
blown dry. 
 
10.19.6 Identification 
 
The flexible hoses should be identified by a permanent marking system 
such that correct correlation can be established with the respective 
recording system. 
 
10.19.7 Recording 
 
Proper and updated records shall be maintained containing the technical 
data, inspection reports, dates of test, testing conditions and any other 
relevant information related to the operating, maintenance, inspection and 
testing history. 
 
10.19.8 Exclusions 
 
The following hoses are excluded from the scope of this policy: 
1. 
Fire hoses. 
2. 
Breathing apparatus hoses. 
3. 
Propriety hoses forming an integral part of machinery and vehicles 
e.g. dry Chemical powder delivery on fire trucks. 
 
Inspection and testing of these types of hoses shall be under the 
responsibility of the HSE Superintendents. 
 
10.20 LIFTING EQUIPMENT AND CRANES 
 
All the available stationary and mobile cranes, lifting accessories, appliances, gears 
and tackles owned by GASCO or contractors shall be periodically inspected, proof 
tested and certified by a competent third party inspection authority.

In line with the GASCO HSEMS, Inspection Section Heads should

10.21 INSPECTION OF SEMI-TRAILERS 
 
Inspection of company owned semi-trailers for hauling liquid sulphur and liquid 
propane shall be as follows: 
 
 
Liquid Sulphur Trailers 
 
 
HABSHAN-BAB plant Division are the custodian of liquid sulphur semi trailers. 
The periodic maintenance and of the tanker trailers is the responsibility of the 
transport contractor. (The inspection of the tanker trailers is assigned to a third party 
inspection agency whose representative inspects and certifies as per agreed 
inspection plan). Such inspection plan shall include periodic inspection as well as 
inspection and approval of all the maintenance activities done by the transport 
contractor. HABSHAN sulphur tanker coordinator is responsible for administrators 
the inspection schedules followed by third party agency in coordination with HBT-I. 
Copy of Inspection reports shall be forwarded to HBT-I 
 
 
Liquid Propane Trailers 
 
 
 Major maintenance and inspection activities of the vessel are the responsibility of 
RUWAIS plant. ORT/2 is responsible for inspection and approving of the vessel 
after major maintenance works. 
 
10.22 PAINT MAINTENANCE 
 
Effective paint systems and their proper application and maintenance are essential 
in ensuring that static and other equipment do not deteriorate due to external 
corrosion and lose their required functionality over the lifecycle. 
 
Paint maintenance for repair of damage, chalking and compensation for paint 
thickness losses or aesthetic appearance is required either to prevent corrosion of 
the substrate metal or keep up aesthetic appearance. 
 
10.22.1 SCOPE OF PAINTING 
 
All originally painted work shall be maintained in a condition such that the 
corrosion of the substrate metal is not adversely affected .

10.22.2 LEVELS OF PAINT MAINTENANCE 
 
 
10.22.2.1 Level 1: 
 
 
 
Paint deterioration is limited to the point where, apart from minor touch 
up repairs, only cleaning and a top coat is required for reconditioning. 
 
 
 
This level shall be applied to all vessels critical & pressure piping 
installed in the process plot area of the plants. 
 
 
 
 
10.22.2.2 Level 2: 
 
Paint deterioration is limited to the point when the distribution and size 
of the damaged areas make surface preparation by hand tool cleaning 
more practical than blast cleaning. 
 
This level shall be applied to all equipment (i.e. pumps, motors, 
compressors, fired heaters, storage tanks), piping and fittings, supports, 
platform walkways and handrails installed in the process plot area of 
the plants. 
 
This same level shall be applied to all equipment and piping operating 
with flammable gases installed outside the process plot area of the 
plants. 
 
 
 
10.22.2.3 Level 3: 
 
Note: It is expected that paint systems on the GASCO facilities are maintained such that 
Level 3 deterioration is not reached. 
 
Beginning of general rust breakthrough requiring blast cleaning. 
 
This level shall be applied to all equipment and piping installed outside 
the process plot area of the plants and operating with non-flammable 
gases. 
 
10.22.3 BLAST CLEANING IN PROCESS AREAS 
 
Only in very special cases will blast cleaning in the process area of the plants be 
permitted upon the Plant Management approval.

10.22.4 PAINTING SPECIFICATIONS AND PROCEDURES 
 
 
All painting work shall be carried out in accordance with GASCO DGS 6600 010 
Specification. 
 
GASCO DGS specification shall be subject to periodic revision at three-year 
intervals to incorporate the technological developments of the paint manufacturing 
industry and also the changes suggested by the performance of past paint 
applications in the plants. Colour coding as per DGS specification shall be 
implemented whenever repainting work is done. 
 
10.22.5 APPROVED PAINTS 
 
 
Only paints from GASCO approved paint manufacturers as defined in GASCO 
Painting Manual shall be used. Approval of brand names of the approved paint 
coating is the responsibility of ESI. 
 
10.22.6 PAINTING PLANNING 
 
All painting works to be done outside the scope of the two year agreement for 
Painting, Insulation and Scaffolding shall be planned and submitted for the Plant 
Manager's approval in advance of the GASCO budget preparation for the year in 
which the painting works are intended to be carried out. 
 
10.22.7 RESOURCES 
 
 
All painting works are carried out by contractors. 
 
 
10.22.7.1 Permanent Paint Team: 
 
This team is part of the two year agreement for Painting, Insulation and 
Scaffolding and will carry out routine painting maintenance, mainly for 
Level 2, as defined in previous paragraph 10.22.2.2. 
 
 
10.22.7.2 Temporary Paint Team: 
 
Such a team is specially contracted by GASCO to carry out budgeted 
and approved painting works that fall out of the scope of the permanent 
contract for Painting, Insulation and Scaffolding. 
 
This team is essentially involved in painting works pertaining to Levels 
1 and 3.

10.22.8 RESPONSIBILITIES 
 
 
10.22.8.1 The Mechanical Section Head or Civil Section Head as applicable in the 
plant pipeline shall be responsible for coordinating and implementing the 
painting works. 
 
 
10.22.8.2 The Inspection Section shall be responsible for verifying the contractor's 
quality control of all painting works. 
 
 
 
10.22.8.3 The Inspection Section Head/ Inspection Engineer is responsible for 
reporting the condition of all paint work and advising the Engineering 
Superintendent of all painting maintenance works required in the plant. 
 
 
10.22.8.4 Senior Inspection Engineer in the HQ is responsible for keeping abreast 
of new paints and to keep the GASCO Painting Specifications up to date.

CHAPTER 11.0 
 
 
 
MEASUREMENT OF MAINTENANCE PERFORMANCE 
=============================================

## Page 259

GASCO MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION POLICY 
 
CHAPTER 11 - MEASUREMENT OF MAINTENANCE 
PERFORMANCE 
EPL/AGM(O)/0001 
Page : 1 of 3 
Issue No: 6 
Rev 
: 6 
Effective Date: 01.05.2006 
 
 
 
 
 
11.1 
GENERAL 
 
 
Maintenance costs are composed of indirect costs (production losses due to 
breakdowns and planned shutdowns) and direct maintenance costs.  The optimum 
maintenance cost is that which keeps the sum of direct and indirect cost at the 
minimum.  In the long term it is impossible for management to minimise both costs 
at the same time.  It is the major challenge for the Engineering Superintendent to 
find the optimum balance under the set maintenance objectives. 
 
 
To fully appraise maintenance performance the indirect and direct costs should be 
measured. 
 
 
As maintenance is a balance between both costs it is not only important to measure 
the absolute value of product losses and direct maintenance cost but also to watch 
the trend development over the months and years.  This is even more important for 
maintenance performance indices. 
 
 
To assess maintenance performance the following indices should be reported 
MONTHLY in addition to the monthly reporting of production losses (due to 
breakdowns and planned shutdowns) and direct maintenance cost for overheads, 
own personnel, contract payments and material costs: 
 
 
 
TRIPS  
: 
Cumulative number of trips, MSDT and RPT 
 
 
MTBF  
: 
Mean Time Between Failure 
 
 
AVAIL  
: 
Availability (%) 
 
 
RELIA  
: 
Reliability (%) 
 
 
These factors are calculated and reported monthly for all the main process packages 
and some utilities (e.g. power generation and air compressor packages). 
 
 
A deeper analysis is done on a yearly basis.

11.3 
STOP CODES 
 
 
 
 
TRIP 
= 
Trip due to a reason within the package. 
 
 
MSDT = 
Mandatory manual shutdown to avoid dangerous situation for 
the equipment. 
 
 
RPT  
= 
This code (RPT) is allocated to TRIP and MSDT which occur 
during restarting period.  Such stops are categorised by few 
minutes to few hours of machine running after 'firing' (the 
maximum is1 hour). 
 
 
 
MSD 
= 
Manual shutdown of the package. 
 
 
TMSD = 
Trip of the package due to an external cause (example: general 
power failure, general fuel gas failure).

11.4 
DEFINITION OF USED FACTORS 
 
 
- 
MTBF: 'Mean Time Between Failure' 
 
 
 
 
MTBF = Running hours of the package during a period 
 
 
 
              (No. of Trips + MSDT) during a period + 1 
 
 
 
Remarks: 
 
 
 
- 
Each month the period of reference is generally the last 12 months 
period.  (MTBF for a period of one month is not significant). 
 
 
 
 
 
- 
AVAIL (%) = Period - Sched - Unsched -x 100 
 
 
 
                                  Period 
 
 
or 
 
 
 
AVAIL (%) = Run Hours  +  ABNR  x  100 
                                                          Period 
 
 
- 
RELIA (%) = Period - Unsched - x  100 
                                                            Period 
 
 
 
Remarks: 
 
 
 
- 
Any downtime due to working shifts or waiting for spare parts, vendors 
specialists or manpower is to be recorded as 'Sched' or 'Unsched' 
downtime as the case may be. 
 
 
 
- 
ABNR is strictly the period in which the machine is ready to be operated 
any time but it is kept on standby for operational reasons. 
 
- No waiting downtime during maintenance periods is to be allocated to ABNR.  If any 
maintenance activity is carried out on the machine while on standby then the elapsed 
time is to be recorded as downtime and not as ABNR.
